# Changelog

All notable changes to the **ORNS Charity Auction Manager** will be documented in this file.

## [Unreleased]

### Added

- **"New Auction Bug Submitted" email to web-admin@orns.org on every saved bug report.** Anyone can submit a report without signing in, and the report only lands in a sheet (or, if that fails, the logs), so one could go unseen.
  - `server/services/adminNotifications.ts` sends it after the report is saved (in the sheet, the production log fallback, or the local file), from `bugReportRoutes.ts`. The email has the title, type, page, time, whether the sender was signed in, and the first 1,500 characters of the description (all HTML-escaped; the subject is fixed). It points to Bug Reports in the app.
  - **Sent as the most recent staff sign-in**: an anonymous report has no Google session, so it uses the stored refresh token the PayPal sweeper already keeps (`system/paypalSweeperAuth`; its scopes include `gmail.send`), then falls back to the submitter's own session. If neither exists, or Gmail refuses, the report is still saved and the failure is logged.
  - **Limit**: at most 10 emails per 10 minutes across all reports (in memory, like the other limiters). Reports over it are still saved; the next email that goes out says how many it skipped.
  - Awaited before the response (8-second cap), not run afterwards: Cloud Run throttles CPU once the response is sent.
  - Tests: `adminNotifications.test.ts` (recipient and subject, sender choice and fallbacks, failure, timeout, limit and skipped-count note, HTML escaping); new cases in `bugReportRoutes.test.ts` (signed-in, anonymous, log fallback, none for refused reports, save survives a failed email).
  - Backend 617 -> 635, total 1303 -> 1321.

- **Apps Script version check and deploy tooling (2026-10-22 review item 4).**
  - Each Apps Script project has a `Version.gs` holding a fingerprint of all its other deployed files (`scripts/apps-script/fingerprint.mjs`), and an `apiGetVersion()` that reports it.
  - `npm run gas:stamp` rewrites the fingerprints and `server/generated/appsScriptVersions.ts`; `npm run gas:deploy` pushes from the repo folders, creates a version and points each deployment at it (deployment IDs now in each `.clasp.json`); `npm run gas:check` downloads each deployed version and diffs it with the repo. clasp 3.4.1 runs through `npx`: as a devDependency its own `google-auth-library` broke the app's googleapis types.
  - Found while deploying: clasp 3.4.1 treats a push whose only change is a deleted file as "already up to date" and sends nothing - Bid-Management version 30 kept the PayPal files PR #6 moved out. Because the fingerprint changes on any change, `gas:deploy` always sends real changes.
  - Settings page: an **Apps Script Versions** card (`AppsScriptVersionsCard.tsx`, `GET /api/apps-script/versions`) asks each live script for its fingerprint on a click and shows Up to date / Out of date / Couldn't check. It can't see a live edit that left `Version.gs` alone; `gas:check` can.
  - Tests: `appsScriptVersions.test.ts` (stamps current; fails after an unstamped edit), `appsScriptRoutes.test.ts`, `AppsScriptVersionsCard.test.tsx`. Backend 602 -> 617, frontend 683 -> 686, total 1285 -> 1303.

- **ROADMAP.md Milestone A: Sync & Retry Resiliency.** Staff now see when Google Sheets is slow or the connection is gone. A winning bid that can't be sent stays on screen and sends itself once the connection is back, instead of being undone. Choices confirmed by the user on the proposal page: queue winning bids only; banner on every page; warn before closing a tab with bids waiting; ship the server safety along with it.
  - **Connection banner** (`src/components/SyncStatusBanner.tsx`, above every page in `App.tsx`). It shows one message at a time, most important first:
    - saved bids the server refused
    - sign-in expired
    - offline
    - sending saved bids (n of m)
    - saved bids waiting
    - Google retrying (attempt n of 4)
    - a brief "all sent" / "responding again"

    It is a polite live region. The sidebar shows the waiting count.
  - **Retry reporting**: `fetchWithRetry` (`src/services/google-api.ts`) reports each retry and recovery to `src/lib/syncStatus.ts` instead of only the console. When every attempt fails it throws `ServerUnreachableError`, so callers can tell "couldn't reach the server" from a 4xx (still returned as a Response).
  - **Saved-bids outbox** (`src/lib/bidOutbox.ts`, replayed by `src/hooks/useOutboxReplay.ts`):
    - **When a bid is saved:** `useWinnersData.recordWinningBid` keeps the bid in `localStorage` instead of rolling it back when the browser is offline, the server can't be reached after the retries, or the session has expired (401). A 4xx is still undone as before.
    - **When saved bids send:** on sign-in, coming back online, the tab becoming visible, and every 30 seconds while any are waiting. They go one at a time, oldest first, each with its original idempotency key. One tab sends at a time (Web Locks).
    - **When the server refuses a resend:** the bid moves to a review list in the banner, with the server's reason and **Try again** / **Remove**.
    - **Across reloads:** saved bids count as won in the winners list, so the same item can't be entered twice. Quick Entry's This Session feed marks them "Saved on this device".
    - **Closing the tab** with bids waiting shows the browser's "Leave site?" prompt.
    - `recordWinningBid` now resolves with the bid's idempotency key.
  - **Server safety** (`record-winning-bid`):
    - **Durable keys:** keys are kept for 24 hours in Firestore (`idempotency` collection, `server/services/idempotencyStore.ts`, via `withIdempotency(..., { durable: true })`). A resend after a Cloud Run restart or scale-to-zero returns the first result instead of recording the bid again. The Firestore lookup runs inside the in-flight promise, so concurrent copies still run once. If Firestore is unreachable, it falls back to memory.
    - **One winner per item:** the route refuses a second winner for a one-winner item with **409** (`server/utils/winningBidChecks.ts`). It reads the item's "Allow Multiple Winners" flag from the Items sheet only when the item already has a winner. Before, only Bid Entry's screen checked this.
    - **Same bid already recorded:** the same bidder at the same price is treated as already recorded (a resend whose first answer was lost) and nothing is written.
    - **When the check is skipped:** with no `itemsSpreadsheetId` in the request (older clients) or an unreadable Items sheet, the check is skipped, as before.
  - Not queued, by design: checkout, receipts, invoices, emails and closing the auction (see `patterns.md`).
  - Optional setup: a Firestore TTL policy on `idempotency.expireAt` (README "Deployment").
  - Tests:
    - New: `bidOutbox.test.ts`, `syncStatus.test.ts`, `SyncStatusBanner.test.tsx`, `useOutboxReplay.test.ts`, `idempotencyStore.test.ts`, `winningBidChecks.test.ts`.
    - New cases in: `useWinnersData.test.ts` (saved instead of undone, offline, 401, 409 still undone, reload overlay), `google-api.test.ts` (retry reporting, `ServerUnreachableError`), `idempotency.test.ts` (durable), `bid-management.test.ts` (409, already recorded, repeatable, check skipped, resend after restart; the three behavior cases fail on the old code), and `BidEntry.test.tsx` (feed marker).
    - Backend 554 -> 573, frontend 638 -> 672, total 1192 -> 1245.

### Fixed

- **Editing an item's value now fills in a cleared opening bid and min raise** (bug report "Item's value change doesn't update min bid and starting value"). In the Edit dialog, changing the value and clearing both amounts saved them blank: the auto-fill only looked at the amounts the item had before the edit, which weren't blank. `useAuctionItemUpdate.ts` now looks at what the save would leave, and fills either amount from the (new) value when it would be blank. An amount the user typed is never overwritten. Tests: two new cases in `AuctionItemsTab.editing.test.tsx` (the first fails on the old code). Frontend 686 -> 688, total 1335 -> 1337.

- **Every Auction-Items, Bidder-Numbers and Dashboard write now goes through a locked server route (2026-10-22 refactor audit R1-R4).** The browser used to write these cells directly through the generic `/api/sheets` passthrough, which takes no lock.
  - **R1 - item edits.** The Edit dialog's changes (all fields, including Allow Multiple Winners in column M) go to `/api/auction/update-item` in one request, under the `auction-items:` lock, instead of one direct cell write per field. A direct write could be lost: an add, delete or bundle that read the sheet just before it rewrote the whole range just after. Edits that don't change category or status rewrite only that item's row. The server checks the row still holds the item the editor opened (by name, because resequencing renumbers IDs) and answers 409 if not; the app then says so and reloads the list. The bulk item-type edit uses the locked `/api/auction/bulk-update-items` like bulk category already did, with an idempotency key. (The audit also said a delete could move rows under an edit. It can't: deletes only mark the row Deleted.)
  - **R2 - Check In and Proxy** on the Bidders page use the new `POST /api/bidders/set-flag` (`bidderService.ts`'s `setBidderFlag`), which finds the row and ticks column E or F under the `bidder-numbers:` lock, as patterns.md requires.
  - **R3 - removed the unused attendee routes** `/api/tickets/update-attendee` and `/update-attendee-field` and their `updateAttendeeField`, a second, partial copy of the ticket-sheet column layout that nothing called. The ticket sheets aren't rewritten by any locked server code, so attendee edits keep writing their own cells; `src/lib/attendeeSheetColumns.ts` stays the one column map.
  - **R4 - Dashboard and passthrough.** The gift card / raffle winner saves use the new `POST /api/auction/special-winner` (`specialWinnerService.ts`): it writes Special-Winners, adds the Follow-Up row for a winner who already checked out, creates either tab if missing, and holds the `bidder-numbers:` lock that `recordWinningBid`'s own Follow-Up writes hold. The two near-identical save functions in `useDashboardState.ts` became one. The passthrough now answers 403 for Auction-Items, Bidder-Numbers, Bid-Management, Special-Winners, Follow-Up and PayPal-Follow-Up, and no longer looks up (or creates) the tab on every write, which saves one Sheets call per attendee edit.
  - Tests: `items.test.ts` (one-row write for several fields, column M, 409 with nothing written, missing row), `bidderService.test.ts` and `bid-management.test.ts` (set-flag columns, unknown bidder, waits for the lock, bad flag, 401), new `specialWinnerService.test.ts` (cells, Follow-Up row, tab creation, waits for the lock), `proxyRoutes.test.ts` (403 for each locked sheet without calling Google; ticket sheets still allowed); client tests now assert the new requests (`AuctionItemsTab.editing.test.tsx` incl. the 409 path, `useAuctionItemsTableState.test.ts`, `useBiddersData.test.ts`, `Dashboard.test.tsx`, `EditAuctionItemDialog.test.tsx`). Removed the dead routes' tests. Backend 635 -> 649, total 1321 -> 1335.

- **School name corrected to Orchard Ridge Nursery School** in `AGENTS.md`, `docs/agents/README.md` and the app's install description in `vite.config.ts` (the PWA manifest text), which still said "Oakridge Neighborhood School". The root `README.md` was fixed in PR #8.
- **Apps Script hardening, round 2 (2026-10-22 review items 1-3).**
  - **No PayPal code in the live Bid-Management script.** `API_PAYPAL_TESTS.gs` could create and send real PayPal invoices, and `testCronJobSweeper` ran the old checker against the real Bidder-Numbers sheet, from the script editor's Run button. They, the old `Class_PayPalService.gs` / `PayPalSweeper.gs` (replaced by `paypalService.ts` / `paypalSweeperJob.ts`) and their two in-editor test files moved to `BidManagementSpreadsheet/not-deployed/`, which `clasp push` skips (`skipSubdirectories`). The PayPal credentials were deleted from the script's Script Properties by hand. `gs-push-order.test.ts` now fails if a deployed file contains PayPal code or a project would push subfolders. **Needs a Bid-Management redeploy** to take effect.
  - **Settings page: Script ID boxes removed.** The server runs the scripts by its `SCRIPT_ID_*` Cloud Run settings and never read the three boxes, so changing them did nothing. A note now says where the IDs live. The three fields stay in `settings/global` (saved through unchanged), because `firestore.rules` requires exactly those 17 keys.
  - **Sign-in no longer asks to edit Apps Script code** (`script.projects` removed from `SCOPES`). Nothing used it. Existing sign-ins keep working. `oauth.test.ts` now checks `SCOPES` covers every scope in all three `appsscript.json` files, since one missing makes Google refuse the script run with 403.
  - Tests: backend 592 -> 602, frontend 682 -> 683, total 1274 -> 1285.

- **Apps Script document and receipt runs could make duplicates, and a failed receipt showed as a success** (found in the 2026-10-21 Apps Script integration review). Also: Cloud Run's request time limit was raised from 300s (the default) to 900s on the live service (`gcloud run services update --timeout=900`), so the app no longer gives up a minute before Apps Script's own 6-minute limit.
  - **No automatic retries:** the 8 Items documents, bidder labels, a single receipt and Print All Receipts are now sent once with `postOnce` (`src/services/google-api.ts`), not `fetchWithRetry`. A timeout or 5xx there usually means the script is still running or already finished, so the old up-to-4 retries could make up to 4 copies. With no answer at all, staff are told to check the folder before trying again (`NO_ANSWER_MESSAGE`).
  - **No overlapping runs:** the server turns away a second identical run while one is going with **409** (`runIfIdle` in `server/utils/asyncLock.ts`): per document per script, per bidder for a receipt, per spreadsheet for Print All.
  - **Print All Receipts in parts:** `/api/receipts/generate-all` takes `part` (from 1) and prints `RECEIPTS_PER_PART` (25) bidders to one PDF per request, named e.g. "Receipts - Part 2 of 4 (Garcia - Lopez)". The browser asks for each part in turn, shows "part n of m", and stops at the first failure, saying which parts were printed. With no winners it says so and runs nothing. Each part rebuilds the sorted list, so print after bidding closes.
  - **Failures reported as failures:** a `{ success: false }` from the script now returns 400 with its reason. `/generate` used to return 200 and the screen said "Receipt Doc created! Subtotal: $undefined"; `/generate-all` said "started!" whatever happened. A failed run (time limit, missing deployment) now reports the script's real error instead of the generic "ScriptError" (`server/utils/appsScriptRun.ts`, shared by the receipt and document routes), and the browser shows the error handler's `message` instead of "Internal Server Error".
  - **Apps Script projects deployed from the repo (2026-10-22).** All three live projects were replaced with the repo copy with clasp: Bid-Management, Items and Tickets. This removed the leftover files the repo had already deleted, including functions that could wipe data (`createBidMgmtSheet` deleted Bid-Management rows after row 23; `setAuctionIDsOnImportSheet` rewrote item numbers; `startCheckOutProcess` marked bidders paid without PayPal) and the old Gmail code. Each project's `appsscript.json` is now in the repo, asking only for the permissions its code uses, all of which the app's sign-in already has. Bid-Management had asked for full Gmail access, deployments and menus.
    - **"The caller does not have permission" (403) on every script run:** the scripts were linked to a different Google Cloud project (641532814621) from the app's sign-in (37624994705). Google only runs a script for an app in the same project. Fixed by hand in each script's Project Settings.
    - **"GlobalVariables is not defined" after the first push:** Apps Script stores API-uploaded files sorted by name and runs them in that order, so subclasses loaded before their base classes. `filePushOrder` didn't help (checked with `projects.getContent`). The base-class files were renamed to sort first (`00_Class_GlobalVariables.gs` ... `03_Class_DocService.gs`), and `gs-push-order.test.ts` loads every project in that order.
  - **Apps Script change:** `apiCreateAllReceipts(folderID, receipts, fileName)` puts all of `receipts` in one PDF when `fileName` is given; without it, the old 1st/2nd Half pair. **This needs a new Bid-Management script version deployed before this goes live**, or each part makes its own 1st/2nd Half pair.
  - Tests: `asyncLock.test.ts` (`runIfIdle`), `receipts.test.ts` (parts, part bounds, 409, script failure -> 400, real error text; the H1 concurrency test now expects the second request to get 409), `production.test.ts` (409, real error text), `gs-bidmanagement-receipt-manager.test.ts` (3 parameters), `Checkout.test.tsx` (parts in turn, stop at failure, nothing to print, failed receipt), `DocumentCreationTab.test.tsx` / `TicketDocumentCreationTab.test.tsx` (sent once, real reason, no answer).

- **Filter-bar dropdowns sat above the centre of the bar (user bug report, 2026-09-07, "The filter dropdown menu's off-center").** Two causes, both measured in a real browser at the reporter's 1470px width:
  - **Dropdowns were 8px too short.** Every filter dropdown passes `h-10` to match the 40px search box and buttons beside it, but `SelectTrigger` set its own height as `data-[size=default]:h-8`. An attribute selector beats any plain `h-*` class in CSS, so the `h-10` never applied: the dropdowns stayed 32px and sat at the top of the 40px row (centre 72px vs 76px for the search box). The default is now a plain `h-8` (`src/components/ui/select.tsx`), so a height passed by the caller replaces it. This also fixes the same mismatch on Bidders, Checkout and Auction Tickets. The bulk-action bar's two selects passed `h-9`, which was also being ignored; they're now written `h-8` so that screen looks exactly as it did.
  - **A scrollbar under the dropdowns pushed them up another ~7px.** The Auction Items filter row was a no-wrap, scrollable strip, and at a 1470px window it overflowed by 15px, so a horizontal scrollbar showed under the dropdowns (the grey bar in the screenshot) and the row's centre moved down with it. Bidders overflowed by about 200px. `FilterToolbar` with `filtersWrap={false}` now keeps the filters together as one group: it stays beside the search box when it fits, drops onto its own line under it when it doesn't, and scrolls sideways only when the group is wider than the whole bar (phones). The Auction Items Sort dropdown went from `w-60` to `w-52` so that bar fits on one row at 1470px; the longest sort label still has 16px to spare.
  - Result at 1470px: search box, all dropdowns, and buttons share one vertical centre, with no scrollbar, on Auction Items and Bidders. At 1440px and below the filters wrap to a second line, tidy and unscrolled.
  - Tests: `select.test.tsx` (default and caller-provided height, `size="sm"` unchanged), `FilterToolbar.test.tsx` (group wraps as a unit, default unchanged), and `AuctionItemsFilterControls.test.tsx` updated for the new Sort width. jsdom has no layout, so these pin the class strings; the alignment itself was measured with Playwright. The two new select tests and the toolbar test fail on the old code. Frontend 672 -> 676, total 1245 -> 1249.

- **ROADMAP.md L1-L9 (2026-10-21 code audit): low-severity fixes.**
  - **L1: receipts and emails use the configured tab names.** Receipt generate/generate-all, email-only/email-all, and checkout's create-an-invoice path now read and write the Config's `bidderNumbersSheet`/`bidManagementSheet` (sent in the request body, as checkout already did) instead of hardcoded `'Bidder-Numbers'`/`'Bid-Management'`. A missing name falls back to the default. Names are quoted for A1 notation by the new `quoteSheetName` (`server/utils/rangeUtils.ts`), so a name with an apostrophe works. Sheet setup/sync and the Apps Scripts still create and use the default names, so renaming the tabs is still not supported end to end.
  - **L2: checkout treats `MARKED_AS_PAID` as paid**, like the sweeper. Both now use `isPaidInvoiceStatus` (`paypalService.ts`).
  - **L3: cash/check payers no longer show a PayPal balance, and failures aren't silent.**
    - Checkout first takes the card-surcharge line off the bidder's invoice, using new `PayPalService.removeSurchargeLine`. That's a full-replace `PUT` with `send_to_recipient=false&send_to_invoicer=false`, so nobody is emailed.
    - It then records the payment at the fee-free total, which leaves no balance. Before, the payment was recorded at the surcharge-inclusive Column K total, so PayPal's ledger overstated cash by the fee.
    - If the surcharge can't be removed, the payment is recorded at the full invoice total (the old behavior), which still closes the invoice.
    - If PayPal already shows the invoice paid, no cash is recorded and staff are warned about a possible double payment.
    - If recording fails (an error or a `false` return), the bidder is still marked paid (the money is in hand). The response carries a `warning`, shown on the checkout screen, saying the invoice is still payable online.
    - Column K is deliberately left at the original total. A later receipt run compares it against the expected total to reuse the invoice; changing it would make that run cancel this invoice and send a new one.
  - **L4: check-in checks its inputs.** `check-in-with-bid` returns 400 for a non-numeric `bidValue`, and for a bidder missing from Bidder-Numbers. Before, it added the ticket bid and reported `checkedIn: true` without checking anyone in. Nothing is written in either case.
  - **L5: the 5-second Sheets read cache is per user.** Keys include a hash of the session's Google token, so one user's cached read is never served to another user who may not have access to that spreadsheet.
  - **L6: error text only for signed-in staff.** The central error handler still returns `message` to signed-in users, because several failures tell staff what to fix by hand. Requests without a session get only `{error: "Internal Server Error"}`, unless body-parser marked the error safe to show (e.g. 413).
  - **L7: Drive folder IDs are validated** (letters, digits, `-`, `_`) before being put into the Drive `q` query. Anything else returns 400.
  - **L8: invoices for bidders with no usable email go to `web-admin@orns.org`** (`FALLBACK_INVOICE_EMAIL`, `receiptDataService.ts`), still in the bidder's name, so staff get the invoice email and its payment link. Before, they went to a fake `no-reply@example.com`. "Usable" is the same `isValidEmailAddress` check the receipt emails use, now in `server/utils/emailAddress.ts`.
  - **L9: security headers on every response** (`server/middleware/securityHeaders.ts`): `Content-Security-Policy: frame-ancestors 'none'; base-uri 'self'; object-src 'none'`, `X-Frame-Options: DENY`, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `Referrer-Policy: strict-origin-when-cross-origin`, and HSTS on the deployed HTTPS build. The CSP doesn't restrict scripts, styles or connections; an allow-list would need testing against Google Fonts, Firebase and Vite.
  - Tests: new `securityHeaders.test.ts`. New cases in:
    - `receiptDataService.test.ts`: renamed tabs, including one with an apostrophe.
    - `checkoutMarkPaid.test.ts`: `MARKED_AS_PAID`; surcharge removed and cash recorded at the fee-free total; fallback to the full total; invoice already paid; PayPal recording throws, returns false, or succeeds.
    - `paypalService.test.ts`: `removeSurchargeLine` (PUT body and query, already paid, no fee line, rejected update).
    - `receiptDataService.test.ts` (L8): no email and unusable email go to `web-admin@orns.org`; a real email is used as is.
    - `tickets.test.ts`: unknown bidder, non-numeric `bidValue`.
    - `proxyRoutes.test.ts`: per-user cache, Drive ID injection.
    - `middleware.test.ts`: anonymous error body.
    - `Checkout.test.tsx`: tab names sent; warning toast.

    Each new backend case fails on the old code. Backend 533 -> 554, frontend 637 -> 638, total 1170 -> 1192.

- **ROADMAP.md M4: the PayPal sweeper runs from Cloud Scheduler instead of an in-process timer.** The `setInterval` in `server.ts` needed Cloud Run's instance-based billing (and realistically a minimum of 1 instance) to fire reliably, which costs money all year for an app used one night a year.
  - New `POST /internal/paypal-sweep` (`server/routes/schedulerRoutes.ts`, no session) runs one sweep when the `X-Sweeper-Secret` header matches `SWEEPER_TRIGGER_SECRET`. It returns 401 on a wrong secret, and 404 when the variable is unset, which is the local default.
  - A Cloud Scheduler job calls it every 5 minutes; it is resumed before the auction and paused after. Setup is in `README.md` "Deployment".
  - Removed `startPaypalSweeperSchedule` and `PAYPAL_SWEEPER_INTERVAL_MINUTES`.
  - `runPaypalSweep(trigger)` now runs one sweep at a time (a concurrent call shares the running one's result), and records Cloud Scheduler runs (`scheduledAt` in `system/paypalSweeperStatus`).
  - The status card replaces "Next expected run" with an "Automatic schedule" row, and shows **Paused** (not "Needs Attention") when the credential is fine but no scheduled run happened in the last 15 minutes.
  - Cloud Run keeps max instances = 1 (already set), minimum 0, and request-based billing.
  - Tests: new `schedulerRoutes.test.ts`; sweeper tests for scheduled-run tracking and single-flight; status card test for Paused. Backend 530 -> 533, frontend 636 -> 637, total 1166 -> 1170.

- **ROADMAP.md M1/M2/M3/M5/M6/M7 (2026-10-21 code audit): security hardening and operational fixes.**
  - **M1: unauthenticated endpoints.**
    - Removed `POST /log`. Nothing in the client called it, and it printed up to 5 MB of anyone's JSON into the server logs.
    - `POST /api/bug-reports` (still open, so a broken session can be reported):
      - `type` must be one of the dialog's three values, and `timestamp` is re-serialized as ISO 8601 or replaced with the server time.
      - The Report ID, timestamp and type columns now go through `sanitizeForSheetFormula` like the rest.
      - Every text field is capped at 50,000 characters and console activity at 200 entries.
      - Requests without a session are limited to 5 per IP per 10 minutes (429 after that) and can't attach a screenshot.
    - In production the fallback for a failed Sheet save is now one structured Cloud Logging entry (`jsonPayload.bugReport`, screenshot left out) instead of a file on Cloud Run's in-memory disk, which was lost whenever an instance recycled. Local development still writes `bug-reports/*.md`. The response's `storage` is `"log"` in that case.
  - **M2: OAuth login CSRF.** `/auth/url` stores a random one-time `state` in the session cookie and sends it to Google. `/auth/callback` rejects (403) any callback whose `state` is missing, wrong, or already used, before it exchanges the code. The domain gate now also requires the userinfo `hd` (Workspace domain) claim to be `orns.org` and the email to be verified, not just an `@orns.org` suffix.
  - **M3: PayPal sandbox/live is decided server-side.** New `server/services/sharedConfig.ts` (`getPaypalIsSandbox`) reads `paypalIsSandbox` from Firestore `settings/global` with a 30-second cache. Receipt generate/generate-all/email-only/email-all and checkout use it, so they always agree with the sweeper. The client no longer sends the flag, and a body value is ignored. If Firestore can't be read and no value has been read before, the request fails with an explicit error instead of guessing an environment. A failed refresh keeps the last value read.
  - **M5: no automatic retry of POSTs.** `server/services/google.ts` dropped `POST` from the global retry list, so a 5xx on `values.append`, `spreadsheets.batchUpdate`, Gmail send or Apps Script run is no longer repeated (if Google had already applied it, the retry added a duplicate row or email). Calls that are safe to repeat opt back in with `IDEMPOTENT_POST_RETRY` (`server/utils/googleRetry.ts`): `values.batchUpdate` when closing the auction, the transaction rollback's `values.clear`, and the clear-range route.
  - **M6: Apps Script runs the deployed version.** `scripts.run` now sends `devMode: false` unless `APPS_SCRIPT_DEV_MODE=true` (`server/utils/appsScriptMode.ts`). `devMode: true` only applied for the script's owner, so the owner ran the editor's latest saved code while everyone else ran the deployed version. **A .gs change now goes live only after a new version of that script's API-executable deployment.**
  - **M7: dependencies.** Removed unused `nodemailer`, `express-session` and `session-file-store` (and their `@types`); moved `msw` and `shadcn` to `devDependencies`; ran `npm audit fix`; and added `overrides` for `qs` (^6.16.1) and the client Firebase SDK's `@grpc/grpc-js` (^1.14.6). `npm audit --omit=dev` went from 34 vulnerabilities (2 critical) to 0. Seven high-severity findings remain, all dev-only (`braces` via the `shadcn` CLI, no fix published).
  - Tests: new `sharedConfig.test.ts` and `googleRetry.test.ts`. New cases in:
    - `auth.test.ts`: state missing, wrong, or replayed; `hd` missing.
    - `bugReportRoutes.test.ts`: type validation, timestamp/ID columns, field caps, rate limit, anonymous screenshot dropped, production log fallback.
    - `receipts.test.ts`: the body's sandbox flag is ignored.
    - `production.test.ts`: `devMode`.

    `server/test/setup.ts` mocks `sharedConfig` to sandbox for every test. Backend 511 -> 530, total 1147 -> 1166.

- **Bidder-Numbers columns L-N now always have header labels.** Column L (the PayPal-pending timestamp) never had one, and M/N were only labeled by an Email All run. One header list now lives in `server/utils/bidderNumbersHeaders.ts`: A-K plus `PayPal Pending Since`, `Receipt Emailed At`, `Receipt Emailed Invoice`. Building the tab writes all 14. On an existing tab, `ensureTrackingHeaders` fills in any blank L1/M1/N1 whenever checkout defers to PayPal or a receipt is emailed (single or batch), keeping any header someone already typed. The header write is best-effort and never blocks a checkout or email. Five new tests in `bidderNumbersHeaders.test.ts`. Totals 1142 -> 1147 (511 backend / 636 frontend).

- **ROADMAP.md H2/H3 (2026-10-21 code audit): receipt emails linked stale invoices, re-sent on every batch run, and were built from unescaped sheet text.**
  - **H2: wrong invoice link.** `processEmail` emailed whatever URL was cached in Bidder-Numbers Column J, possibly for an older total or one `resolveInvoiceUrl` had already cancelled, or a bare `https://www.paypal.com` link when there was none. Meanwhile it computed the "Pay $X" amount fresh, so the button and the link could disagree. Emails now resolve the invoice exactly as receipt printing does: a new `attachInvoice` in `receiptDataService.ts`, split out of `buildBidderReceiptWithInvoice`, runs under the `bidder-numbers:` lock. The email shows that invoice's own `adjustedTotal`. The email routes now take `paypalIsSandbox` like the generate routes, and the client sends it (`useCheckoutReceiptActions.ts`/`useCheckoutBatchActions.ts`). Without it, live mode would have emailed sandbox links.
  - **H3: re-sends.** Email All had no record of who was already emailed, so a retried or re-clicked batch emailed everyone again. It also re-read three full sheets plus userinfo per bidder, which makes quota errors and timeouts (and so retries) likely. `processAllEmails` now:
    - resolves every invoice once, under the lock, skipping bidders with no valid address (no invoice created for them);
    - records each send in new Bidder-Numbers columns M (`Receipt Emailed At`) and N (`Receipt Emailed Invoice`), with the header written on first run (see `schema.md`);
    - skips a bidder whose Column N already equals their current invoice, so a re-run only emails bidders whose total changed;
    - stops with an explicit error if a send can't be recorded.

    The route is also idempotent (`withIdempotency`, client sends a key), and the toast now reports sent/skipped/failed counts instead of "being sent... check in a few minutes".
  - **H3: unsafe construction.**
    - `createRawEmail` strips CR/LF from every header value, so a line break in a name can't inject `Bcc:`.
    - Non-ASCII subjects and From names are RFC 2047-encoded, so "José" arrives intact.
    - The recipient must be a single plain address (`isValidEmailAddress`); `"a@x.com, b@y.com"` is refused.
    - `generateEmailHtml` escapes every interpolated value, and only allows an http(s) link on the Pay button.
  - Tests: rewrote `emailService.test.ts` and `receiptService.test.ts` against stateful Sheets/Gmail/PayPal mocks (new `server/test/helpers/receiptSheets.ts`). They cover:
    - invoice create, replace-stale, and reuse;
    - the M/N records, and a re-run sending nothing;
    - only the bidder whose total changed getting re-sent;
    - invalid and multi-address skips;
    - one failed send not aborting the batch;
    - the stop when a record can't be written;
    - header injection, encoding, HTML escaping, and non-http links.

    Because `processEmail`/`processAllEmails` changed signature, these can't be run against the pre-fix code the way the H1/H5/H6 tests were.

- **ROADMAP.md H5 (2026-10-21 code audit): the PayPal sweeper could overwrite a checkout.** `runPaypalSweepInner` worked from a snapshot of Bidder-Numbers and wrote rows without the `bidder-numbers:` lock. A bidder staff marked "Cash" mid-sweep could then be overwritten with "Failed Verification - Follow Up Required". The PayPal status lookup still runs outside the lock (so slow PayPal responses never hold up checkout), but each row's writes now happen in `applySweepResultLocked` under the lock. It re-reads the row first and leaves it alone unless it is still the same bidder, still pending, and on the same invoice. Three new tests (staff marked Cash after the snapshot; checkout re-ran on a new invoice; the sweep waits for a held lock) all fail against the pre-fix code.

- **ROADMAP.md H6 (2026-10-21 code audit): idempotency keys didn't stop concurrent duplicates.** `checkIdempotency`/`saveIdempotency` only recorded a key once its request had finished, so a retry arriving mid-request (exactly what `fetchWithRetry` sends after a timeout) ran the whole action again. Keys were also global across routes.
  - Replaced both with `withIdempotency(scope, key, fn)` (`server/utils/idempotency.ts`). It tracks in-flight keys, so a concurrent retry waits for and shares the first run's result. A failure is never cached, so a later retry runs again. Keys are namespaced per route.
  - All 10 mutating routes moved over (bundle process/restore, settings update, item add/delete/bulk-update, record-winning-bid, checkout, walk-in, check-in-with-bid), plus email-all.
  - A duplicate checkout now returns the original's status (202 for a deferred PayPal verification) instead of always 200; the client accepts either.
  - New unit tests cover in-flight sharing, failure not cached, per-route scoping, and TTL. A new route test (two simultaneous checkouts with the same key: 3 writes, not 6) fails against the pre-fix code.

- Totals: 1129 -> 1142 (506 backend / 636 frontend). `tsc --noEmit` clean.

- **ROADMAP.md H1 (2026-10-21 code audit): batch receipt generation could send bidders duplicate PayPal invoices.** `buildAllBidderReceiptsWithInvoices` (`server/services/receiptDataService.ts`) created *and sent* each bidder's invoice (PayPal emails the bidder on send) but only saved the URLs/amounts to Bidder-Numbers J/K in one `batchUpdate` after the whole loop. Any run that died part-way - a Cloud Run request timeout, a Sheets 429, or `fetchWithRetry` re-running the POST after a 5xx - left invoices sent but unrecorded, so the next run created and sent new ones. The receipt routes also didn't take the `bidder-numbers:` lock that checkout's own invoice creation holds, so a receipt and a checkout (or two receipt requests) for the same bidder could both create an invoice.
  - Each new invoice's J/K is now written immediately after it's created (shared `persistInvoice` helper, also used by `buildBidderReceiptWithInvoice`). If that write fails, the batch stops with an error naming the bidder, the invoice URL, and the sheet row to fill by hand, rather than skipping the bidder and carrying on sending more invoices that nothing records. A failure to *create* an invoice still just skips that bidder, as before.
  - `/api/receipts/generate` and `/generate-all` (`server/routes/receiptRoutes.ts`) now wrap invoice resolution in `withLock('bidder-numbers:<spreadsheetId>')`, at route level rather than inside the service, since `checkoutMarkPaid` already holds that same non-reentrant lock when it calls `buildBidderReceiptWithInvoice`. A retried or concurrent run now waits, then reuses the invoices the first run saved.
  - Tests: replaced the "batch-writes the changed ones" test with three on a stateful Bidder-Numbers mock: create/save ordering per bidder, the batch stopping on a failed J/K write, and a re-run after an interrupted batch creating no duplicates. Also added a route test that two concurrent `/generate` requests create exactly one invoice. All four fail against the pre-fix code. Backend 505 -> 508 (1154 total). `tsc --noEmit` clean.

- **ROADMAP.md D1-D7 (2026-10-21 code audit): docs that no longer matched the code.**
  - `file-reference.md`: rebuilt the topography from the real tree. It listed seven files that don't exist (`server/services/paypal.ts`, `server/middleware/auth.ts`/`cache.ts`/`session.ts`, `server/utils/sheets.ts`/`crypto.ts`, `src/lib/firebase.ts`) and left out most real ones.
  - `patterns.md`: the protected Firebase file is `src/services/firebase.ts` (matching `AGENTS.md`), not `src/lib/firebase.ts`. Rewrote the PayPal section: `apiSendAllEmails`/`apiSendReceiptEmail` don't exist (emailing is Node-side via Gmail), and invoice resolution is Node's `resolveInvoiceUrl`.
  - `schema.md`: replaced the Bidder-Numbers table (it listed Phone/Ticket Type/Table Number/Status/Notes columns) with the real A-K header from `syncService.ts` plus Column L's sweeper timestamp. In the Firestore field list, dropped `auctionItemsSheet` (not in `Config`/`firestore.rules`) and added `paypalIsSandbox`. Replaced the nonexistent `/api/checkout/calculate-total` endpoint with where `calculatePayPalSurcharge` really runs.
  - `testing-batches.md`: test counts updated (were 502 backend / 1148 total).
  - `README.md` (docs/agents): `SESSION_SECRET` added to the hard-required env vars; `/server/utils/` description no longer claims "cryptographic session managers".
  - `refactor-opportunities.md` was stale (it reported Code Hardening "fully closed" and its "Passed Audit Inspection" list cited `server/services/paypal.ts`). Cleared to a pointer; the live backlog now lives in `ROADMAP.md`. `AGENTS.md`'s registry and the backlog references in `component-standards.md`, `react-patterns.md`, and `performance.md` now point there.

- **User-reported UI bug: "Filter bar is clipped" - Bidders' Sort by dropdown trigger cut off its own selected label text ("Bidder # (Low to Hig...").** Root cause: `BiddersFilterControls.tsx`'s Sort by `SelectTrigger` was fixed at `w-50` (200px), too narrow for its longest label ("Status (Checked-In First)", 26 characters) once the icon/padding/chevron overhead is accounted for.
  - **First attempt was wrong and the user caught it**: widening the trigger alone (`w-50` -> `w-72`) fixed the trigger's own internal clipping, but pushed the whole 4-filter row's total width past what fits in the toolbar at normal desktop widths, forcing horizontal scrolling to see the Sort by filter at all - the opposite of "show all options without scrolling," which is what the user actually wanted (and immediately reported after testing). Also applied the same oversized width to `AuctionItemsFilterControls.tsx`'s twin Sort by trigger (same `w-50` pattern, same latent bug) for consistency, with the same regression.
  - **Fixed properly with three smaller, combined changes** instead of one large one: (1) shortened the two long "Status (...)" sort labels to drop the redundant "Status" prefix (`"Status (Checked-In First)"` -> `"Checked-In First"`, `"Status (Not Present First)"` -> `"Not Present First"`) - the shorter strings mean the trigger only needs to fit "Bidder # (Low to High)" (22 characters) instead of 26, letting a smaller width increase suffice (`w-50` -> `w-64`, not `w-72`); (2) sized `AuctionItemsFilterControls.tsx`'s Sort by trigger to `w-60` (its own longest label, "Item # (Low to High)", is 21 characters); (3) per the user's own suggestion, narrowed `Bidders.tsx`'s search input (`searchClassName`, `md:max-w-xs` -> `md:max-w-52`) to free up the room the wider Sort by trigger needs, since a search box's placeholder ("Search bidders...") tolerates a narrower box far more gracefully than a dropdown's selected-value label does. `AuctionItemsTab.tsx`'s own search input was left untouched - its placeholder is much longer ("Search by name, description, donor...") and narrowing it risks clipping that instead.
  - Updated `BiddersFilterControls.test.tsx`/`AuctionItemsFilterControls.test.tsx`'s width regression tests to assert the final `w-64`/`w-60` classes (not the over-corrected `w-72`), and added a test confirming the two Status labels render shortened. Sanity-checked by reverting to the pre-fix width/labels and confirming both tests failed with the real symptom, then restored. Test count: 1136 -> 1139 (493 backend / 646 frontend); updated everywhere asserted. `tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run build` clean.

- **2026-09-07 standards + testing audit findings (bug fixes only - see `refactor-opportunities.md` for the remaining logged-but-not-yet-fixed items from this same audit).**
  - **`useWinnersData.ts`'s "bidder already checked out" toast violated `patterns.md`'s winner-notification rule** - it used `toast.info(...)` with a 5-second auto-dismiss and no confirmation action, instead of the `duration: Infinity` + `"I have confirmed"` action pattern already correctly implemented in `Bidders.tsx`/`Checkout.tsx`/`Dashboard.tsx`. This was the real production gap, not a redundant duplicate of those already-correct instances: both Bid Entry modes (`QuickBidEntry.tsx`/`BatchBidEntry.tsx`) record winning bids through this exact hook. Also never named the item won (only the raw `itemId` was in scope). Fixed by switching to `toast.warning("Checked Out Warning", { description, duration: Infinity, action: { label: "I have confirmed", ... } })`, matching `useDashboardState.ts`'s existing special-winners warning text/shape exactly, and resolving the item's real name via a new `items: AuctionItem[]` parameter threaded into `useWinnersData` from `AuctionContext.tsx` (falls back to `"Item <id>"` if not found, mirroring the existing `bidderName` fallback pattern one line above).
  - **`Checkout.test.tsx` never verified `itemsSpreadsheetId` reaches the checkout-mark-paid request body** - its `ConfigContext` mock was missing `auctionItemsSpreadsheetId` entirely (always `undefined`), so nothing guarded the field the PayPal-invoice-on-checkout fix depends on (`useCheckoutPaymentFlow.ts` threads `config.auctionItemsSpreadsheetId` into the POST body so the server can create an invoice on a credit-card checkout with none yet) from silently regressing. Added the field to the mock and a new test asserting the credit-card "Mark paid" request body includes it. Fixing the mock also surfaced that the "Print All Receipts"/"Email All Winners" batch actions *already* send `itemsSpreadsheetId` too (a real, correct, pre-existing behavior the incomplete mock had been masking) - updated those two tests' exact-body assertions to include it rather than weakening them to a partial match.
  - Sanity-checked both fixes by reverting each and confirming the new/updated tests failed with the real symptom, then restored. Test count: 1092 -> 1094 (493 backend / 601 frontend); updated everywhere asserted. `tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run build` clean.

- **User-reported workflow issue: "Attendee Tickets workflow challenges" - no way on the Attendee Tickets page to edit tickets purchased, no way to add guests to an attendee, and no way to collect guests (or their own-bidder-number opt-in) when adding a new entry.** The Edit dialog only ever let staff edit an *existing* guest's own fields plus contact info and payment status - `ticketsPurchased`, `paymentMethod`, and `totalPaid` had no column mapping in `updateField` at all, and there was no way to add a new guest slot or remove one. The Add dialog collected no guest data whatsoever. Per explicit user confirmation: Payment Method/Total Paid should become editable too (General Tickets only - see below), a 3-guest cap per attendee is fine (matches the sheet's fixed column layout), and "own bidder number" staying General-Tickets-only (no sheet column exists for it on Staff/Sponsor) with no automatic bidder-number generation is acceptable as-is.
  - **Unified Add and Edit's layout**, per the user's explicit ask ("update the Add Tickets and Edit Tickets so that they share the same essential component"): both now use the same sectioned `FormSection` wrapper already built for the Auction Item dialogs (renamed from `AuctionItemFormSection.tsx` to the generic `FormSection.tsx`, since it was never item-specific) and a new shared `AttendeeGuestsEditor.tsx` for guest collection - one guest-add/remove/edit UI, used by both dialogs, instead of Edit having its own bespoke guest markup and Add having none.
  - **New `src/lib/attendeeSheetColumns.ts`** is now the single source of truth for the ticket sheets' guest-column layout (previously duplicated between `attendeeParsing.ts`'s raw row-index reads and `useTicketAttendeeState.ts`'s own hardcoded letter map) and the payment-column layout (new). Exposes `guestColumnsForSheet`/`guestRowIndicesForSheet`/`paymentColumnsForSheet`, each branching on the general "tickets" sheet's extra "own bidder number" column vs. the tighter-packed staff/sponsor layout.
  - **`useTicketAttendeeState.ts`**: `updateField` extended with `ticketsPurchased` (col E), `hasGuests` (col F), `paymentMethod`/`totalPaid` (via `paymentColumnsForSheet`), and guest `ownBidderNumber` (via `guestColumnsForSheet`) - all reusing the existing single-cell optimistic-update + rollback path unchanged for editing a field on an *existing* guest. Adding or removing a whole guest slot needed different logic (no prior guest object for the per-field optimistic map to find/merge into) - new `addGuestSlot`/`removeGuestSlot` write/clear a guest's entire 3-4 column range in one call and update `guestsInfo` as a whole-object insert/removal. `handleEditSave` now reconciles all 3 fixed guest slots against the draft (not just what's in the draft) so a removed guest is actually cleared on the sheet, not silently skipped, and re-derives `hasGuests` only when the guest *count* actually changes during this save (comparing against the stored, sometimes already-stale `hasGuests` flag directly would have "fixed" pre-existing unrelated mismatches as a side effect of an unrelated field edit). `handleAddSubmit` now places up to 3 guests into the correct row-index positions via `guestRowIndicesForSheet` (general "tickets" sheet shifted vs. staff/sponsor unshifted, exactly matching `attendeeParsing.ts`'s read side) and sets `hasGuests`.
  - **Payment Method/Total Paid are only shown as editable for the General Tickets sheet** - `attendeeParsing.ts` always overrides staff/sponsor rows to hardcoded `Comp`/`Paid`/`$0` regardless of what those columns hold, so making them "editable" there would have silently reverted on the next refresh, a worse bug than not exposing them at all. "Own bidder number" is similarly General-Tickets-only in both dialogs, since staff/sponsor sheets have no column for it.
  - **`hasMoreGuestsThanTicketsPurchased` (`attendeeValidation.ts`)** is now actually wired up, per its own prior docblock's instruction ("if a feature that lets someone add a guest or edit ticketsPurchased is ever built, it should call this first and block the save") - both dialogs call it on every change and disable Save (with an inline explanation) while guests exceed tickets purchased, instead of just being available for a future feature to use.
  - Updated `EditAttendeeDialog.test.tsx`'s stale "shows the own-bidder-number badge" test to match the new editable checkbox (was a read-only badge). Added `AddAttendeeDialog.test.tsx` guest-collection/validation tests, `EditAttendeeDialog.test.tsx` tests for ticketsPurchased/Payment Method/Total Paid editing and guest add/remove/validation, and `useTicketAttendeeState.test.ts` tests for the new column mappings, `addGuestSlot`/`removeGuestSlot`'s whole-range writes (both sheet layouts), the `hasGuests` re-derivation, and `handleAddSubmit`'s guest row-placement (both sheet layouts) plus its own guest/ticket validation block. Also renamed `AuctionItemFormSection.test.tsx` to `FormSection.test.tsx` alongside the component rename. Test count: 1072 -> 1092 (493 backend / 599 frontend); updated everywhere asserted. `tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run build` clean. No UI browser check was possible in this environment (no browser automation tool available) - verified via the full RTL suite (real Dialog/Select components, unmocked, in the dialog-level tests) instead; a manual click-through is worth doing before relying on this in production.

- **User-reported gap: the 11 "standard" items every auction is supposed to have (raffle tickets, Open Appeal tiers, "A Night with the ORNS Staff," Auction Tickets) had silently stopped existing anywhere, and the user couldn't find them in the Items tab.** Traced via git history to a real regression from an earlier hardening pass: these items used to be synthesized (id + name only, no other fields) by `syncService.ts`'s old "Sync Step 7" - but only into Bid Management's own local, now-deleted Items snapshot, never as real rows in the master Auction-Items spreadsheet. Once that snapshot mechanism was correctly removed (see "Eliminated Bid Management's separate, drifting Items snapshot" below), nothing created these items anywhere - they'd never actually existed in the real spreadsheet to begin with, so their disappearance was invisible until someone went looking for them.
  - Fixed by seeding them for real: `auctionSetupService.ts`'s year-rollover flow (`/setup-new`) already clears `Auction-Items!A2:Z` on every new auction (backing up the old year's spreadsheet to the archive folder first) - added a `STANDARD_ITEM_ROWS` constant and a write step immediately after that clear, so the 11 items are re-created as part of the same annual ritual the organizer already runs, instead of relying on them being manually re-added (and then re-wiped the following year, since the clear step targets the same reused spreadsheet, not a fresh copy). Category names match the id-range convention `categoryHelpers.ts` already encoded (1401->Movie Night, 1501->Raffle Tickets, 1601->Open Appeal); Value/Opening Bid/Min Raise deliberately left blank per the user's explicit choice, matching the old removed seeding, so the organizer sets real dollar amounts once and they carry forward with the spreadsheet untouched by future setups. `status: "Active"` is required (not a stylistic choice) since `Items.tsx`'s Auction Catalog only shows Active items.
  - **Renumbered "Auction Tickets" from 1606 to 1701**, per explicit user correction: since it's a real category of its own now (not a sub-item living inside 1601-1605's Open Appeal range), it deserves its own hundred-block base, matching every other category's convention. This item ID was hardcoded in three places, not one - `server/services/bidderService.ts`'s `registerWalkInBidder` (records a walk-in bidder's ticket price as this item ID in the bid grid) and `checkInBidderWithBid` (same, for the ticket-payment check-in-with-bid flow), plus this fix's own new seed row - so it's now a single shared `AUCTION_TICKETS_ITEM_ID`/`AUCTION_TICKETS_CATEGORY_BASE` export from `categoryHelpers.ts` (server copy) instead of three independent literals that could drift apart. Also added an explicit `"auction ticket"` check to `getCategoryBase()` *before* the existing generic `clean.includes("ticket")` branch (both the server and client copies of `categoryHelpers.ts`) - without that ordering, "Auction Tickets" would have silently matched the generic Sports/Tickets branch and resolved to base 1201 instead of 1701, since it also contains the substring "ticket".
  - Added a per-item `Allow Multiple Winners` column (M) to the master Auction-Items schema - set `true` on all 11 seeded standard items, per an explicit product decision made while discussing a Bid Entry redesign (an Artifact mockup, not yet implemented): these items are pledges/ticket sales, not real auction lots, so many different bidders legitimately "win" the same item ID. An explicit per-item flag was chosen deliberately over category-name pattern matching, since a renamed category next year would silently break detection at the exact moment it matters.
  - Added `server/test/categoryHelpers.test.ts` (4 tests - `getCategoryBase`/`resequenceCategoryIds` had **zero** test coverage anywhere in the repo before this: "Auction Tickets" resolves to 1701 not the generic 1201, real Sports/Tickets and Raffle Tickets categories are unaffected by the new branch, and `AUCTION_TICKETS_ITEM_ID`/`AUCTION_TICKETS_CATEGORY_BASE` never drift apart) and a new test to `server/test/auction-setup.test.ts` (the 11 standard items are written with the correct IDs/categories/Active status/Allow-Multiple-Winners flag, right after the existing `Auction-Items!A2:Z` clear). Updated `server/test/tickets.test.ts`'s stale `"1606"` literal assertion to the new shared constant. Sanity-checked both the seeding step and the category-collision guard independently by reverting each and confirming its own test failed with the real symptom (the seed write never happening; "Auction Tickets" resolving to 1201), then restored both. Test count: 1016 → 1021 (491 backend / 530 frontend); updated everywhere asserted. `tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run build` clean. Doesn't touch Apps Script (this whole path is Node-side).

- **User-reported bug: "PayPal Integration Issue" - checking out a bidder via Credit Card/PayPal (sandbox mode) created no invoice at all.** Screenshots showed the bidder correctly stamped "PayPal Verification Pending" in the sheet, but PayPal's own Sandbox Invoicing dashboard showed zero invoices ever created - so there was nothing for the customer to actually pay, and nothing for `paypalSweeperJob.ts` to ever resolve.
  - Root cause, traced end-to-end: `checkoutMarkPaid`'s (`server/services/auctionService.ts`) `credit_card` branch has always been lookup-only - it reads whatever invoice URL already sits in the Bidder-Numbers sheet's column J and checks its live PayPal status, but **never creates a new invoice itself**. Invoice creation has only ever lived in `receiptDataService.ts`'s `resolveInvoiceUrl`/`buildBidderReceiptWithInvoice`, wired up exclusively from the `/generate`/`/generate-all` receipt routes. So checkout only ever worked correctly for a bidder whose receipt had already been generated/printed at least once - for anyone else, `extractInvoiceIdFromUrl` returned `null` on an empty column J, the `if (invoiceId)` guard around `getInvoiceStatus` was skipped entirely, and execution fell straight into the "deferred" branch with `liveStatus` still at its initial `"UNPAID"` value - silently stamping "PayPal Verification Pending" with no invoice anywhere. The existing test (`checkoutMarkPaid.test.ts`'s "defers without calling getInvoiceStatus when there's no invoice ID to check") had actually locked this broken behavior in as if it were correct.
  - Fixed by having the `credit_card` branch call the same `buildBidderReceiptWithInvoice` the receipt routes use when `invoiceId` is falsy, creating the invoice (and writing its URL/amount to columns J/K, same as the receipt path does) before falling into the existing deferred flow - a freshly created invoice can't already be "PAID", so the rest of the branch's logic needed no changes. Added `itemsSpreadsheetId` to `CheckoutParams` (`auctionService.ts`) and threaded it through `bidRoutes.ts`'s `/checkout-mark-paid` route, `useCheckoutPaymentFlow.ts`'s request body, and `useCheckoutState.ts`'s composition (the value itself already existed everywhere in this chain - `Checkout.tsx` already has `config.auctionItemsSpreadsheetId` for its receipt actions - it just wasn't being passed to the payment flow). Missing `itemsSpreadsheetId` now surfaces a clear `"Auction Items Spreadsheet ID is required to create a PayPal invoice."` error instead of silently deferring; a failure inside invoice creation itself (e.g. the bidder has no resolvable won items) now surfaces that underlying error too, instead of being swallowed.
  - Rewrote `checkoutMarkPaid.test.ts`'s stale "no invoice ID" test into three real ones: invoice creation is actually attempted (and its result correctly still defers, still skipping `getInvoiceStatus`); a missing `itemsSpreadsheetId` throws the clear error without attempting creation; a failure from invoice creation itself propagates as the real underlying error. Also fixed 3 real integration-test failures this correctly surfaced in `bid-management.test.ts` - its credit_card checkout tests were exercising the old broken lookup-only path against a Bid-Management mock with no resolvable won item for bidder 42, so once the fix made checkout actually attempt invoice creation, `buildBidderReceipt` legitimately failed to find one. Fixed the mocks to include a real won item (matching the item each test's own `record-winning-bid` call already established), added `itemsSpreadsheetId` to each request body, and updated the idempotency test's `updateCallCount` assertion from 2 to 3 (the new J:K invoice-URL write is a real, additional sheet write, not a test artifact). Sanity-checked the core fix by reverting it and confirming all 3 new `checkoutMarkPaid.test.ts` tests failed with the real symptom, then restored. Test count: 1014 → 1016 (486 backend / 530 frontend); updated everywhere asserted. `tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run build` clean. Doesn't touch Apps Script (this whole path was already Node-side).

- **User-reported bug: "Search & Filter Actions inside the main content area"** - the Auction Items Management page was the only one of the four redesigned pages where the search bar and filter dropdowns lived inside the main content `Card` (as a bespoke bar between `CardHeader` and `CardContent`) instead of above it, matching `Items.tsx`/`Bidders.tsx`/`TicketAttendeeTab.tsx`'s shared `FilterToolbar.tsx` convention. This inconsistency was also the root cause of the whitespace double-gap bugs fixed just above/below this entry - a bespoke Card-child bar with its own padding will always be fighting `Card`'s automatic `gap-4` on both sides.
  - Deleted `AuctionItemsFilterBar.tsx` (the old self-contained, labeled-field bar with its own border/background/padding) and replaced it with `AuctionItemsFilterControls.tsx`, matching `BiddersFilterControls.tsx`'s convention exactly: just the Category/Item Type/Sort `Select`s plus the "Reset Filters" button, rendered inside `FilterToolbar.tsx`'s `filters` slot. The search `Input` itself moved to `FilterToolbar`'s own `searchValue`/`onSearchChange` props (previously a labeled field inside the old bar).
  - `AuctionItemsTab.tsx`: now renders `<FilterToolbar filtersWrap={false} .../>` above the `Card`, with the "Add New Item" button / "Auction Closed" badge moved into `FilterToolbar`'s `trailing` slot (mirroring `Bidders.tsx`'s `WalkInTicketDialog`/badge placement) instead of living in a `flex flex-row justify-between` `CardHeader` override. `CardHeader` is now bare (title + description only), and `CardContent` is back to a plain `p-0` - the `-mt-4` cancellation hack from the previous double-gap fix is no longer needed, since the filter bar is no longer a `Card` sibling at all.
  - Updated stale docblock references to the deleted `AuctionItemsFilterBar.tsx` filename in `FilterToolbar.tsx`, `AuctionItemsList.tsx`, `src/lib/itemBadgeColors.ts`, and `src/components/ui/select.test.tsx` (comments only, no behavior change).
  - Test changes: deleted `AuctionItemsFilterBar.test.tsx`, added `AuctionItemsFilterControls.test.tsx` (same 3 `SelectValue`/popup-width regression tests carried over unchanged, plus a new "Reset Filters" click test). `AuctionItemsTab.test.tsx`'s existing Category/Type/Sort/Search tests needed no changes (still index into `getAllByTestId("mock-select")`/query by placeholder text, both unaffected by the move) - only its `-mt-4` layout test was replaced with one asserting the filter bar no longer renders as a `flex-row` `CardHeader` child. Sanity-checked by reverting the `CardHeader` className and confirming the new test failed with the real symptom, then restored. Test count: 993 → 993 (net zero: 4 old tests removed, 4 new tests added). `tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run build` clean.

- **Two more "too much whitespace" UI issues reported after the Auction Tickets/Auction Items redesigns: extra space under the Attendee List's header/subheader, and extra space above the Auction Items Database's "Select all" bar.** Both traced to the same root cause - `Card`'s own `flex flex-col gap-4` (`src/components/ui/card.tsx`) already inserts a 16px gap between every pair of its children, so any child that *also* carries its own bottom padding before a `border-b` stacks a second gap on top of that one.
  - `TicketAttendeeTab.tsx`: `CardHeader className="pb-3"` was redundant with `Card`'s `gap-4` - `CardHeader` has no default vertical padding of its own, so the `pb-3` (12px) simply added on top of the 16px flex gap that already separates it from `CardContent`. Removed the override entirely (bare `<CardHeader>`), matching `Items.tsx`'s convention (a page not reported as buggy).
  - `AuctionItemsTab.tsx`'s Auction Items Database: `AuctionItemsFilterBar.tsx` is rendered as a sibling `Card` child *between* `CardHeader` and `CardContent` (unlike the other three redesigned pages' shared `FilterToolbar.tsx`, which sits outside/above the `Card` entirely) - so its own `pb-6` (24px) stacked with `Card`'s `gap-4` (16px) a second time between the filter bar's `border-b` and `AuctionItemsList`'s "Select all" bar. Went through two iterations here, each caught by a follow-up screenshot: first tried reducing the filter bar's `pb-6` to `pb-3` then `pb-2` alone, but a Tailwind flex `gap` can't be reduced selectively for just one boundary between siblings - shrinking the filter bar's own padding fixed the *after* side but left the space *before* its border too tight (the labels/inputs crowded the line). The actual fix needed both sides addressed independently: `AuctionItemsFilterBar.tsx`'s root padding went back up to `pb-4` (comfortable room between the controls and the border), while `AuctionItemsTab.tsx`'s `CardContent` got an explicit `-mt-4` to cancel out `Card`'s automatic `gap-4` that would otherwise reappear after the border - so the "Select all" bar now sits right under the line, with no doubled gap on either side.
  - A follow-up screenshot on the Auction Items Database also flagged a related, but distinct, issue on the same "Select all" bar once the double-gap was fixed: `AuctionItemsList.tsx`'s select-all bar carried a `bg-slate-50/60` tint that visually read as a highlighted box wrapping only the checkbox+label, making the large amount of unused space to its right ("not centered") look like a layout defect instead of blending into the row list the way every other row does (no other row-list bar in the app - `AttendeesList.tsx`, `BiddersList.tsx`, `ItemsList.tsx` - tints its header/select-all region this way). Removed the tint, leaving just the existing `border-b` divider.
  - Added `TicketAttendeeTab.test.tsx`'s `TicketAttendeeTab - Layout` describe block (asserts the `CardHeader` no longer carries a `pb-3` token), `AuctionItemsFilterBar.test.tsx`'s `AuctionItemsFilterBar - Layout` describe block (asserts the root div carries `pb-4`, not `pb-6`), and `AuctionItemsTab.test.tsx`'s `AuctionItemsTab - Layout` describe block (asserts the "Select all" bar no longer carries `bg-slate-50`, and that `CardContent` carries the `-mt-4` cancellation). Sanity-checked all four fixes independently by reverting each and confirming its own new test failed with the real symptom, then restored each. Test count: 989 → 993 (484 backend / 509 frontend); updated everywhere asserted. `tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run build` clean.

- **Two UI issues reported right after the Bidders tab redesign: too much white space under the page header/subheader, and the filter row (Status/Payment/Source/Sort) wrapping onto 2-3 lines instead of staying on one row.** Both were introduced or exposed by that same redesign - the second one directly: adding the new "Sort by" select as a 4th filter pushed the filter row's total width past what fit on one line, so it wrapped internally.
  - The white space: `Bidders.tsx`'s `CardHeader` had `className="px-4 py-6 md:px-6"` - a much larger vertical padding override than every sibling page uses (`Items.tsx` uses the plain default `CardHeader` with no override at all; `TicketAttendeeTab.tsx` uses just `pb-3`). Fixed to `pb-3`, matching `TicketAttendeeTab.tsx`'s convention exactly (the same page this whole redesign effort is modeled on).
  - The wrapping filters: `FilterToolbar.tsx`'s filters wrapper always used `flex-wrap`, fine for 1-3 short filters but not 4. Added a new `filtersWrap` prop (default `true`, so every other caller - `TicketAttendeeTab.tsx`'s single filter - is unaffected) - when `false`, the filters wrapper uses `flex-nowrap overflow-x-auto` instead, keeping every filter on one row (horizontally scrollable as a last resort on very narrow viewports rather than wrapping). `Bidders.tsx` passes `filtersWrap={false}`; each of `BiddersFilterControls.tsx`'s 4 `SelectTrigger`s got `shrink-0` so they don't get visually squished in the now-nowrap row.
  - Added `src/components/FilterToolbar.test.tsx` (2 tests: the default `filtersWrap=true` behavior is unchanged for every existing caller; `filtersWrap={false}` produces `flex-nowrap overflow-x-auto` instead) and 2 tests to `Bidders.test.tsx` (the filter wrapper carries `flex-nowrap` not `flex-wrap`; the Bidder Check-In card's header no longer carries the old `py-6`). Sanity-checked both independently by reverting each fix and confirming its own test failed with the real symptom (the wrapper still `flex-wrap`; the header still carrying `py-6`), then restored both. Test count: 961 → 965 (484 backend / 481 frontend); updated everywhere asserted. `tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run build` clean.

### Changed

- **The app no longer assumes it runs inside Google AI Studio (2026-10-21).** Development is now Claude Code in this repo -> GitHub -> a Cloud Build trigger on `main` -> Cloud Run service `orns-auction-manager-gh` (<https://orns-auction-manager.cloud.run>). AI Studio-era workarounds and rules were removed:
  - **Iframe token bridge removed; the session cookie is the only credential.** AI Studio ran the app in an iframe, where the `orns_session` cookie was blocked as third-party. To work around that, `/auth/callback` posted the OAuth tokens (refresh token included) to the opener window, the client kept them in `localStorage`, `fetchWithRetry` sent them on every request as `x-google-tokens`, and `server/middleware/tokenRestorer.ts` turned that header back into a session. The app is now always a top-level page, so all of that is gone: `tokenRestorer.ts` deleted; the callback page no longer contains any tokens; `useGoogleAuth.ts` always does a plain redirect (no popup); `useApiAuthStatus.ts` no longer listens for postMessages and deletes any `google_tokens` copy a browser still has; `google-api.ts` sends no auth header. Closes most of ROADMAP.md's M2 (refresh token in `localStorage`, no audience check on header tokens, unpruned validation cache).
  - **`server/middleware/cookiePolicy.ts` deleted.** It switched the cookie to `SameSite=None` for iframe requests (detected via `ais-dev-`/`ais-pre-` hosts or `Sec-Fetch-Dest: iframe`). The cookie is now set once in `app.ts`: `SameSite=Lax`, `httpOnly`, and `Secure` in the deployed production build.
  - **CORS middleware and the `cors` dependency removed.** The allowlist served a Tauri desktop shell that doesn't exist (`tauri://`) and accepted lookalike origins such as `http://localhost.attacker.com` (ROADMAP.md H4). Every API call is same-origin, so no CORS headers are needed.
  - **Local runs work again (ROADMAP.md H7).** Commit `e937903` had emptied `server/env.ts`, so nothing loaded `.env` and `npm run dev` stopped at boot on a missing `SESSION_SECRET`. Restored the `dotenv` load. Verified from a clean shell: 35 variables loaded from `.env`, server up, `/api/health` ok, OAuth redirect URI `http://localhost:<PORT>/auth/callback`.
  - **New: `PAYPAL_SWEEPER_INTERVAL_MINUTES=0` turns the background PayPal sweep off** (`startPaypalSweeperSchedule` returns `null`). A local server reads the same Firestore config as production, so without this it would sweep the live Bidder-Numbers sheet alongside Cloud Run's. `.env.example` now defaults to `0`.
  - Smaller cleanups:
    - `oauth.ts` upgrades any non-localhost `http://` `APP_URL` to `https://`, instead of a list of AI Studio host patterns.
    - The "check the Secrets panel in AI Studio" messages in `oauth.ts`/`validateEnv.ts` now point to `.env` or the Cloud Run service's variables.
    - Removed the `aistudio-build` Gemini User-Agent and `vite.config.ts`'s `DISABLE_HMR` switch, so Vite HMR is always on.
    - Removed the Tauri leftovers from `boot.ts`/`.gitignore`/`dev:watch`.
    - Deleted the AI Studio applet files `metadata.json` and `firebase-blueprint.json` (nothing referenced them).
    - Renamed the package from the template name `react-example` to `orns-auction-manager`.
    - Updated the Apps Script bridge-class header comments. The `GoogleAIStudio` class names are unchanged, since renaming them means re-pasting all three script projects.
  - **Docs:**
    - Removed the AI Studio-era "never delete `bidRoutes.ts`" and "do not edit `firebase.ts`/`ConfigContext.tsx`/`firestore.rules`" rules from `AGENTS.md`/`patterns.md`.
    - Replaced the two-batch test mandate (an AI Studio sandbox memory limit; the full suite runs in one ~20 s pass locally) with `npm run test:quiet` + `npm run lint`.
    - Added the rule never to push straight to `main`, since that deploys to production.
    - New "How This App Is Built and Shipped" section in `AGENTS.md`.
    - New "Running Locally" guide and rewritten Deployment section in `docs/agents/README.md`.
    - Rewrote the root `README.md`, which was still AI Studio's template.
    - Rewrote `testing-batches.md`.
  - Tests:
    - Route tests now sign in with a real signed `orns_session` cookie (`server/test/helpers/session.ts`) instead of the removed header.
    - Removed the cookiePolicy/tokenRestorer/postMessage/popup tests along with their code.
    - Added tests that the old header no longer authenticates, that the callback page never contains tokens, that a leftover `localStorage` token copy is deleted and never sent, that a postMessage can't plant tokens, that a localhost `APP_URL` stays `http`, and that the sweeper schedule can be turned off.
    - Totals: 1154 -> 1129 (493 backend / 636 frontend). `tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` clean.

- **Closed the last open finding from the 2026-09-07 audit: `FormSection.tsx`'s hardcoded `text-[11px]` -> `text-xxs`**, finishing the audit's "Code quality" section entirely (and the whole 2026-09-07 audit, across all its sections). `tailwind-style-guide.md`'s Type Scale section names this exact case: no sanctioned 11px step exists, so "a little smaller than `text-xs`" should use this app's own `text-xxs` (10px) token rather than inventing an in-between arbitrary value. One-line fix, shared by all 4 sectioned dialogs (`AddAttendeeDialog.tsx`, `EditAttendeeDialog.tsx`, `AddAuctionItemDialog.tsx`, `EditAuctionItemDialog.tsx`) via `FormSection.tsx`. Added a regression test to `FormSection.test.tsx` asserting the label carries `text-xxs` and not `text-[11px]` - sanity-checked by reverting the fix and confirming it failed with the real symptom, then restored. Test count: 1135 -> 1136 (493 backend / 643 frontend); updated everywhere asserted. `tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run build` clean.

- **Closed the last "Redundant/duplicated logic" finding from the 2026-09-07 audit: extracted `RefreshableListCard.tsx`**, finishing that section of the audit entirely. `Receipts.tsx` and `BugReportsAdmin.tsx` each hand-rolled the same outer page shell - a `Card`/`CardHeader` (icon + title + description on the left, a spinning-on-refresh `Button` on the right) wrapping a `CardContent` around a `DataTableWrapper` body - instead of sharing it, since `ListPageCard.tsx` was built for the four search-and-list pages' "search/filter above the Card" shape, not this "no search, just a refresh button" one. `RefreshableListCard` takes `icon`/`title`/`description`/`loading`/`onRefresh`, with `children` rendered inside `CardContent` (each page keeps building its own `DataTableWrapper` - only the shell around it is shared). `BugReportsAdmin.tsx`'s `BugReportDetailDialog` moved from a JSX sibling of `CardContent` (inside the old hand-rolled `Card`) to a sibling of the whole `RefreshableListCard` - functionally identical, since this app's `Dialog` primitive already portals to `document.body` regardless of where it's mounted in the tree. Pure refactor, no visual or behavioral change - verified by both pages' existing tests passing unmodified. Added `RefreshableListCard.test.tsx` (new file, 4 tests: renders icon/title/description/children, clicking Refresh calls `onRefresh`, the button disables and its icon spins while `loading`, neither happens when not loading). Test count: 1131 -> 1135 (493 backend / 642 frontend); updated everywhere asserted. `tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run build` clean.

- **Closed 3 more duplication findings from the 2026-09-07 audit: `StatTile`, `WorkflowTabsShell`, and `ConfigErrorCard`** (fixes a real, minor bug along the way).
  - **`StatTile.tsx`** (new, shared) replaces `DashboardStatsRow.tsx`/`CheckoutStatsRow.tsx`'s 8 independently hand-rolled `Card`/`CardHeader`/`CardTitle`+icon/`CardContent` KPI tiles - `CheckoutStatsRow.tsx`'s own docblock previously said it was written to match `DashboardStatsRow.tsx`'s "exact pattern" on purpose, i.e. duplicated rather than shared. `value`/`subtext` accept any `ReactNode`, not just text, since Checkout Status's "N Pending / of M Present" row and a couple of tiles' plain currency strings need more than a single string. Pure refactor, no visual change - every tile's label, icon, value, color, and subtext is unchanged.
  - **`WorkflowTabsShell.tsx`** (new, shared) replaces `AuctionItemsWorkflow.tsx`/`AuctionTicketsWorkflow.tsx`'s near byte-identical ~25-line header (title/description, the closed-auction amber badge, the "Open Spreadsheet" button) + tabs wrapper (horizontally-scrollable `TabsList` over `TabsContent` panes) - each page now just passes `title`/`description`/`spreadsheetId`/`isAuctionClosed`/`tabs`. The one real inconsistency the audit flagged between the two originals (`sm:items-end` vs `sm:items-center` on the header row) had no evidence either was deliberate rather than copy-paste drift, so it's kept as a `headerAlign` prop (defaulting to `"end"`, with `AuctionTicketsWorkflow.tsx` explicitly passing `"center"`) rather than silently picking one - this extraction changes no page's appearance, it just stops the two from being able to drift apart on everything else.
  - **`ConfigErrorCard.tsx`** (new, shared) replaces the "spreadsheet/folder not configured" placeholder previously duplicated 3 times (`AuctionItemsTab.tsx`, `Receipts.tsx`, `BugReportsAdmin.tsx`) - and **fixes a real, visible drift bug in the process**: `Receipts.tsx`/`BugReportsAdmin.tsx`'s copies both had `h-100 flex items-center justify-center` on the wrapping `CardContent`, but `AuctionItemsTab.tsx`'s copy was missing those classes, so its "Not Configured" placeholder rendered top-aligned instead of vertically centered like the other two - exactly the class of bug `ListPageCard.tsx`'s own docblock says it exists to prevent. Fixed automatically by extracting the one correct implementation instead of a third hand-copy.
  - Added `StatTile.test.tsx` (new, 4 tests), `WorkflowTabsShell.test.tsx` (new, 7 tests - including one asserting `headerAlign="center"` actually swaps the class, and one confirming the default preserves `sm:items-end`), and `ConfigErrorCard.test.tsx` (new, 1 test asserting the centering classes are present - the direct regression guard for the `AuctionItemsTab.tsx` bug this closes). Test count: 1119 -> 1131 (493 backend / 638 frontend); updated everywhere asserted. `tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run build` clean.

- **Closed the 2 remaining test-coverage gaps from the 2026-09-07 audit** (`AttendeeGuestsEditor.tsx`'s 3-guest cap, `itemBadgeColors.ts`'s zero coverage) - neither turned up a real bug, both were already correct and just untested.
  - Added `AttendeeGuestsEditor.test.tsx` (new file, 11 tests): the `MAX_GUESTS` (3) boundary - "Add Guest" present at 0-2 guests, hidden at exactly 3, clicking it at 2 guests appends slot 3 not a 4th - plus a gap-filling edge case (guest 2 present, 1 and 3 empty - "Add Guest" correctly fills the lowest empty slot, 1, not the next sequential one), own-bidder-number checkbox visibility, editing/removing a guest, and `makeGuestDraft`.
  - Added `itemBadgeColors.test.ts` (new file, 7 tests): `categoryBadgeClass()` is stable across repeated calls for the same category, pinned exact hash-to-palette mappings for several real category names (so a change to the hash or palette shows up as a deliberate diff, since `ItemRow.tsx`/`AuctionItemRow.tsx` must agree on these), different categories route to different palette entries, and `ITEM_TYPE_STYLES`' three entries have distinct classes/icons with none overlapping the Bundle badge's purple.
  - Test count: 1101 -> 1119 (493 backend / 626 frontend); updated everywhere asserted. `tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run build` clean.

- **Adopted `RowListShell.tsx`'s loading skeleton for the Auction Items Database, per explicit user request**, closing the residual gap the `RowListShell` extraction below deliberately left open. `AuctionItemsTab.tsx` previously replaced its whole page (filters, "Add New Item" button, everything) with a full-page `Loader2` spinner while items loaded - the only one of the four flexible-row-list pages that worked this way, unlike `Items.tsx`/`Bidders.tsx`/`TicketAttendeeTab.tsx`, which all keep their filter bar visible and show skeleton rows inline. Removed the full-page-spinner branch and threaded a `loading` prop into `AuctionItemsList.tsx` (already supported by `RowListShell`, unused until now) instead, matching those three pages' convention exactly - filters, the Add button, and the auction-closed badge now stay visible and interactive from the moment the page mounts.
