  - Added a `useAuctionItems` mock override to `AuctionItemsTab.test.tsx` (previously a static, non-overridable factory - converted to `vi.fn()` so a test can drive `loading: true` for one render) and a new test asserting the filter bar's search input stays present and skeleton rows (`.animate-pulse`) render instead of the page going blank. Sanity-checked by reverting the fix and confirming the new test failed with the real symptom (spinner instead of filters/skeleton), then restored. Test count: 1100 -> 1101 (493 backend / 608 frontend); updated everywhere asserted. `tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run build` clean.

- **Extracted a shared `RowListShell.tsx` for this app's 4 flexible-row lists** (`ItemsList.tsx`, `BiddersList.tsx`, `AttendeesList.tsx`, `AuctionItemsList.tsx`), per the 2026-09-07 audit's highest-call-site-impact finding - each had hand-duplicated the identical loading-skeleton (`animate-pulse` placeholder rows) and empty-state (icon + message) markup, the div-row analogue of what `DataTableWrapper.tsx` already solved for the earlier `<table>`-based lists. `RowListShell` takes `items`/`renderRow`/`emptyIcon`/`emptyMessage`, plus optional `loading`/`skeletonRowCount` and an `animated` flag (wraps rows in `AnimatePresence` - `BiddersList.tsx`/`AuctionItemsList.tsx` need this, `ItemsList.tsx`/`AttendeesList.tsx` don't, matching each one's pre-existing behavior exactly). Pure refactor, no behavior change: every list's loading skeleton count, empty-state text, and animation behavior is unchanged - verified by the full existing test suite passing unmodified. `AuctionItemsList.tsx` didn't yet pass `loading` through at the time of this extraction (its loading was still handled one level up in `AuctionItemsTab.tsx` via a full-page spinner, a real UX difference from the other three) - left open rather than folded in here, since adopting the skeleton there would be a real behavior change, not pure deduplication; closed immediately above by explicit user request.
  - Added `RowListShell.test.tsx` (new file, 6 tests: skeleton row count - explicit and default - empty state, row order, and both animated/non-animated rendering). Test count: 1094 -> 1100 (493 backend / 607 frontend); updated everywhere asserted. `tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run build` clean.

### Added

- **Unified the Add and Edit Auction Item dialogs' visual layout, per explicit user request.** `AddAuctionItemDialog.tsx` previously used its own `grid-cols-4` label-left layout and a `600px` width, visually unrelated to `EditAuctionItemDialog.tsx`'s sectioned (Details/Pricing/Donor & Type), stacked-label, `max-w-lg` layout - despite both editing the same kind of record. Rebuilt Add to match Edit's structure exactly, including a new shared `AuctionItemFormSection.tsx` component (the uppercase section-header wrapper) both dialogs now import, so the section styling can't drift apart the next time either changes.
  - Add's field set is deliberately unchanged, per the user's explicit instruction: still just Category/Name/Description/Value/Donor/Type/Allow Multiple Winners - no Item # (server-assigned on save) and no editable Opening Bid/Min Raise, since those are calculated server-side from Value (`itemService.ts`'s `calculateBidAmounts`) and only exist to show/override once the item is real, in Edit. Added a small explanatory line under Add's Value field ("Opening bid and minimum raise are calculated automatically from this value once saved.") so the still-different field set doesn't read as a bug now that the two dialogs otherwise look identical.
  - Didn't merge the two into one component outright - their state models differ too much to share cleanly (Add is a controlled `newItem`/`onNewItemChange` object with no field-level diffing; Edit holds its own internal draft and diffs against the original item to send only changed fields). Sharing just the presentational section wrapper gets the visual unification the user asked for without forcing an artificial single form to cover two genuinely different save behaviors.
  - Added `AuctionItemFormSection.test.tsx` (new file, 1 test: renders its title and children) and 2 tests to `AddAuctionItemDialog.test.tsx` (renders the same three section headers Edit uses; confirms Item #/Opening Bid/Min Raise are still absent, proving the field-set change wasn't smuggled in alongside the layout change). Test count: 1069 -> 1072 (493 backend / 579 frontend); updated everywhere asserted. `tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run build` clean.

- **Added the missing UI to set an item's "Allow Multiple Winners" flag - previously the only way to set it was editing column M directly in the Auction-Items sheet.** Flagged as a backlog item after the Bid Entry redesign added the flag itself (see below) but no way to toggle it from the app. `AddAuctionItemDialog.tsx` gained a checkbox (defaults unchecked, matching every real, non-repeatable auction lot) that's sent through `useAddAuctionItem.ts` to `/api/auction/add-item`; `EditAuctionItemDialog.tsx` gained the same checkbox via its existing `FIELD_COLUMNS`/`EditableFields` diffing pattern (`allowMultipleWinners: "M"`), so it's only sent to the server when actually changed, same as every other field on that dialog.
  - The add path needed a real backend change: `itemService.ts`'s `addItem` only ever read/wrote the `'Auction-Items'!A:L` range (12 columns), which doesn't cover column M. Rather than widening that range (and risking ragged-row edge cases for existing rows that don't have an M value yet), added a second, single-cell write to `'Auction-Items'!M{rowIndex}` right after the row is created - issued *before* the main A:L write so the main write stays "the last thing written" for anything (including this repo's test mocks) that inspects the most recent sheet write. The edit path needed no backend change at all: `EditAuctionItemDialog.tsx`'s saves already go through `useAuctionItemUpdate.ts`'s generic single-cell `handleUpdate`, which writes directly to `Auction-Items!<colLetter><rowIndex>` regardless of column - adding `"M"` to the column map was sufficient. Widened `handleUpdate`'s `value` parameter from `string` to `string | boolean` so the checkbox's real boolean flows straight through (Sheets accepts a raw boolean with `USER_ENTERED`) instead of round-tripping through `"TRUE"`/`"FALSE"` strings, which would have made every truthy non-empty string (including the literal text `"FALSE"`) read back as `true` via this app's existing `!!item.allowMultipleWinners` checks (Batch Entry's repeatable-item detection, the Repeatable badge) until the next full data refresh.
  - Added `AddAuctionItemDialog.test.tsx` (new file, 3 tests: checkbox defaults unchecked, reflects a pre-set `true`, and checking it calls `onNewItemChange` with the flag set while preserving other fields) and 5 tests to `EditAuctionItemDialog.test.tsx` (prefill checked/unchecked from the item, checking sends `{field: "allowMultipleWinners", colLetter: "M", value: true}`, unchecking sends `value: false`, and leaving it untouched excludes it from the saved changes). Added 2 tests to `items.test.ts` asserting the new column-M write's payload (`true` when requested, `false` by default) and updated its duplicate-idempotency test's `updateCallCount` assertion from 1 to 2 (a real add now issues 2 writes, not 1). Updated `itemService-concurrency.test.ts`'s fake `update` mock to exclude the new single-cell M write from its full-row `callOrder` tracking, so that lock-ordering test still asserts on just the write it cares about. Sanity-checked the write-ordering fix by reverting it (M-write after the main write) and confirming `items.test.ts`'s `lastAppendedRow` assertions broke as expected, then restored. Test count: 1059 -> 1069 (493 backend / 576 frontend); updated everywhere asserted. `tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run build` clean. Doesn't touch Apps Script (this whole path is Node-side).

- **UI / Layout Tweaks: redesigned Bid Management's "Winning Bid Entry" workflow, pitched as an Artifact mockup first and implemented after explicit approval.** The user's ask (paraphrased): entering winning bids is the most stressful part of running the auction, since bids arrive in 3-4 waves - the first two small, the third by far the largest (30-60 items at once), the last (live auction items) landing while everyone's also trying to check out. Design question: is there a better workflow than the single always-on-screen form, and does it belong merged into the Items tab or stay separate? Recommendation given and approved: keep it a separate tab (Items is a browse/reference catalog - a different mental mode from heads-down repeated transcription), but replace the single form with two modes matching the two wave sizes.
  - **Quick Entry** (`QuickBidEntry.tsx`/`useQuickBidEntryState.ts`) - for the smaller waves. Same three fields (Item Number/Bidder ID/Winning Bid) and the same validation as the old form (unresolvable item/bidder, non-checked-in non-proxy bidder, blank price), but now keyboard-driven: Enter in Item Number moves to Bidder ID, Enter in Bidder ID moves to Price, Enter in Price submits and refocuses Item Number automatically - a whole entry is one continuous keystroke sequence, no mouse needed. Also fixed a real, related gap in the old form: it only `autoFocus`ed once on initial mount, so after every single submission staff had to click back into Item Number by hand before continuing.
  - **A live "This Session" feed** next to the form (not present at all in the old design) - every attempted entry, successful or blocked, appears immediately with the item, bidder, price, and a relative timestamp, so a mis-typed price or item number is caught by glancing at the last few entries instead of only surfacing later at Checkout. Deliberately does not include an "Undo" affordance despite one appearing in the approved mockup - reversing an already-recorded winning bid would need a new backend capability (deleting a specific bid-grid entry) that doesn't exist anywhere in this app today, and shipping a button that only removed the feed's local display without actually reversing the sheet write would be actively misleading in a live auction. Flagged as a natural follow-up, not built here.
  - **Batch Entry** (`BatchBidEntry.tsx`/`useBatchBidEntryState.ts`) - for the big wave. A spreadsheet-style grid (auto-appending a fresh trailing row once the last row gets any input) where every row resolves live as you type - green "ready" once item, bidder, and price all resolve; red "unresolved" for an item/bidder number that doesn't exist; amber "duplicate" for a non-repeatable item that already has a winner *or* that appears twice within the batch itself (the second check catches two paper slips for the same lot landing in the same stack, independent of whether the sheet already has a winner on record). Nothing is recorded until "Record N Ready Bids" is clicked, and only the rows with no problem are ever submitted - a duplicate or unresolved row stays in the grid to fix, never silently dropped or partially submitted.
  - **A real, user-reported data-integrity gap this surfaced**: entering the same item number twice for a normal auction lot silently created two winning-bid records for one item, with no warning anywhere - a real risk during a stressful high-volume wave. Both modes now check the item's winner status (via the already-loaded `winners` map) before recording, and block/flag the duplicate instead.
  - **"Allow Multiple Winners" as a real per-item flag, not category-name matching**: some items (raffle tickets, Open Appeal pledge tiers, "A Night with the ORNS Staff," Auction Tickets - see the entry below) are legitimately meant to have many winners, since they track pledges/ticket sales rather than being real auction lots. Added `allowMultipleWinners?: boolean` to the `AuctionItem` type (optional, matching this session's established precedent for a field added after most fixtures already existed - see `Bidder.paymentMethod?`) and parsed it from a new "Allow Multiple Winners" column (the user added it as column M on the real Auction-Items sheet) in `itemParsing.ts`'s header-based column detection. When set, both modes skip the duplicate check entirely and instead show a "Repeatable" badge plus a session-scoped "N sold this session" tally (deliberately session-only, not all-time - there's no existing all-time "how many times has this item been won" aggregate anywhere in this app, and `winners` is a single itemId->name map, not a count; building a true aggregate wasn't needed for this feature).
  - Both modes call the exact same `recordWinningBid` (`useWinnersData.ts`) the old form always used - no backend changes needed for either mode, and Batch Entry's "submit all ready rows" is just that same call fired once per row (not a new bulk endpoint), matching `recordWinningBid`'s existing fire-and-forget design (optimistic update, background sync, its own per-row success/rollback toast).
  - A real bug caught and fixed by this work's own tests before it could ship: Quick Entry's Item Number field was disabled while `submitting`, and the auto-refocus-after-submit call ran before React had committed `submitting: false` to the DOM - so `.focus()` silently no-op'd on a still-disabled input. Fixed by never disabling the Item Number field specifically (it's the auto-refocus target, and submits are already near-instant given the fire-and-forget architecture, so there's no real double-submit risk from leaving it enabled).
  - Added `src/hooks/useQuickBidEntryState.test.ts` (10 tests: all the preserved old-form validation paths, the happy path clearing the form and appending a feed entry, the rollback-error path leaving the form intact, the duplicate-block path, the repeatable-item pass-through, and the session-scoped sold-count tally) and `src/hooks/useBatchBidEntryState.test.ts` (12 tests: row status resolution for every case including the two distinct duplicate triggers, row add/remove, and `submitReady` recording only the ready rows while leaving duplicate/error rows in the grid). Rewrote `BidEntry.test.tsx` (13 tests, preserving every original assertion plus new coverage for Enter-to-advance, submit-then-refocus, the duplicate-block feed entry, the Repeatable badge, and mode switching) and added `BatchBidEntry.test.tsx` (5 tests). Extended `itemParsing.test.ts` (+4 tests: the new column parses a literal boolean `true` - Sheets' checkbox-cell read shape - and the string `"TRUE"`, both this app's existing boolean-column conventions; defaults to `false` when absent). Sanity-checked the trickiest new logic (within-batch duplicate detection) and the disabled-focus bug's fix by reverting each and confirming its own test failed with the real symptom, then restored both - the disabled-focus bug was caught *by* writing the submit-then-refocus test in the first place, not found separately. This change is not client-only after all: `server/services/itemDataService.ts` (used by every `/api/production/item-entry/*` document-generation route) shares `src/lib/itemParsing.ts`'s `parseItemRows` with the client, so the new `allowMultipleWinners` field reaches its Apps Script call parameters too - `production.test.ts`'s hardcoded `EXPECTED_DEFAULT_ITEMS` fixture needed `allowMultipleWinners: false` added to both entries to match. (A `git stash`-based check for this initially and wrongly concluded the resulting 8 failures were pre-existing and unrelated - invalidated by this environment auto-committing at the end of each turn, so the stash never actually removed the change being tested for; corrected once the user asked to double-check.) Test count: 1021 → 1059 (491 backend / 568 frontend); updated everywhere asserted. `tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run build` clean; full suite (server + frontend) clean.

- **UI / Layout Tweaks: redesigned Bid Management's "Checkout" tab, pitched as an Artifact mockup first and implemented after explicit approval.** The user's ask: make the Checkout/Payment screen more user-friendly and visually appealing while preserving every existing action (batch print/email, per-winner email/print/checkout, the Pending/Checked-Out tab split, and the Cash/Check/Card payment picker).
  - **New KPI row** (`CheckoutStatsRow.tsx`, matching `DashboardStatsRow.tsx`'s exact `Card`/`CardHeader`/`CardTitle`+icon/`CardContent` pattern): Total Winners, Awaiting Checkout, Checked Out, and Total Collected (of total won) - all four numbers were already derivable from data the page loads, but only "Total Winners" was surfaced before, as a small badge inside the old action bar.
  - **Merged the two previously-stacked bars into one `FilterToolbar.tsx`** (search + a new Status quick-filter select + trailing Refresh/Print All Receipts/Email All Winners), matching the same "unify search and actions above the Card" direction already applied to Auction Items this session, instead of a slate action box directly above a separate search-only row.
  - **New Status quick-filter** (All Statuses / Awaiting Checkout / Pending Verification / Paid), since the existing "Already Checked Out" tab silently bundled both Paid and Pending-Verification winners with no way to isolate just the ones still awaiting PayPal confirmation. Implemented via a new `winnerStatus()` helper in `useWinnersList.ts` that classifies each winner as `awaiting`/`review`/`paid`.
  - **Removed the Pending Checkout / Already Checked Out tabs entirely**, per explicit user feedback questioning whether they duplicated the Status filter above: "awaiting" is exactly what the old Pending tab showed, and "review"+"paid" together are exactly what the old Completed tab showed - the filter's 4-way split (which can already isolate Paid from Pending Verification, something the tabs never could) made the tabs' coarser 2-way split pure redundant effort, not a second, independent axis. `useWinnersList.ts` no longer tracks `activeTab`/computes a `pendingWinners`/`completedWinners` split at all - just one `filteredWinners` list (search + status filtered) rendered directly into one `WinnerGrid`. `useCheckoutState.ts`'s `pendingFilteredWinners`/`completedFilteredWinners`/`activeTab`/`setActiveTab` were removed in favor of the single `filteredWinners`.
  - **`WinnerCard.tsx` redesign**: a color-coded left border + status chip (slate/amber/emerald for awaiting/pending-verification/paid) so the grid scans at a glance instead of requiring each card's footer text to be read individually; the item list capped to a fixed height with internal scroll instead of growing the card indefinitely (previously a winner with many items stretched taller than its row-mates, making the grid look ragged); Email/Print as a two-up compact row instead of two full-width stacked buttons; and the disabled Email button now reads "No Email" directly instead of relying on a hover-only `title` attribute (easy to miss, and inaccessible on touch). A paid winner whose payment method is on file now also shows "via {method}" under the Paid state. A bidder-initials avatar was in the first pass but removed per explicit user feedback after comparing against the approved mockup screenshot.
    - This last part surfaced a small, real, pre-existing gap: `server/services/auctionService.ts`'s `checkoutMarkPaid` has always written a payment-method label to the Bidder-Numbers sheet's column I on every checkout (`"Cash"`/`"Check"`/`"Credit Card / PayPal"`/`"PayPal Verification Pending"`), but `src/lib/bidderParsing.ts`'s `parseBidderRows` never read that column back into the client `Bidder` object - the data existed, it just wasn't surfaced anywhere in the UI. Added `paymentMethod?: string` to the `Bidder` interface (`src/types.ts`), parsed it from `row[8]` in `bidderParsing.ts`, and threaded it through `useWinnersList.ts`'s `WinnerRecord.paymentMethod` into `WinnerCard.tsx`.
  - **`PaymentMethodDialog.tsx` redesign**: each method (Cash/Check/Credit Card-PayPal) now gets its own icon plus an explicit checkmark-in-a-circle for the selected state, instead of a plain `variant`/background-color swap on an otherwise-identical button - same three methods, same total display, same Cancel/Confirm actions and exact `paymentMethod` strings sent to the server as before.
  - **`FilterToolbar.tsx` now wraps its content in the app's real `Card` component** (with `p-4`), not a bare flex `<div>` - the first Checkout pass had the search/filter/action row sitting directly on the page background, unlike the white card treatment shown in the approved mockup. Per explicit follow-up feedback ("the white background should apply to every instance of FilterToolbar.tsx"), this was made at the shared-component level rather than as a one-off wrapper around Checkout's own call site, so `Items.tsx`, `Bidders.tsx`, `TicketAttendeeTab.tsx`, and `AuctionItemsTab.tsx` all picked up the same white-card treatment in one change - all four pages' existing tests passed unmodified against it.
  - Added `src/lib/bidderParsing.test.ts` (4 tests - `parseBidderRows` had **zero** test coverage anywhere in the repo before this, direct or indirect: full-row parse including the new `paymentMethod` field, blank-column default, id-less row filtering, lowercase `"true"` boolean parsing), `src/components/CheckoutStatsRow.test.tsx` (2 tests: all four stats render with correct currency formatting; renders cleanly with all-zero inputs), and extended `WinnerCard.test.tsx` (+6 tests: each status chip text, the "via {method}" line appearing only when a method is on file, the "No Email" label, no initials avatar), `FilterToolbar.test.tsx` (+1 test: root renders as the shared `Card`, `data-slot="card"`), and `Checkout.test.tsx` (+1 test: every winner regardless of checkout status renders in one list with no "Pending Checkout"/"Already Checked Out" tab text anywhere). `useCheckoutState.test.ts`'s old tab-scoped "pendingFilteredWinners / completedFilteredWinners" describe block (2 tests) was replaced with a single "filteredWinners" describe block (1 test) once there was only one list to test, and its "statusFilter" describe block's 3 tests were rewritten to assert against that same single list instead of two tab-scoped ones. Fixed 3 pre-existing `Checkout.test.tsx` assertions that broke against the redesign for real reasons: two tests asserted the exact literal button text `"Print All Receipts"`/`"Print Receipt"` which an earlier draft of this redesign had shortened to `"Print All"`/`"Print"` - reverted those two labels back to their original full text (the shortening wasn't part of the approved design, just an artifact-mockup shorthand) rather than loosen the tests; the third now correctly expects **two** "Paid" matches (`getAllByText("Paid").length === 2`) instead of one, since the new status chip and the existing footer state both legitimately render the word "Paid" on a paid winner's card. Sanity-checked the `statusFilter`/`stats` logic, the `FilterToolbar` Card wrap, the avatar removal, and the tabs removal independently by breaking/reintroducing each and confirming its own test failed with the real symptom, then restored each. Test count: 997 → 1014 (484 backend / 530 frontend); updated everywhere asserted. `tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run build` clean. Marked done in `refactor-opportunities.md`'s "UI / Layout Tweaks" item.

- **Extracted `src/components/ui/ListPageCard.tsx`, a shared `Card`/`CardHeader`/`CardContent` shell for this app's four search-and-list workflow pages (`Items.tsx`, `Bidders.tsx`, `TicketAttendeeTab.tsx`, `AuctionItemsTab.tsx`), consolidating a skeleton each page had independently hand-rolled: `<Card><CardHeader>{icon+title+description}</CardHeader><CardContent className="p-0">{list}</CardContent></Card>`.** That duplication was the direct root cause of the whitespace double-gap bugs fixed earlier this session - `Card`'s own `flex flex-col gap-4` already supplies the header-to-content gap, but with four independent copies of the header, nothing stopped an individual page from adding a redundant `className` override (`pb-3`, `py-6`) on top of it. `ListPageCard` takes `icon` (optional), `title`, `description`, `className` (merged onto the root `Card`, for the few pages needing extra layout like `TicketAttendeeTab.tsx`'s `flex flex-col min-h-0`), `contentClassName` (merged onto `CardContent` alongside its always-present `p-0`), and `children` (the list content) - there's no `className` slot on `CardHeader` at all, so the double-gap bug class can't recur through this component by construction. Deliberately does not include the search/filter bar itself (`FilterToolbar.tsx`, already shared and composed as this component's sibling, not its child).
  - Migrated all four pages: `Items.tsx` and `AuctionItemsTab.tsx` needed no extra props beyond `icon`/`title`/`description`; `Bidders.tsx` dropped its residual `CardHeader className="pb-3"` in the process (found during this migration - the same double-gap pattern as the `TicketAttendeeTab.tsx`/`AuctionItemsFilterBar.tsx` bugs fixed earlier this session, just less severe and not yet reported since `pb-3` is a smaller redundant addition than the `py-6`/`pb-6` overrides those bugs involved); `TicketAttendeeTab.tsx` used `className="flex flex-col min-h-0"` and `contentClassName="flex-1 overflow-auto min-h-100"` to preserve its scrolling-list layout exactly.
  - Added `src/components/ui/ListPageCard.test.tsx` (4 tests: `CardHeader` never carries `pb-3`/`py-6`; `CardContent` always carries `p-0` merged with any `contentClassName`; the optional `icon` renders inline with the title and is omitted entirely when not given; an extra `className` merges onto the root `Card`). Sanity-checked all three non-trivial behaviors (bare header, `p-0` merge, className merge) by breaking each in the component and confirming its own test failed with the real symptom, then restored. Existing `Items.test.tsx`/`Bidders.test.tsx`/`TicketAttendeeTab.test.tsx`/`AuctionItemsTab.test.tsx` all passed unmodified against the migration (including `Bidders.test.tsx`'s pre-existing "does not use the old oversized header padding" regression test, which now also implicitly covers the `pb-3` removal). Updated `component-standards.md`'s `src/components/ui/` file list (also added the previously-missing `StatusStatePlaceholder.tsx` entry, found while updating this list) and its card-primitives paragraph to describe the new shared shell. Test count: 993 → 997 (484 backend / 513 frontend); updated everywhere asserted. `tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run build` clean.

- **UI / Layout Tweaks: redesigned the Auction Items Management page's "Auction Items Database" - the editable counterpart to the read-only Bid Management "Items" tab - to match the same Attendee-List/Bidders/Items row-based visual language, per the user's explicit ask to mimic that layout here too while preserving every existing capability (edit, delete, batch category/type set, and bundle).** This was the most functionally complex of the four row-list redesigns this session - unlike the read-only Items tab, this page supports live editing, row selection for batch operations, and bundle creation/restoration, all of which had to keep working exactly as before.
  - **Single-item edit**: replaced the old `<table>` row's 9 separate per-field `EditableCell` inline editors (id/name/description/category/value/openingBid/minRaise/donor/itemType, each its own pencil-icon-to-edit affordance) with a single **`EditAuctionItemDialog.tsx`**, mirroring `EditAttendeeDialog.tsx`'s pattern on the Attendee List exactly (per the user's specific suggestion) - one form, one "Save Changes". On save, the dialog diffs the draft against the original item and reports only the changed fields (with their sheet column letters) back to the caller; `AuctionItemsTab.tsx`'s new `handleEditSave` then calls the existing, completely unchanged `useAuctionItemUpdate.ts`'s `handleUpdate` once per changed field - zero backend/hook logic changes, just one consolidated UI instead of nine. A real ordering risk this surfaced: since one Save can now change multiple fields at once (previously each committed via its own separate inline edit), a category change (which triggers a server-side resequencing + full resync) is now deliberately applied *after* every other changed field's write has been dispatched, so the resync can't race a same-Save field's still-in-flight optimistic update. `EditableCell.tsx` (the shared inline-edit-in-place widget) is now fully unused anywhere in the app - its other consumer, `AttendeeRow.tsx`, was already migrated off it in an earlier redesign this session - so it and its test file were deleted (see `component-standards.md`'s component list, updated to match).
  - **Row layout**: new `AuctionItemsList.tsx` (replacing `AuctionItemsTable.tsx`) + rewritten `AuctionItemRow.tsx` follow `ItemsList.tsx`/`ItemRow.tsx`'s exact pattern - an ID chip, name with colored category/itemType/Bundle badges inline, description, and Value/Opening/Raise/Donor on one compact line - plus what the read-only version doesn't need: a checkbox (row selection, always visible since it's a primary/frequent action here), and hover/focus-reveal Edit/Delete `IconButton`s (matching `AttendeeRow.tsx`'s convention). The category/itemType badge coloring itself (hash-per-category, fixed-map-per-type) was extracted out of `ItemRow.tsx` into a new shared `src/lib/itemBadgeColors.ts`, so both catalogs always render the same category in the same color instead of maintaining two copies that could drift. The "Restore Items" bundle-restore button (previously always visible whenever an item was bundled) is now placed inline in the row's content area rather than a table cell, unchanged otherwise. The pre-existing `motion.tr` enter/exit animation on this table (unlike the read-only Items tab, which never animated) was preserved as `motion.div`.
  - **Sort**: the old clickable-column-header sort (`AuctionItemsTable.tsx`'s `SortIndicator`, six sortable columns) had nothing left to attach to once the table became a flexible row list - replaced with a 12-option "Sort by" `Select` in `AuctionItemsFilterBar.tsx` (each of ID/Name/Category/Value/Donor/Type, ascending or descending), backed by `useAuctionItemsFilterSort.ts`'s `sortConfig` state directly (its old toggle-in-place `requestSort` function was removed in favor of exporting `setSortConfig`, the same API shape change already made for `useBiddersState.ts`'s equivalent redesign).
  - **Row selection / batch operations / bundling**: entirely unchanged functionally - `useAuctionItemsFilterSort.ts`'s `selectedRows`/`toggleRow`/`toggleAll`/`clearSelection`, `AuctionItemsBulkActionBar.tsx`'s floating bar (Bundle/Set Category/Set Type/Clear), `BundlingDialog.tsx`, and `useBundling.ts` were not touched at all. The one layout change: the select-all checkbox (previously a `<table>` header cell) now lives in a small header bar at the top of `AuctionItemsList.tsx`, rendered first so it stays the first checkbox in the DOM - existing tests indexing into `getAllByRole("checkbox")` (expecting select-all at index 0, row checkboxes after) needed no changes as a result.
  - Rewrote `AuctionItemRow.test.tsx` for the new markup (no more `<table>`/`<tbody>` wrapper; the keyboard-focus-reveal regression guard now checks the shared actions-wrapper `<div>`'s className instead of each button's own, matching `AttendeeRow.tsx`/`BidderRow.tsx`'s established wrapper-level convention) and `AuctionItemsFilterBar.test.tsx`/`AuctionItemsTab.test.tsx` for the new required `sortConfig`/`onSortConfigChange` props and the "Sort by" select replacing the `columnheader`/`row`-role-based sort test. Added `EditAuctionItemDialog.test.tsx` (7 tests: prefill, re-prefill on item switch, only-changed-fields-with-correct-column-letters on save, multi-field save, no-op save when nothing changed closes without calling `onSave`, category save, Cancel) and a new `AuctionItemsTab.editing.test.tsx` (4 tests) proving the *real* end-to-end wiring - unlike `AuctionItemsTab.test.tsx`, which leaves `@/services/google-api` unmocked since it never needed the network, this file mocks it (matching `AuctionItemsTab.bundling.test.tsx`'s convention for `useBundling`) to prove Edit-then-Save actually calls `updateSheetData`/the resequencing endpoint correctly - including a dedicated regression test for the category-last save ordering, which failed with the real symptom (resequencing dispatched *before* the other field's write) when first written without that ordering, confirming the risk was real and not just theoretical. Sanity-checked every non-trivial guard (the no-op-save early return, the category-last ordering) independently by reverting each and confirming its own test failed with the real symptom, then restored each. Test count: 976 → 989 (484 backend / 505 frontend, net of the 2 deleted `EditableCell.test.tsx` tests); updated everywhere asserted. `tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run build` clean. Marked done in `refactor-opportunities.md`'s "UI / Layout Tweaks" item.

- **Refined the Items tab redesign per user feedback on the first pass: `itemType` moved from plain text into a colored Badge inline with the category/Bundle badges, category badges got hashed-per-name colors (previously all identical plain outline badges), and `donor` moved from its own separate line to sit inline with Value/Opening/Raise (keeping its icon, just relocated).** `ItemRow.tsx`'s `categoryBadgeClass()` hashes the category string against a 10-color palette so the same category always gets the same color across every row/render, since categories are free-text (not a fixed enum) - no lookup table to maintain as new categories get added in the spreadsheet. `itemType` is a fixed 3-value enum (`Physical Item`/`Certificate`/`Both`, matching `AuctionItemRow.tsx`'s `<select>` options on the separate editable Auction Items workflow), so it gets an explicit color+icon map instead (sky/Box, amber/Gift, teal/Layers respectively) - deliberately none of these overlap with the Bundle badge's purple. Added 5 tests to `ItemRow.test.tsx`: each itemType renders its own distinct color/icon (and a same-render-cycle aliasing bug in the first draft of this test - `rerender()` reuses the same DOM node, so holding onto live element references across rerenders instead of snapshotting `.className` into a string immediately falsely equated all three itemTypes - was caught and fixed before it could hide a real regression); no itemType badge renders when itemType is empty; the same category gets the same color across independent renders while different categories get different colors; and donor renders inside the same flex container as Value/Opening/Raise, not on its own line. Sanity-checked all three behaviors independently by reverting each and confirming its own test failed with the real symptom, then restored each. Test count: 971 → 976 (484 backend / 492 frontend); updated everywhere asserted. `tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run build` clean.

- **UI / Layout Tweaks: redesigned Bid Management's "Items" tab (the read-only "Auction Catalog") to match the same Attendee-List/Bidders row-based visual language, per the user's follow-up ask to continue that treatment onto this page.** The old table (`Items.tsx` on a bare `DataTableWrapper`) only ever showed 4 columns - Item #, Item Name, Type (category), Winning Bidder - even though every `AuctionItem` already carries a description, value/opening bid/min raise, donor, item type, and bundle status; all of that was invisible in this view. Mockup shared and approved before implementation (published as an Artifact for review).
  - New `src/components/ItemsList.tsx` (list wrapper) + `src/components/ItemRow.tsx` (the row itself) replace `DataTableWrapper` for this page - `DataTableWrapper.tsx` itself is untouched and not deleted, since `Receipts.tsx`/`BugReportsAdmin.tsx`/`WinnerGrid.tsx` still use it. `ItemRow.tsx` shows an ID chip (mirroring `BidderRow.tsx`'s), the item name with a category `Badge` and, when `item.bundleId && item.status === "Active"` (the exact same convention as the editable Auction Items workflow's `AuctionItemRow.tsx`), a purple "Bundle" badge; the description (when present); a compact Value/Opening/Raise/item-type line (when any financial field is present); the donor (when present); and a Winning Bidder status on the right - a trophy badge with the winner's name, or a muted "Not yet won" placeholder.
  - Deliberately **no** `motion`/`AnimatePresence` on this list/row, unlike `BiddersList.tsx`/`BidderRow.tsx` - those preserved a pre-existing `motion.tr` the old `BiddersTable.tsx` already had; this table's old `<table>` row never animated at all, so adding it here would have been new, unrequested behavior (and, caught directly: doing so broke `Items.test.tsx`'s synchronous search-filter assertions, since `AnimatePresence`/`mode="popLayout"` keeps an exiting row in the DOM until its real, unmocked exit animation finishes - `Items.test.tsx`, unlike `Bidders.test.tsx`, never mocked `motion/react`). Matches `AttendeeRow.tsx`/`AttendeesList.tsx`'s plain-div convention instead.
  - Every existing behavior is unchanged: search (by item # substring or name substring, case-insensitive), Active-status-only filtering, the numeric-ascending sort by item id (fixed in an earlier entry below), the "Total Items" count, and both empty-state messages. Confirmed `Items.test.tsx`'s existing 7 tests needed **zero changes** to keep passing against the new DOM (unlike the Bidders redesign, which needed `.closest("tr")` replaced with a `data-testid` - this suite only ever queried by visible text content, never by row/table structure).
  - Added a `data-testid={`item-row-${item.id}`}` per row (the same convention `BidderRow.tsx` established) and 3 new tests to `Items.test.tsx` covering the newly-surfaced fields (the Bundle badge appearing/not appearing per item; donor/value/opening/raise fields rendering when present and omitting cleanly when absent; the winner trophy badge vs. the "Not yet won" placeholder) plus a new `src/components/ItemRow.test.tsx` (3 tests) unit-testing `ItemRow`'s Bundle-badge guard directly - `Items.tsx`'s own Active-only filter means an inactive-but-bundled item can never actually reach a rendered row through the integration tests, so the `status === "Active"` half of that guard needed its own direct test to be exercised at all. Sanity-checked all three guards (the Bundle badge's Active check, the financial-fields line, and the winner placeholder text) independently by reverting each and confirming its own test failed with the real symptom, then restored each. Test count: 965 → 971 (484 backend / 487 frontend); updated everywhere asserted. `tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run build` clean.

- **UI / Layout Tweaks: redesigned Bid Management's "Bidders" tab (Bidder Check-In) to match the Attendee List's visual language, per the user's explicit ask ("use the newly created Attendee List table on the Auction Tickets page as inspiration").** The old `BiddersTable.tsx`/`BidderRow.tsx` was a `<table>` with two stacked status badges (Ticket Payment, Auction Status) crammed into one narrow "Status" column - cramped and visually inconsistent with the Attendee List's newer flexible-row design. All the same information and every existing action are preserved, just re-laid-out:
  - New `BiddersList.tsx` replaces `BiddersTable.tsx`, mirroring `AttendeesList.tsx`'s plain-div-list pattern (loading skeleton rows, an empty state distinguishing "nothing loaded yet" from "filtered down to nothing" - unchanged from before) instead of `DataTableWrapper`'s `<table>` markup.
  - `BidderRow.tsx` rewritten as one flexible row per bidder (an ID chip, name, source badge, email, both status badges inline, and the Check In/Proxy/Read-Only actions) instead of fixed `<td>` columns - matching `AttendeeRow.tsx`'s row shape. Email is no longer hidden below the `lg` breakpoint (it was previously `hidden lg:table-cell`) since a flexible row has room for it always.
  - The old clickable-column-header sort (`BiddersTable.tsx`'s `SortableHead`, toggled via `useBiddersState.ts`'s `handleSort`) has nothing to attach to once there's no table header - replaced with an explicit "Sort by" `Select` in `BiddersFilterControls.tsx` (Bidder # / Name / Status, each ascending or descending), backed by the hook's `sortConfig` state directly (`handleSort`'s toggle-in-place API removed in favor of exposing `setSortConfig`, since a dropdown sets an explicit target state rather than toggling).
  - `BiddersTable.tsx` deleted (fully superseded, nothing else imported it).
  - Every existing behavior deliberately unchanged: search, the three status/payment/source filters, check-in/proxy mutual exclusion, the pending-payment confirmation dialog (with its transactional check-in-with-bid path), the walk-in ticket dialog, and the closed-auction read-only state.
  - Updated `Bidders.test.tsx`'s 16 per-row assertions that relied on `.closest("tr")` (which returns `null` now that rows are `<div>`s, not `<tr>`s) to a new `data-testid={`bidder-row-${id}`}` on each row instead - explicit and no longer coupled to table semantics. Added a new functional test proving the "Sort by" select actually re-sorts the visible rows (not just displays a label), and extended `BiddersFilterControls.test.tsx`'s existing SelectValue-label regression suite to cover the new sort select. Sanity-checked by reverting the sort select's `onValueChange` wiring and confirming the new test failed with the real symptom (row order never changed), then restored. Test count: 959 → 961 (484 backend / 477 frontend); updated everywhere asserted. `tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run build` clean. Marked done in `refactor-opportunities.md`'s "UI / Layout Tweaks" item - the rest of the app's pages haven't been passed through this treatment yet.

- **PayPal Sweeper status card on the Configuration page - the user asked, after closing out Code Hardening item #1, whether there was a way to confirm from inside the app that item #2's two manual-only steps had actually happened (an admin signing in once to store a background-sync credential, and the Node job running reliably), rather than only being inferable from server logs.** Two backend pieces, both reachable only via the existing session-cookie-based auth (`requireAuth`, matching every other route in `dataRoutes.ts`):
  - `server/services/paypalSweeperJob.ts`'s new `getSweeperStatus()` reads the stored credential doc (`system/paypalSweeperAuth` - already written by `persistSweeperCredential` on login) for who/when it was last set, and a new in-process `lastRun` record (deliberately in-memory, not Firestore - this is a live "is the current server process healthy" signal, not a durable one; it resets on restart by design) for the last sweep's outcome and a best-effort `nextRunAt` estimate. `runPaypalSweep`'s body moved to a private `runPaypalSweepInner`, with the public `runPaypalSweep` now a thin wrapper that records every outcome into `lastRun` regardless of which return path fired (skipped, or the pending/resolved/escalated tally) - `startPaypalSweeperSchedule` also now records the configured interval and its own start time for the `nextRunAt` estimate.
  - Two new routes in `dataRoutes.ts`: `GET /api/paypal-sweeper/status` (returns the status object) and `POST /api/paypal-sweeper/run-now` (triggers an immediate sweep - lets an admin verify the job works right now instead of waiting up to the full 5-minute interval).
  - Client side: `src/hooks/usePaypalSweeperStatus.ts` (fetches on mount, polls every 30s independent of the sweeper's own interval, and drives the manual "Run Sweep Now" action with toast feedback) backing a new `src/components/PayPalSweeperStatusCard.tsx`, added to `ConfigSettings.tsx` right after `PayPalIntegrationCard` - shows the credential status (who/when signed in, or a prompt to sign in), the last sweep's timestamp and outcome, the next expected run, and the manual trigger button. New `SweeperStatus`/`PaypalSweepOutcome` types added to `src/types.ts`, and `getPaypalSweeperStatus`/`runPaypalSweepNow` added to `src/services/google-api.ts`.
  - Added 19 tests: 4 in `server/test/paypalSweeperJob.test.ts` (`getSweeperStatus`'s credential-configured/not-configured reporting, recording a completed run's outcome with a correctly-computed `nextRunAt`, and `scheduleRunning` reflecting `startPaypalSweeperSchedule`), 5 route-level tests in `server/test/items.test.ts` (both routes' happy paths, both requiring auth, and an error forwarded to the error handler - `paypalSweeperJob.ts` mocked at the module boundary, matching `auth.test.ts`'s existing convention, so these stay hermetic), 6 in `src/hooks/usePaypalSweeperStatus.test.ts`, and 4 in `src/components/PayPalSweeperStatusCard.test.tsx`. Sanity-checked the three load-bearing pieces independently by reverting each and confirming its own test(s) failed with the real symptom (the outcome never landing in `lastRun`; the card's "healthy" badge never appearing; the routes 404ing), then restored each. Verified live against a running dev server (not just tests): both routes correctly return `401 Unauthorized` with no session, confirming `requireAuth` guards them like every other mutating/status route in this app. Test count: 936 → 955 (484 backend / 471 frontend); updated everywhere asserted. `tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run build` clean.

### Fixed

- **Bug reported via the in-app tool: the Bid Management "Items" tab's Auction Catalog table wasn't ordered numerically by item number.** Root cause: `Items.tsx`'s `filteredItems` only filtered `items` (by status/search term) and rendered them in whatever row order the spreadsheet currently had - no sort at all. Item numbers can drift from row order over time since resequencing (`itemService.ts`'s `addItem`/`updateItem`/etc.) only ever rewrites an item's ID *value*, never reorders the underlying rows, so an edited/bundled/deleted-and-re-added catalog can easily end up with row order unrelated to numeric order - exactly what the user's screenshot showed (102, 201, 101, 401, 801, 701, 802). Fixed by sorting `filteredItems` numerically ascending by item number after filtering, matching the numeric-sort convention already used for the same field elsewhere in this app (`itemDataService.ts`'s `getValidSortedItems`, `useDashboardState.ts`): `(parseInt(a.id, 10) || 0) - (parseInt(b.id, 10) || 0)`, so a non-numeric id sorts as `0` rather than producing `NaN` comparisons. Scoped to just this one table - the top-level Auction Items workflow's own catalog view was not reported as affected and wasn't touched.
  - Added a regression test to `src/components/Items.test.tsx`: mock items deliberately supplied out of numeric order (102, 201, 101, 401 - reproducing the exact reported scenario) must render in the DOM in ascending numeric order (101, 102, 201, 401). Sanity-checked by removing the `.sort()` call and confirming the new test failed with the real symptom (the original out-of-order sequence), then restored. Test count: 958 → 959 (484 backend / 475 frontend); updated everywhere asserted. `tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run build` clean.

- **Two bugs reported via the in-app tool in `BidEntry.tsx`'s Winning Bid Entry card, fixed together since they touch the same code.**
  - **Bug: the item/bidder name label showed up under the Item Number/Bidder ID fields even when they were empty.** Root cause: `selectedItem`/`selectedBidder` were computed as `items.find(i => i.id === itemId)` with no guard for an empty `itemId`/`bidderId` - if any row in `items`/`bidders` itself has an empty-string `id` (a real data-quality case, e.g. an inactive/bundled item row whose id was blanked out but is still present in the array), `"" === ""` matches it as soon as the field is empty, showing that row's name/label under a blank input - exactly what the user's screenshot showed ("New Art Bundle" appearing under an empty Item Number field). Fixed by only running the `.find()` when the field has actual (trimmed) content: `itemId.trim() ? items.find(...) : undefined`, same for `bidderId`.
  - **UI issue: "the labels are way too close to the inputs."** Each field's wrapping `<div>` had no vertical spacing at all between its `<Label>` and `<Input>` - `Label` carries no built-in margin, so the two rendered flush against each other. Fixed by adding `space-y-2` to all three field wrappers (Item Number, Bidder ID, Winning Bid), matching the spacing convention already used elsewhere in this app's forms (e.g. `BugReportDialog.tsx`).
  - Added 2 tests to `src/components/BidEntry.test.tsx`: one asserting neither label renders when both fields are empty (using mock item/bidder rows with an empty-string `id`, reproducing the exact reported scenario), and one asserting each field wrapper carries `space-y-2`. Sanity-checked both independently by reverting each fix and confirming its own test failed with the real symptom (the stale "New Art Bundle" label rendering with an empty field for the first; the wrapper's className missing `space-y-2` for the second), then restored both. Test count: 956 → 958 (484 backend / 474 frontend); updated everywhere asserted. `tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run build` clean.

- **Bug reported via the in-app tool: the Resolve button (and the rest of the Actions column) fell off the visible area of `BugReportsAdmin.tsx`'s table when a report's Title text was long, and the user asked for Title to wrap with a fixed width instead, plus max widths on every column.** Root cause: the shared `Table` component's default `<table>` uses browser auto layout, and only the Title cell had any width constraint at all (a `truncate max-w-75` span) - the Route column had none, so a long, unbroken Route/Title string simply grew that column and pushed the whole table (Actions included) wider than its container; the wrapping `overflow-x-auto` div then required horizontal scrolling to reach the Resolve/Screenshot/View buttons, exactly as the user's screenshot showed. Fixed by switching this table to a fixed layout (`tableClassName="table-fixed"`, using `DataTableWrapper`'s existing prop) with an explicit width on every column (Type/Title/Route/Reported/Actions), so no single cell's content can ever expand past its assigned share and push a later column out of view - the Actions column in particular now has a guaranteed-fixed width, wide enough for all three buttons, that can never shrink. Per the user's explicit ask, the Title and Route cells now wrap (`whitespace-normal break-words`) instead of truncating with an ellipsis - the old `truncate`/`max-w-75` span is gone; the Title cell's `title` attribute (full description on hover) is preserved on the cell itself.
  - Added a regression test to `src/components/BugReportsAdmin.test.tsx`: a 200-character title must render with no `truncate` class and both `whitespace-normal`/`break-words` present, the table element must carry `table-fixed`, and the Resolve button must still be present in the DOM. (A real "does it visually overflow the viewport" check needs an actual browser layout engine, which jsdom doesn't provide - this asserts the CSS mechanism the fix relies on instead.) Sanity-checked by reverting to the old truncating-span/unconstrained-columns version and confirming the new test failed with the real symptom (the `truncate` class present again), then restored. Verified the dev server still boots cleanly with the change (`npm run dev`); no browser automation tool is available in this environment for a full visual check. Test count: 955 → 956 (484 backend / 472 frontend); updated everywhere asserted. `tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run build` clean.

- **Code Hardening item #1 (fifth and final fix from the hardening audit): the two "Minor" findings - non-numeric `price` accepted by `record-winning-bid`, and `settings/update`/`delete-item` missing the lock/idempotency-key coverage their sibling mutating routes already have.** Closes out the full audit (see `refactor-opportunities.md`'s item #1, now fully resolved). Three fixes:
  - `server/routes/bidRoutes.ts`'s `POST /auction/record-winning-bid` validated `price` only by truthiness (`!price`), not that it was numeric - a non-numeric `price` got stored as-is, and `receiptDataService.ts`'s `resolveWonItems` (`parseFloat(...) || 0`) would then silently treat it as $0, recording the item as "won" but invoicing for nothing with no error surfaced anywhere. Fixed by coercing `price` to a number (accepting both a real number and a numeric string, matching how JSON bodies can arrive) and rejecting the request with a 400 if it's `NaN`/non-finite, before the coerced value is passed on to `recordWinningBid`.
  - `server/routes/dataRoutes.ts`'s `POST /settings/update` had neither `withLock` nor an idempotency key, unlike every other mutating route in this app - low likelihood given Settings is normally single-admin, but it does the exact same unsafe shape (read the full `'_Settings'!A:C` range to find a key's row, then write back to it) as everything already protected. Fixed by adding both: a new `settings:${spreadsheetId}` lock key (a different resource than `auction-items:*`/`bidder-numbers:*`) wrapping the read-then-write, and `checkIdempotency`/`saveIdempotency` around the route body, matching the `add-item`/`bulk-update-items` convention exactly. `useConfigSettingsState.ts`'s `syncSettingToCloud` now generates and sends an `idempotencyKey` (`window.crypto.randomUUID()`, generated once before `fetchWithRetry`'s internal retries reuse the same body).
  - `server/routes/dataRoutes.ts`'s `POST /auction/delete-item` had no `idempotencyKey` unlike `add-item`/`bulk-update-items` - low real risk on its own (re-deleting an already-blanked row is a no-op) but the same inconsistency. Fixed the same way; `src/services/google-api.ts`'s `deleteAuctionItem` now generates and sends an `idempotencyKey` too. (`deleteItem` itself was already covered by the `auction-items:${spreadsheetId}` lock added in the fix above this one - only the missing idempotency key needed closing here.)
  - Added 5 tests: `server/test/bid-management.test.ts` (non-numeric price rejected with 400; a numeric-string price is coerced and recorded correctly), `server/test/items.test.ts` (duplicate `delete-item` idempotency key returns the cached response instead of deleting twice; duplicate `settings/update` idempotency key returns the cached response without re-hitting the Sheets API; a concurrency test proving a second `settings/update` call can't even start its own read until the first has fully finished writing). The concurrency test surfaced a real gotcha in its own right: supertest's `Test` object is a *lazy* thenable that doesn't dispatch its HTTP request until something calls `.then()`/awaits it, so firing two requests and only awaiting both together via `Promise.all` at the end (the pattern used everywhere else in this session for lock tests) silently dispatched them one at a time instead of concurrently - fixed by wrapping each in its own immediately-invoked async function to force dispatch right away, matching the pattern this specific tool needs (route-level lock tests going forward should use this form, not the direct-service-call form `bidderService.test.ts`/`itemService-concurrency.test.ts` use). Sanity-checked all three fixes independently by reverting each and confirming its own test(s) failed with the real symptom (the non-numeric price silently accepted; the duplicate delete/settings-update re-executing; the second settings/update read starting before the first finished), then restored each. Test count: 931 → 936 (475 backend / 461 frontend); updated everywhere asserted. Also updated `testing-batches.md`'s Batch 1 command to include `NODE_OPTIONS=--no-experimental-webstorage` (previously Batch-2-only) - purely cosmetic, silencing Node 22+'s harmless `ExperimentalWarning: localStorage is not available` notice on the backend suite too, since it never touches `localStorage`. `tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run build` clean.

- **Code Hardening item #1 (fourth fix from the hardening audit): no client-side error boundary anywhere in the app - a render-time exception white-screened the entire app with no recovery path.** Found via the same hardening audit as the fixes above (see `refactor-opportunities.md`'s item #1): confirmed via grep that `src/` had zero `ErrorBoundary`/`componentDidCatch` usage, and `src/main.tsx` rendered `ConfigProvider > AuthProvider > AuctionProvider > App` with no catch boundary at any level - a crash mid-checkout or mid-bidding at a live event (e.g. a malformed/missing Sheets response reaching a `.map`/property access) had no recovery path short of the user realizing to manually reload. Fixed by adding `src/components/ErrorBoundary.tsx`, a class component (the one place in this app that still needs to be a class - `componentDidCatch`/`getDerivedStateFromError` have no hook equivalent) wrapping everything in `main.tsx`, including the context providers themselves, not just `<App />` - so a crash inside `ConfigProvider`/`AuthProvider`/`AuctionProvider` is caught too. On catch it feeds the error into `errorBuffer.ts`'s `recordError` (the same ring buffer a bug report's "Recent console activity" section already reads from - that function's own docblock already anticipated this exact use), and shows a friendly fallback (matching the app's existing `Card`-based full-page state pattern from `App.tsx`'s auth/loading screens) with the error message and a "Reload Page" button - deliberately not a "try again" reset, since the app doesn't know enough about what broke to safely re-render the same subtree without risking an immediate repeat crash. Added `src/components/ErrorBoundary.test.tsx` (4 tests: renders children normally, catches a descendant's render error and shows the fallback instead of white-screening, records into `errorBuffer`'s ring buffer, and the Reload button calls `window.location.reload`). Sanity-checked by breaking `getDerivedStateFromError` to always return a non-error state and confirming 3 of the 4 new tests failed with the real symptom (the error was silently swallowed and the boundary never rendered its fallback), then restored. Test count: 927 → 931 (470 backend / 461 frontend); updated everywhere asserted. `tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run build` clean.

- **Stale GAS unit tests found while the user was manually running `GoogleAIStudioAndSweeper_UnitTests.gs` in the live Apps Script editor to confirm the "Follow-Up" fix below: the 5 `recordPayment: *` test cases all failed with `studio.recordPayment is not a function`.** Not a regression from that fix - `GoogleAIStudio.recordPayment`/`apiRecordPayment` were deliberately ported to Node and removed from `Class_GoogleAIStudio.gs` back in Code Hardening item #2, Step 3 (`auctionService.ts`'s `checkoutMarkPaidLocked` is the replacement), but the 5 native GAS tests exercising the old method were never cleaned up and had been permanently failing since. Removed the 5 dead test functions, their now-unused `gasTests_bidderRow_` fixture helper (only they used it), and their entries in the runner's `cases` array, leaving only the 5 still-valid `sweeper: *` tests; updated the file's docblock to note the removal. No Node-side test changes needed - nothing in `server/test/` loaded this native-only test file. Confirmed by re-running the full backend suite (470/470 unaffected) and `tsc --noEmit`/`npm run build` clean, then the user re-ran `runGoogleAIStudioAndSweeperUnitTests` in the live editor (all 5 remaining sweeper tests passing, including the renamed `PayPal-Follow-Up` escalation from the fix below).

- **Code Hardening item #1 (third fix from the hardening audit): two independent code paths auto-created and wrote to a sheet literally named "Follow-Up" in the same Bid-Management spreadsheet, with incompatible column schemas.** Found via the same hardening audit as the two fixes below (see `refactor-opportunities.md`'s item #1). `auctionService.ts`'s `recordWinningBidLocked` (3-col header `["Bidder ID","Item ID","Amount"]`, writes `Follow-Up!A:C` when a bid is recorded for an already-checked-out bidder - read back by the client's Dashboard "Post-Checkout Item Wins" panel, `useDashboardState.ts`, which also appends 3-col rows for gift-card/raffle winners) collided with `paypalSweeperJob.ts`'s `ensureFollowUpSheet` (6-col header `["Timestamp","Bidder Number","Name","Amount Flagged","Invoice URL","System Notes"]`, writes `Follow-Up!A:F` when a PayPal-pending invoice times out). Whichever trigger fired first "won" the header/column layout for the auction; the other's later appends landed under the wrong header with misaligned data - already flagged as a known, not-yet-resolved risk when the sweeper moved to Node (Code Hardening item #2 Step 4's entry above). Fixed by renaming the PayPal sweeper's escalation destination to its own sheet, `PayPal-Follow-Up`, in both the Node port (`server/services/paypalSweeperJob.ts`) and the still-live reference copy (`scripts/GoogleAppsScriptsCode/BidManagementSpreadsheet/PayPalSweeper.gs`) - the two concepts (a staff follow-up on a won item vs. a diagnostic escalation for a stuck PayPal verification) are genuinely different data shapes, so a shared sheet/schema was never the right fix; a distinct sheet name removes the collision entirely with no client-facing change, since the Dashboard/`recordWinningBidLocked` 3-col "Follow-Up" sheet is untouched. Updated `server/test/paypalSweeperJob.test.ts` (added a regression assertion that the escalation append targets `PayPal-Follow-Up!A:F`, not `Follow-Up!A:F`), `server/test/gs-paypal-sweeper.test.ts`, and `scripts/GoogleAppsScriptsCode/BidManagementSpreadsheet/GoogleAIStudioAndSweeper_UnitTests.gs` to match. Sanity-checked by reverting the Node-side sheet-name constant to `"Follow-Up"` and confirming `paypalSweeperJob.test.ts`'s new assertion failed with the real symptom (the escalation append targeted `Follow-Up!A:F`), then restored. `PayPalSweeper.gs`/`GoogleAIStudioAndSweeper_UnitTests.gs` need manual re-copy into the live Bid Management Apps Script editor, per this repo's existing convention for `.gs` changes. Test count: 927 backend + frontend combined (470 backend / 457 frontend) - unchanged, since this fix edited existing tests rather than adding new ones; `tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run build` clean.

- **Code Hardening item #1 (second fix from the hardening audit): none of `itemService.ts`'s `addItem`/`deleteItem`/`updateItem`/`bulkUpdateItems` or `bundleService.ts`'s `processBundle`/`restoreBundle` were wrapped in the app's existing concurrency lock.** Found via the same hardening audit as the `checkInBidderWithBid` fix below (see `refactor-opportunities.md`'s item #1). All six functions do the same unsafe shape: read the full `'Auction-Items'!A:L` range, mutate the in-memory 2D array, then overwrite the whole range via `values.update({range: "'Auction-Items'!A1:L", ...})` - none imported or used `withLock`. Two staff working the item-entry/bundling workflow concurrently (a realistic pre-event pattern, unlike single-admin auction night) could silently discard each other's change: an added item vanishing, a deleted item reappearing, a bundle's item status reverting, a category re-sequencing getting undone. Fixed by wrapping all six in a new `auction-items:${spreadsheetId}` lock key (distinct from the bidder-mutating functions' `bidder-numbers:${spreadsheetId}` key - different resource, no reason to serialize against each other), following the exact `xLocked` helper-function split already established for the bidder-mutating functions. Added a concurrency regression test (`server/test/itemService-concurrency.test.ts`) mirroring `bidderService.test.ts`'s `checkInBidderWithBid` test: gates the first `addItem` call's Auction-Items read, proves the second doesn't even start until the first fully finishes writing, and asserts both items survive in the final sheet (not one clobbering the other). Sanity-checked by temporarily removing the `withLock` wrapper and confirming the new test failed with the real symptom (the second call started reading before the first had written, and its write would have clobbered the first's row), then restored. Test count: 927 backend + frontend combined (470 backend / 457 frontend); updated everywhere asserted. `tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run build` clean.

- **Code Hardening item #1 (first fix from the hardening audit): `checkInBidderWithBid` was the one bidder-mutating write path never wrapped in the app's existing concurrency lock.** Found via a broad hardening audit (see `refactor-opportunities.md`'s item #1) - `registerWalkInBidder`/`recordWinningBid`/`checkoutMarkPaid` all serialize per Bid-Management spreadsheet via `withLock` (`bidder-numbers:${spreadsheetId}`) because each does the same unsafe shape (scan for the bidder's own column/row from a fresh read, then write to it - two callers racing can compute the same target and the second write silently clobbers the first). `checkInBidderWithBid` (the ticket-payment check-in bid, `server/services/bidderService.ts`) does the identical shape but was never wrapped - two staff at two check-in stations processing bidders around the same time (or the same physical ticket at two stations, each generating its own idempotency key so the route's existing idempotency-cache check doesn't dedupe them) could silently lose one bidder's check-in bid. Fixed by wrapping it in the same lock key its siblings already use, following the exact `xLocked` helper-function split already established for the other three. Added a concurrency regression test to `server/test/bidderService.test.ts` (mirrors `auctionService.test.ts`'s `checkoutMarkPaid` concurrency test: gates the first check-in's Bid-Management read, proves the second doesn't even start until the first fully finishes). Sanity-checked by removing the `withLock` wrapper and confirming the new test failed with the real symptom (the second check-in started before the first's gate was released), then restored. Test count: 926 backend + frontend combined (469 backend / 457 frontend); updated everywhere asserted. `tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run build` clean.

- **Real bug found by the user right after Code Hardening item #3 shipped: every generated Items-spreadsheet document showed a doubled "$" and mismatched currency formatting on Value/Opening Bid/Min Raise (e.g. "$$1,000.00" instead of "$1,000.00" or "$1000").** Root cause: `server/services/itemDataService.ts`'s `getValidSortedItems` read `'Auction-Items'!A:L` with no `valueRenderOption`, defaulting to the Sheets API's `FORMATTED_VALUE` - since staff enter these fields as currency text (e.g. "$100.00") via `USER_ENTERED` writes elsewhere (`itemService.ts`), Sheets auto-converts them into real numbers with currency formatting applied, so `FORMATTED_VALUE` returns the pre-formatted display string ("$1,000.00") rather than the raw number. GAS's native `SpreadsheetApp.getValues()` - what every Doc generator's item-resolution path used before Step 3 moved it to Node - always returned that number RAW (no `$`, no thousands separator), which is exactly what every generator's own hardcoded `"$"` prefix (`Class_BidSheetDoc.gs`'s `valueLabel`, `Class_CatalogDoc.gs`'s `` `Value: $${item.value}` ``, `Class_BiddingGuideDoc.gs`, `Class_TableTentDoc.gs`, `Class_LiveAuctionSlides.gs`'s `VALUE_TEXT`) assumes. Fixed by requesting `valueRenderOption: "UNFORMATTED_VALUE"` on that one read - restores the exact raw-number values GAS always returned, with zero changes needed to any of the 5 Doc generators that print these fields (their own `"$"` prefix was already correct for a bare number). Scoped to only this new resolver - the item-entry CRUD screens' own separate reads (which intentionally want the formatted "$100.00" display for their editable Value input) are untouched.
  - Added 2 tests to `server/test/itemDataService.test.ts`: a regression test asserting the request actually carries `valueRenderOption=UNFORMATTED_VALUE`, and one confirming a numeric (not string) cell value from the mocked response resolves with no `$` prefix or thousands separator. Sanity-checked by removing the `valueRenderOption` override and confirming the regression test failed with the real symptom, then restored.
  - Test count: 925 backend + frontend combined (468 backend / 457 frontend); updated everywhere asserted. `tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run build` clean.

### Added

- **Code Hardening item #3: migrated the Auction Items spreadsheet's 8 Doc/Slides generators to the same "Node resolves data, GAS only produces the artifact" shape already used for Bid Management's receipts and Tickets' bidder labels.** Purely for architectural consistency (confirmed by the user) - unlike Bid Management, this project had no bug or live duplication/drift risk forcing the change.
  - `server/services/itemDataService.ts`'s new `getValidSortedItems(sheets, spreadsheetId)` ports `Class_AuctionItemManager.gs`'s `getValidSortedItems` (filter out `inactive`/`deleted` status case/whitespace-insensitively, sort ascending numerically by ID) - reusing `src/lib/itemParsing.ts`'s existing, already-tested row parsing rather than re-encoding the Auction-Items schema a second time (confirmed esbuild's server bundle resolves the `@/types` alias inside that shared file automatically, so no duplication was needed).
  - `server/routes/productionRoutes.ts`'s 8 `/item-entry/*` routes now require `spreadsheetId` in the request body (400 if missing, matching every other route that resolves data before bridging to Apps Script), call the new resolver, and forward `[folderId, items]` to each `apiCreate*` bridge call instead of just `[folderId]`. `src/components/DocumentCreationTab.tsx` now sends `config.auctionItemsSpreadsheetId` alongside `folderId`.
  - GAS side: added `Class_AuctionItem.gs`'s `AuctionItem.fromData()` static factory (mirrors `Class_Receipt.gs`'s `Receipt.fromData()` precedent), translating Node's resolved-item field names (`donor`/`itemType`/`bundleId`/`bundledDateTime` - `src/types.ts`'s `AuctionItem` shape) to this class's own (`donatedBy`/`phyOrCert`/`bundleID`/`bundleTimeStamp`). `Class_GoogleAIStudio.gs`'s 8 `create*` methods and bridge functions now accept `items` as a parameter and map it via `items.map(AuctionItem.fromData)`, instead of lazily constructing a shared `AuctionItemManager` - its `itemManager` getter/constructor-override point is gone. All 7 Doc generators (`Class_BundlingMeetingDoc.gs`, `Class_PackagingLabelsDoc.gs`, `Class_LiveAuctionSlides.gs`, `Class_BiddingGuideDoc.gs`, `Class_BidSheetDoc.gs`, `Class_CatalogDoc.gs`, `Class_TableTentDoc.gs`) now take `items` directly in their constructor instead of `itemManager`, replacing every `this.itemManager.getValidSortedItems()` call with `this.items` - each generator's own additional filtering (Live-only, skip Live/Movie Night, etc.) is unchanged, just reading from the new source.
  - Deleted `Class_AuctionItemManager.gs` entirely - `getValidSortedItems()`/`getAuctionItemAtIndex_`/the lazy `auctionItems` getter were its whole contents, and all three are now dead (confirmed via grep - nothing else in the `.gs` tree referenced the class after the above). Trimmed `AuctionItemManager_UnitTests.gs` (the native-tier, run-from-the-Apps-Script-editor test file) to drop its now-defunct `getValidSortedItems`/`itemManager`-override cases and fake-spreadsheet scaffolding, keeping `AuctionItem`'s display-fallback tests and adding new ones for `fromData()`.
  - Added `server/test/itemDataService.test.ts` (5 tests: status filtering, numeric sort, empty-sheet/no-values-at-all edge cases, field resolution). Rewrote `server/test/gs-items-auction-item-manager.test.ts` (now tests only `AuctionItem`'s display-fallbacks and its new `fromData()`, since `AuctionItemManager.gs` no longer exists) and trimmed `server/test/gs-items-google-ai-studio.test.ts` (dropped its `itemManager` constructor-override describe block; `_executeGeneration` coverage unchanged). Updated `server/test/production.test.ts`'s 8 item-entry test cases (each now sends `spreadsheetId` and expects the Node-resolved item list as the Apps Script call's 2nd parameter, computed once from `handlers.ts`'s existing default Auction-Items fixture) and its script-ID/business-failure tests (also need `spreadsheetId` now to get past the new required-field gate), plus a new "returns 400 when spreadsheetId is missing" test. Fixed `src/components/DocumentCreationTab.test.tsx`'s body-payload assertion to include `spreadsheetId`.
  - Sanity-checked the resolver's sort comparator by swapping it for a lexicographic one - the "sorts numerically, not lexicographically" test failed with the real symptom (`1301` sorting before `150`), then restored.
  - Test count: 923 backend + frontend combined (466 backend / 457 frontend); updated everywhere asserted. `tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run build` clean. **Requires manual re-copy into the live Items Apps Script editor**: `Class_AuctionItem.gs`, `Class_GoogleAIStudio.gs`, `Class_BundlingMeetingDoc.gs`, `Class_PackagingLabelsDoc.gs`, `Class_LiveAuctionSlides.gs`, `Class_BiddingGuideDoc.gs`, `Class_BidSheetDoc.gs`, `Class_CatalogDoc.gs`, `Class_TableTentDoc.gs`, `AuctionItemManager_UnitTests.gs`; **and manual deletion of `Class_AuctionItemManager.gs` from the live Apps Script project** (this repo's reference-copy deletion doesn't remove it there).

### Removed

- **Code Hardening item #2, Step 5: deleted `Class_EmailManager.gs`/`Class_Email.gs` - the GAS-side receipt-email path they supported was provably dead code, not just visibly-duplicate templates.** Re-investigating this step's original premise ("`Class_EmailManager.gs` currently duplicates `emailService.ts`'s receipt-email feature via a different code path, rendering visibly different HTML templates") found that premise no longer held: `_generateHtmlTemplate_` (GAS) and `generateEmailHtml` (`server/services/emailService.ts`) turned out to already be byte-for-byte equivalent in rendered output (same fonts/colors/copy/structure, `Class_GlobalStyles.gs`'s constants confirmed to resolve to the exact literals Node hardcodes) - likely unified by an earlier, unrelated fix (the fee-rate-mismatch fix already referenced both templates as already saying "3.49% + $0.49"). More importantly, grepping the whole client and `.gs` tree found `Class_GoogleAIStudio.gs`'s `createIndividualReceipt`'s `emailReceipt` flag (the only thing that ever triggered GAS's own `GmailApp.sendEmail` path) was **never once passed `true`** by anything real: the sole client caller (`useCheckoutReceiptActions.ts`'s `handlePrintReceipt`) hardcoded `emailReceipt: false`, `createAllReceipts`'s batch path hardcoded `mgr.shouldSendEmails = false` unconditionally, and even if some hypothetical caller had passed `true`, `ReceiptManager.handleEmailDispatch_`'s own `if (!this.shouldSendEmails) return;` guard would have no-opped anyway since nothing in `createIndividualReceipt` ever flipped that flag on the `ReceiptManager` instance it dispatched through - the email path was doubly unreachable. The app's one real, live "email a receipt" feature has always been Node's `/email-only`/`/email-all` routes.
  - Deleted `Class_EmailManager.gs`, `Class_Email.gs` entirely. `Class_ReceiptManager.gs` no longer constructs an `EmailManager`, no longer has `handleEmailDispatch_`/`shouldSendEmails`, and `processBatch_` no longer has an email-dispatch branch. `Class_GoogleAIStudio.gs`'s `createIndividualReceipt` dropped the `emailReceipt` parameter entirely (now `(bidderId, folderID, receiptData)`); `createAllReceipts` dropped the now-pointless `mgr.shouldSendEmails = false` line. `apiCreateIndividualReceipt`'s bridge signature shrank to match (`(bidderId, folderID, receiptData)`). Also deleted `Class_UIStrings.gs`'s `emailReceiptTitle`/`emailReceiptCheckoutQuestion` (already unreachable since the custom menu is disabled, doubly so now with the feature itself gone).
  - Node side: `server/services/receiptService.ts`'s `createIndividualReceipt` dropped its `emailReceipt` parameter and now sends `parameters: [String(bidderId), null, receiptData]` (3 positions, not 4) to match GAS's new signature exactly - the parameter *order* shift here is the one place a real bug could hide (an unnoticed position mismatch would silently misroute `receiptData` into `folderID`'s slot), so it got a dedicated sanity check. `receiptRoutes.ts`'s `/generate` no longer destructures/forwards `emailReceipt`. `useCheckoutReceiptActions.ts`'s `handlePrintReceipt` no longer sends the now-meaningless `emailReceipt: false` field.
  - Updated `server/test/gs-bidmanagement-receipt-manager.test.ts`'s three `createIndividualReceipt` validation tests and its bridge-arity regression test (now asserts 3 parameters, not 4) for the new signature; updated `server/test/receipts.test.ts`'s happy-path parameter-forwarding test (dropped the `emailReceipt: true` request field and the `true` entry in the expected Apps Script parameters array) and its other tests' now-unnecessary `emailReceipt: false` request fields.
  - Sanity-checked the Node-to-GAS parameter *order* realignment specifically (the one genuinely new/risky part of this change, versus the rest being straightforward deletion) by swapping `receiptData`/`null`'s positions in `receiptService.ts`'s `parameters` array - the forwarding test failed with the real symptom (receiptData landing in `folderID`'s slot), then restored.
  - Test count: 922 backend + frontend combined (465 backend / 457 frontend, unchanged net - deletions and test updates balanced out); updated everywhere asserted. `tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run build` clean. **Requires manual re-copy into the live Bid Management Apps Script editor**: `Class_ReceiptManager.gs`, `Class_GoogleAIStudio.gs`, `Class_UIStrings.gs`; **and manual deletion of `Class_EmailManager.gs`/`Class_Email.gs` from the live Apps Script project** (this repo's reference-copy deletion doesn't remove them there).

### Fixed

- **The `firestore.rules` fix below didn't actually resolve the reported "Missing or insufficient permissions" bug on its own - found and fixed the real remaining cause.** After deploying the rules fix, the user still hit the same error. Two things turned out to be true at once:
  - `firebase.json`'s `firestore` config was a single object (`{ "database": "...", "rules": "..." }`) instead of the array form (`[{ "database": "...", "rules": "..." }]`) the Firebase CLI needs to reliably target a named/non-default database - this project's Firestore database is the named `ai-studio-8ae64d57-...` one, not `(default)`. Fixed the array shape; a redeploy afterward reported "already up to date, skipping upload," confirming the rules content was in fact already live on the correct database - so this wasn't actually the active bug, just a latent risk worth fixing anyway.
  - **The real cause**: read the live `settings/global` document directly (via a one-off script using the newly-added `firebase-admin`, now that ADC was set up for the PayPal sweeper) and found it still carried a stale `auctionItemsSheet` field - left over from before that field was removed from `Config`, and never cleaned up because `ConfigContext.tsx`'s Firestore snapshot handler merged as a blind `{...defaultConfig, ...docSnap.data()}` spread. That let the stale key ride along into local `config` state on every load, and every subsequent save wrote it right back out alongside the 15 real `Config` fields - 18 keys total once `paypalIsSandbox` was added, tripping `firestore.rules`' strict `keys().size() == 17` check on literally every save, regardless of the rules content being otherwise correct. Confirmed with the user before editing `ConfigContext.tsx` (protected file, per `AGENTS.md`).
