Fixed the merge to copy only the keys `defaultConfig` actually declares, field-by-field, instead of spreading whatever Firestore hands back - drops any current or future stale/removed field automatically, while keeping the existing "newly-added field defaults instead of `undefined`" behavior intact. One-time cleanup: deleted the stale `auctionItemsSheet` field from the live document directly (the same one-off admin script). Added a regression test to `src/context/ConfigContext.test.tsx` (a Firestore doc carrying `auctionItemsSheet` alongside the real `Config` fields must resolve to exactly `Config`'s shape, with `auctionItemsSheet` gone). Sanity-checked by reverting to the blind-spread version and confirming the new test failed with the real symptom (the stale field surviving into local state), then restored. Test count: 922 backend + frontend combined (465 backend / 457 frontend); updated everywhere asserted. `tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run build` clean.

- **`firestore.rules`'s `isValidConfig()` was silently rejecting every Config save since Step 1 of this same item.** `paypalIsSandbox` was added to `Config` (`src/types.ts`/`ConfigContext.tsx`) without updating `firestore.rules`'s strict `hasAll([...]) && keys().size() == 17` allowlist - the exact same bug already documented and fixed once before in this file for `bugReportsSpreadsheetId`/`bugReportsFolderId`, this time self-inflicted. `firestore.rules` and `ConfigContext.tsx` are both explicitly protected files per `AGENTS.md`'s critical rules ("do not edit... unless explicitly ordered") - confirmed with the user before touching `firestore.rules` (same protocol as the earlier fix). Replaced the rules' stale `auctionItemsSheet` entry (a field `Config` doesn't even have any more) with `paypalIsSandbox` (`is bool`) in both `isValidConfig()`'s key list/type-checks and the `update` rule's `affectedKeys().hasOnly([...])` list, keeping the strict count at 17. **Requires `firebase deploy --only firestore:rules` to actually take effect** - not verifiable by this session (no Firestore emulator available here, same as the earlier fix).

### Added

- **Code Hardening item #2, Step 4: gave `PayPalSweeper.gs`'s background reconciliation loop a Node-side home.** Unlike Steps 1-3, this isn't "data Node fetches on request" - it's a standing background service with no request to attach to, which surfaced a real architectural gap: this app has no standing server credential at all (every existing Google API call authenticates via whichever staff member's OAuth session is live). Resolved per the user's explicit choice (persist an admin's refresh token) rather than piggybacking on the last active session or moving the sweep to client-side polling.
  - **New standing credential store**: `server/services/firebaseAdmin.ts` (added the `firebase-admin` dependency) gives the server admin-privileged Firestore access via Application Default Credentials (automatic on Cloud Run; needs `GOOGLE_APPLICATION_CREDENTIALS` locally, documented in `.env.example`). `authRoutes.ts`'s `/auth/callback` now best-effort-persists every login's refresh token to `system/paypalSweeperAuth` (admin-only, not covered by `firestore.rules`, never client-writable) via `paypalSweeperJob.ts`'s new `persistSweeperCredential` - a normal login already returns a fresh refresh token every time (`prompt: "select_account consent"`), so no new consent screen was needed; the sweeper simply always authenticates as whichever staff member most recently logged in. A failed persist never blocks or fails the actual login.
  - **`server/services/paypalSweeperJob.ts`**: `runPaypalSweep` ports `PayPalSweeper.gs`'s `checkPendingPayPalInvoices` exactly - scans `Bidder-Numbers` bottom-up for the `"PayPal Verification Pending"` marker (Step 3's deferred credit-card path), resolves it via `getInvoiceStatus` (`PAID`/`MARKED_AS_PAID` -> checked-out/paid/"Credit Card / PayPal", clearing the timestamp), seeds a missing/invalid timestamp defensively, or escalates to `Follow-Up` (creating the sheet if needed, with its own 6-column header - a different, pre-existing schema than `auctionService.ts`'s own 3-column Follow-Up usage for already-checked-out bidders' new wins; both already target the same sheet name in production today, an existing risk this port doesn't introduce or resolve) once pending longer than 10 minutes. One row's failure is logged and skipped rather than aborting the sweep. Reads `bidManagementSpreadsheetId`/`bidderNumbersSheet`/`paypalIsSandbox` directly from the same `settings/global` Config document the client syncs (via the same admin Firestore access). `startPaypalSweeperSchedule` runs it on a fixed interval (`PAYPAL_SWEEPER_INTERVAL_MINUTES`, default 5) via a plain `setInterval` - not `node-cron`, since a fixed-interval background loop needs nothing a cron-expression library adds, and this keeps the change dependency-light. Wired into `server.ts` only (never `server/app.ts`, which the test suite exercises directly via supertest - no background timer runs during tests).
  - Deleted... nothing yet: `PayPalSweeper.gs` and its GAS time-driven trigger are deliberately **not** deleted this session - Step 4's own plan says to delete them "once the Node job is confirmed running reliably," which isn't verifiable from here (no way to observe production sweep runs). `Class_PayPalService.gs` also stays, still needed by `PayPalSweeper.gs` and by nothing else GAS-side. `server/test/gs-paypal-sweeper.test.ts`/`gs-paypal-service.test.ts` (the GAS reference-copy tests) are unaffected and still pass.
  - Added `server/test/paypalSweeperJob.test.ts` (11 tests: skip conditions for no stored credential/no configured spreadsheet, the PAID/MARKED_AS_PAID resolve path, the >10-minute escalation path including the real appended Follow-Up row's contents, the missing-timestamp seed path, the no-invoice-ID skip, per-row error resilience across two pending rows, non-pending rows being ignored, and the sandbox-flag passthrough to `PayPalService`'s constructor) and 1 assertion to the existing `server/test/auth.test.ts` happy-path test (the callback's refresh token reaches `persistSweeperCredential`). `firebaseAdmin.ts` and `paypalService.ts` are fully module-mocked in both so no test ever attempts a real Firestore/PayPal network call.
  - Sanity-checked the PAID-vs-timeout branch dispatch by forcing the PAID/MARKED_AS_PAID check to always fail - the three tests that depend on that resolve path failed with the real symptom, then restored.
  - Test count: 921 backend + frontend combined (465 backend / 456 frontend); updated everywhere asserted. `tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run build` clean.

- **Code Hardening item #2, Step 3: moved checkout mark-paid's PayPal recording into Node.** `server/services/auctionService.ts`'s `checkoutMarkPaid` no longer bridges to Apps Script for the actual payment recording - it reads the bidder's row from `Bidder-Numbers` itself, constructs `new PayPalService({ isSandbox: paypalIsSandbox !== false })` (same sandbox-safe default as Steps 1-2), and ports `Class_GoogleAIStudio.gs`'s (now-deleted) `recordPayment` branch-for-branch: cash/check writes checked-out/paid/method-label and best-effort-records the offline payment on PayPal (a PayPal-side failure never blocks marking the bidder paid in the sheet); credit_card checks the live invoice status via `getInvoiceStatus` and either marks paid immediately (status `PAID`) or defers to the background sweeper, writing `"PayPal Verification Pending"` plus an ISO timestamp for it to pick up later (Step 4, still pending); an unrecognized method throws, as before. Threaded `paypalIsSandbox` through `bidRoutes.ts` -> `useCheckoutPaymentFlow.ts` -> `Checkout.tsx`'s `config.paypalIsSandbox`, the same path used for receipts in Step 2.
  - Deleted `Class_GoogleAIStudio.gs`'s `recordPayment` (plus a long-dead, already-commented-out earlier draft of the same method) and its `apiRecordPayment` bridge function, and the `paypalService` constructor-injection point that existed only for that method. `Class_GoogleAIStudio.gs` no longer references `PayPalService` at all.
  - Rewrote `server/test/auctionService.test.ts`'s `checkoutMarkPaid` concurrency test: it used to gate a mocked `google.script` bridge call; now it gates the mocked `google.sheets` Bidder-Numbers read directly, since there's no Apps Script call left to intercept. Deleted `server/test/gs-google-ai-studio.test.ts` (tested the now-deleted GAS `recordPayment`/`apiRecordPayment`) and replaced it with `server/test/checkoutMarkPaid.test.ts` (10 tests), mirroring every one of its scenarios against the new Node logic directly (cash/check happy path plus the "Check" label variant, still-marks-paid-when-PayPal-throws, skips-PayPal-call-with-no-invoice-ID, credit_card's PAID/deferred/no-invoice-ID/getInvoiceStatus-failure branches, bidder-not-found, and unrecognized-method) - `PayPalService` is fully module-mocked here so assertions can pin exact call arguments the same way the deleted GAS test did with its injected fake `paypalService`.
  - Updated `server/test/bid-management.test.ts`'s and `server/test/tickets.test.ts`'s checkout tests to match: removed a describe block that only proved the (now-gone) Apps Script bridge received the right parameters regardless of payment-method casing; replaced an "Apps Script failure -> 500" test with an equivalent "Sheets API failure -> 500" test (there being no Apps Script call left to fail); replaced an idempotency test's `appsScriptCallCount` assertion with an `updateCallCount` one (the duplicate submission must not re-run the two Bidder-Numbers writes); and fixed a test that happened to send a bidder ID absent from the (now actually consulted) default Bidder-Numbers mock.
  - Sanity-checked the credit_card PAID/deferred branch dispatch by forcing the `liveStatus === "PAID"` check to always fail - the "marks paid immediately" test failed with the real symptom (fell into the deferred branch instead), then restored.
  - Test count: 910 backend + frontend combined (454 backend / 456 frontend); updated everywhere asserted. `tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run build` clean. **Requires manual re-copy into the live Bid Management Apps Script editor**: `Class_GoogleAIStudio.gs`.

- **Code Hardening item #2, Step 2: moved PayPal invoice creation for receipts out of `Class_ReceiptManager.gs` and into Node, and fixed a real, currently-broken production bug found along the way.** `server/services/receiptDataService.ts` now has the full invoice get-or-create logic ported from `Class_ReceiptManager.gs`'s (now-removed) `getOrCreateInvoiceUrl`: `findExistingInvoice` (reads Bidder-Numbers columns J/K for an already-fetched grid), `resolveInvoiceUrl` (reuse an existing invoice if the invoiced amount still matches within $0.01, best-effort-cancel-then-recreate if it's stale, or create fresh if there's none - using the Step 1 `paypalService.ts`), and two new top-level orchestrators, `buildBidderReceiptWithInvoice`/`buildAllBidderReceiptsWithInvoices`, which resolve the receipt AND the invoice together and write the result back to Bidder-Numbers (`values.update` for one bidder, a single `values.batchUpdate` for the whole batch). `server/routes/receiptRoutes.ts`'s `/generate`/`/generate-all` now call these instead of the plain `buildBidderReceipt`/`buildAllBidderReceipts`, constructing `new PayPalService({ isSandbox: paypalIsSandbox !== false })` - defaulting to sandbox unless the client's Config toggle (see Step 1) explicitly says otherwise, so a missing value can never mean production. Threaded `paypalIsSandbox` through `useCheckoutState.ts` -> `useCheckoutReceiptActions.ts`/`useCheckoutBatchActions.ts` -> `Checkout.tsx`'s `config.paypalIsSandbox`.
  - **Found and fixed a real, currently-reachable production bug while touching these same functions**: `Class_GoogleAIStudio.gs`'s bridge functions `apiCreateAllReceipts(folderID)` and `apiCreateIndividualReceipt(bidderId, emailReceipt, folderID)` were declared with fewer parameters than `server/services/receiptService.ts` actually sends via the Apps Script Execution API (`parameters: [bidderId, emailReceipt, null, receiptData]` / `[folderID, receipts]`) - since JS silently drops call-site arguments that don't bind to a declared parameter, every real production call was resolving a full receipt in Node and then throwing it away before `createIndividualReceipt`/`createAllReceipts` ever saw it, so every real receipt-generation call was already failing with "No receipt data provided." (or silently running with an empty receipts list) before this fix. Fixed both bridge functions to declare and forward every parameter Node sends.
  - `Class_ReceiptManager.gs` no longer touches `PayPalService` at all: deleted `getOrCreateInvoiceUrl`, `getPaymentLink`, `calculateAdjustedTotalWithFees`, and the now-unused `customPaymentUrl` field - it consumes `receipt.paymentUrl`/`receipt.adjustedTotal` directly, both now carried by `Class_Receipt.gs`'s `Receipt.fromData` (mirroring the items/total precedent from Step 0). `Class_ReceiptDoc.gs`'s `writeReceiptBody_` and `Class_ReceiptManager.gs`'s `handleEmailDispatch_`/`processBatch_` updated accordingly. `Class_GoogleAIStudio.gs`'s `createIndividualReceipt` now validates `receiptData.paymentUrl` is present (mirroring its existing `receiptData` validation) before touching `ReceiptManager` at all, since Node must always resolve the invoice before calling this now.
  - Added 14 tests to `server/test/receiptDataService.test.ts` (`findExistingInvoice`, `resolveInvoiceUrl`'s reuse/stale-cancel-and-recreate/create-fresh/cancel-failure paths, `buildBidderReceiptWithInvoice`, `buildAllBidderReceiptsWithInvoices`'s per-bidder resilience) and 4 to `server/test/gs-bidmanagement-receipt-manager.test.ts` (`Receipt.fromData` carrying `paymentUrl`/`adjustedTotal`, the new missing-`paymentUrl` validation, and an arity-based regression guard pinning the bridge-function parameter-count bug specifically, since `new GoogleAIStudio()`'s construction always throws with no `SpreadsheetApp` override in this sandbox - the same constraint the existing bridge-function tests already work around). Added default PayPal sandbox MSW handlers to `server/test/handlers.ts` (token/invoices/send/cancel/status) so any test exercising this path gets a deterministic mock instead of ever reaching real PayPal - the live `api-m.paypal.com` host is deliberately left unmocked so a test that ever resolves to production fails loudly (`setup.ts` throws on any unmocked external request) instead of silently "succeeding." Added `PAYPAL_CLIENT_ID`/`PAYPAL_CLIENT_SECRET`/`PAYPAL_IS_SANDBOX` test values to `vitest.config.ts` so tests never depend on (or risk touching) a developer's real local `.env` credentials. Updated `server/test/receipts.test.ts`'s existing `/generate` happy-path assertion for the new `paymentUrl`/`adjustedTotal` fields now attached to the Apps Script call.
  - Sanity-checked `resolveInvoiceUrl`'s reuse-vs-recreate branch by forcing the amount-match check to always fail - the reuse tests failed with the real symptom (falling through to a live invoice-creation attempt instead of reusing the existing URL), then restored.
  - Test count: 911 backend + frontend combined (455 backend / 456 frontend); updated everywhere asserted. `tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run build` clean. **Requires manual re-copy into the live Bid Management Apps Script editor**: `Class_Receipt.gs`, `Class_ReceiptManager.gs`, `Class_ReceiptDoc.gs`, `Class_GoogleAIStudio.gs`.

- **Added a link to the PayPal sandbox/business dashboard on the Settings page**, per the user's request while working on the above - `PayPalIntegrationCard.tsx` now has an "Open PayPal Sandbox Dashboard" / "Open PayPal Business Dashboard" button (swapping URL and label with the sandbox/production toggle) so staff don't have to remember the URL while testing. 2 new tests.

- **Code Hardening item #2, Step 1: ported `Class_PayPalService.gs` to Node, and added an in-app sandbox/production toggle so the next steps can be tested safely.** Added `server/services/paypalService.ts`, a straight structural port of the GAS class's `getAccessToken_`/`createAndSendInvoice`/`cancelInvoice`/`recordOfflinePayment`/`getInvoiceStatus`/`extractInvoiceIdFromUrl` (same request shapes, same behavior), swapping `UrlFetchApp.fetch` for Node's `fetch`, Script Properties for `PAYPAL_CLIENT_ID`/`PAYPAL_CLIENT_SECRET`/`PAYPAL_IS_SANDBOX` env vars (documented in `.env.example`), and reusing `financeUtils.ts`'s existing `calculatePayPalSurcharge` rather than porting a second copy (per the plan already written into `refactor-opportunities.md`). Nothing in the app calls this class yet - Steps 2-4 (wiring it into receipt/invoice creation, checkout mark-paid, and the PayPal sweeper) are still pending - so this is a standalone, fully-tested unit with no live caller.
  - **Sandbox/production toggle**: per the user's explicit ask ("the ability within the app to switch...so we can test the integration...without fear of creating real invoices and sending real emails out to the bidders on accident"), added `paypalIsSandbox: boolean` to `Config` (`src/types.ts`), defaulting to `true` (sandbox) everywhere it's read - `ConfigContext.tsx`'s `defaultConfig` only reads production from an explicit localStorage `"false"`, so a missing/corrupted value can never silently mean production. Exposed via a new `src/components/PayPalIntegrationCard.tsx` on the Settings page (`ConfigSettings.tsx`) - a checkbox plus a Sandbox/Production `Badge`, with a visible red warning when production mode is on. Saved through the existing config-sync path (`useConfigSettingsState.ts`'s `handleSave` -> `saveConfig` -> Firestore's global `settings/global` doc, the same mechanism every other `Config` field already uses) - no new sync plumbing needed. This field has no consumer yet; Steps 2-4 should read `config.paypalIsSandbox` and pass it as the `isSandbox` override to `new PayPalService(...)` rather than relying on the `PAYPAL_IS_SANDBOX` env var, so a deploy-wide env var can't override a per-session safety choice.
  - Added `server/test/paypalService.test.ts` (28 tests) mirroring `server/test/gs-paypal-service.test.ts`'s conventions (mocked `fetcher`, request-shape assertions) method-for-method, plus Node-specific coverage the GAS tests don't need: explicit sandbox-vs-production base-URL selection (both via override and via `PAYPAL_IS_SANDBOX` env var), env-var credential fallback, and `cancelInvoice` (untested on the GAS side). Added `src/components/PayPalIntegrationCard.test.tsx` (4 tests): badge/checkbox state and the production warning banner both render correctly for each mode, and toggling the checkbox in either direction flips `config.paypalIsSandbox` via `setConfig` without touching any other field.

- **Closed the next test-coverage gap and fixed a real bug it surfaced**: `src/lib/itemParsing.ts`'s header-name detection (`getCol`, matching a header cell against a list of candidate substrings, falling back to a fixed index only if nothing matches) had zero direct test coverage. Before writing tests, pulled the ACTUAL header row `server/services/itemService.ts`'s `addItem` writes to a brand-new Auction-Items sheet (`"Auction Item ID", "Category", "Item Name", "Description", "Value", "Opening Bid", "Min Raise Amount", "Donated By", "Physical Item, Certificate, or Both?", "Status", "Bundle ID", "Bundled Date/Time"`) - noticeably more verbose than the shorter header text used across this codebase's own test fixtures (`"Item Number"`, `"Min Raise"`, `"Physical/Cert"`) - and used it as the real-world ground truth. Found that `donorIdx`'s only candidate (`"donor"`) never matches `"Donated By"` in either header flavor, and `typeIdx`'s candidates (`"item type"`, `"type"`) never match `"Physical/Cert"` or `"Physical Item, Certificate, or Both?"` either - both fields were *always* silently falling back to their hardcoded default index (7 and 8), never actually doing the name-based detection the function's own docblock promises ("Column positions are detected by header name rather than assumed fixed"). Currently masked because the default column order happens to still be correct today, but a real-world column reorder - exactly the drift scenario this mechanism exists to survive - would have silently bound `donor`/`itemType` to the wrong column with no error. Fixed by widening the candidate lists: `donorIdx` now also matches `"donated"`, `typeIdx` now also matches `"physical"`/`"cert"` - both chosen to match the real production header text, not just the shorter test-fixture one. Confirmed the Apps Script side has no equivalent bug: `ItemsSpreadsheet/Class_AuctionItem.gs` reads columns by fixed position only, with no header-name-detection logic to have the same drift risk. Added `src/lib/itemParsing.test.ts` (10 tests): correct mapping under both real header-text flavors, a reordered-columns test that follows the header labels rather than fixed positions for every field (this is the one that caught the bug - `donor` read `"$250"`, the opening-bid value, instead of `"Joe's Pizza"` before the fix), header-name matching being case/whitespace-insensitive, positional fallback when a header cell is blank, the `status` default of `"Active"`, and row-inclusion/`rowIndex` behavior. Sanity-checked by reverting the candidate-list fix and confirming exactly the reordered-columns test failed with the real `donor`-reads-the-wrong-column symptom, then re-applied. - **Closed the third test-coverage gap and fixed the exact rollback bug `refactor-opportunities.md` already suspected**: `src/hooks/useWinnersData.ts` (only ever consumed through `useCheckoutState.ts`, which `Checkout.test.tsx` mocks wholesale, so none of its real logic had ever run under the suite) had zero direct coverage. Writing a test for the *correct* rollback behavior - two winning bids recorded back-to-back, before either re-renders, where the second one fails - immediately caught the bug the audit had flagged as "the clearest instance" of the whole-snapshot-rollback pattern: `recordWinningBid` captured `previousWinners`/`previousTotal` from the render closure and restored them absolutely on failure, so bid #2's rollback silently erased bid #1's already-committed, successfully-synced win too. Fixed by scoping the rollback to just the one item/amount each call actually touched - `previousWinnerForItem = winners[itemId]` (a single value, not a whole-map snapshot) and a functional `setTotalRaised(prev => prev - price)` - matching the pattern already applied to `useAuctionItemsTableState.ts`'s `handleUpdate`. Added `src/hooks/useWinnersData.test.ts` (11 tests) via `renderHook`: `fetchWinners`'s bidder-name-lookup happy path and its `"Bidder <id>"` fallback when the winner isn't in the cached bidders list, its no-spreadsheetId/no-bidders-yet no-ops and swallowed-fetch-failure path; `recordWinningBid`'s missing-spreadsheetId throw, the optimistic update landing before the network call settles (deferred-promise test), the real POST payload and success toast (plus the Follow-Up info toast when `followUpAdded`), a single failed sync rolling back cleanly, and the regression guard itself - two concurrent bids where the second fails must leave the first's win and its contribution to `totalRaised` untouched. Sanity-checked by reverting the scoped rollback to the old whole-snapshot version and confirming exactly the single-failure and concurrent-rollback tests failed with the real symptom (the first bid's already-committed win disappearing), then re-applied the fix. - **Closed the last item on the original test-coverage-gap list: guarded `server/utils/financeUtils.ts`'s `calculatePayPalSurcharge` against negative input.** `calculatePayPalSurcharge(-1)` used to silently return `{ total: -0.53, surcharge: 0.47 }` - a negative invoice total, which is nonsensical - with no guard. Its only real caller, `emailService.ts`'s `processEmail` (generates a bidder's real receipt/invoice email), sums item prices via `parseFloat(...) || 0`, which only catches `NaN`, not a genuinely negative number sitting in a corrupted price cell. Per the user's explicit choice (throw rather than silently clamp to $0, since this is real money and a corrupted bidder's receipt failing loudly is safer than telling them a made-up amount), added a guard that throws when `bidAmount < 0`. Making this throw surfaced a second, pre-existing gap while implementing it: `server/services/receiptService.ts`'s `processAllEmails` (the "email every bidder their receipt" batch operation, used by `/api/receipts/email-all`) loops calling `processEmail` per bidder with **no per-bidder try/catch** - any thrown error, from this new guard or any other cause (a Sheets API error, a malformed row), already aborted the whole batch uncaught, silently skipping every remaining bidder's receipt. Fixed by wrapping the per-bidder call in its own try/catch, `console.error`-ing the specific bidder's failure and continuing - matching the "one bad row shouldn't kill the whole batch" resilience already applied to `auctionSetupService.ts`'s year-end reset earlier this session. Added 2 tests to `server/test/financeUtils.test.ts` (the negative-guard throw, for both `-1` and `-0.01`) and a new `server/test/receiptService.test.ts` (4 tests, `processAllEmails` had zero coverage of any kind before this - `processEmail` itself mocked away since its own Sheets/Gmail/OAuth surface is a separate, larger gap out of scope here): every bidder gets emailed and counted, one bidder's thrown error doesn't abort the batch and the rest still get emailed, a `{success: false}` return isn't counted, and rows with no id/no email are skipped without ever calling `processEmail`. Sanity-checked both fixes independently by reverting each and confirming the relevant test failed with the real symptom (the guard test threw `undefined` instead of an error; the resilience test's batch aborted uncaught after bidder 102, per bidder 103 never being attempted), then re-applied. Test count: 541 → 546; updated the count everywhere it's asserted. Doesn't touch Apps Script. `tsc --noEmit` clean; full suite 546/546, `npm run build` clean.

- **Closed the top-ranked remaining test-coverage gap and fixed the same whole-snapshot rollback bug in a third file**: `src/hooks/useAttendeesData.ts` (only ever consumed through `useTicketAttendeeState.ts`/`TicketAttendeeTab.tsx`, which `TicketAttendeeTab.test.tsx` mocks wholesale, so none of its real logic had ever run under the suite) had zero direct coverage. `addAttendee` and `removeAttendee` both had the exact bug already fixed in `useWinnersData.ts`'s `recordWinningBid`: each captured `previousAttendees` from the render closure and restored it absolutely on failure, so two adds/removes triggered back-to-back (before either re-renders) both capture the same baseline - if the second fails, its rollback erases the first's already-committed change too. Fixed both to scope their rollback to just the one entry each call touched: `addAttendee`'s failure now removes only its own optimistically-added temp entry (`setAttendees(prev => prev.filter(a => a.id !== tempId))`); `removeAttendee`'s failure now re-adds back only the one attendee it removed, guarded against double-adding if it's already present. Writing the concurrent-add regression test surfaced a second, related latent bug: `tempId = \`temp-${Date.now()}\`` alone could collide for two adds triggered in the same millisecond (a real possibility once the rollback is scoped by id) - fixed by adding a random suffix so concurrent temp ids never collide. Added `src/hooks/useAttendeesData.test.ts` (14 tests) via `renderHook`: `fetchAttendees`'s combine-three-sheets happy path and its per-sheet swallowed-failure resilience; `addAttendee`'s optimistic update (real payment columns on the general "tickets" sheet vs. hardcoded `Comp`/`Paid`/`$0` on staff/sponsor, matching `attendeeParsing.ts`'s already-verified mapping), its real `appendSheetData` payload and re-fetch-on-success, a single failed rollback, and the concurrent-rollback regression guard; the equivalent set for `removeAttendee`. Sanity-checked all the new-behavior assertions by reverting each fix independently (both to a `setAttendees([])` reset) and confirming exactly the relevant tests failed with the real symptom (the first, already-committed entity wiped out by the second's rollback), then re-applied. Test count: 546 → 560; updated the count everywhere it's asserted. Doesn't touch Apps Script. `tsc --noEmit` clean; full suite 560/560, `npm run build` clean.

- **Closed `src/context/ConfigContext.tsx`'s test-coverage gap and fixed the already-logged Firestore listener leak - the file `patterns.md` marks "DO NOT MODIFY... unless explicitly ordered."** Per the user's explicit go-ahead (given specifically for this fix), confirmed the bug while designing the tests: the `useEffect`'s `return () => unsubConfig()` was returned from *inside* the nested `onAuthStateChanged` callback, not from the effect's own callback - Firebase ignores that return value entirely (a nested callback's return isn't how the effect-cleanup contract works), so the Firestore config listener was never actually unsubscribed on sign-out/re-sign-in or unmount; every sign-out→sign-in cycle stacked another live listener. Fixed by hoisting `unsubConfig` to the effect's outer scope, tearing it down both before starting a new one on every `onAuthStateChanged` firing and in the effect's real cleanup. Added `src/context/ConfigContext.test.tsx` (13 tests) via `renderHook`, with a fully-controllable mock of `@/services/firebase`'s `getFirebase()` (manually-fireable `onAuthStateChanged`/`onSnapshot` callbacks, inspectable unsubscribe spies): the signed-out default-config path, applying a real Firestore snapshot on sign-in (including the full localStorage mirror and both legacy keys), the `permission-denied` toast branch (and confirming other error codes don't trigger it), `loginToFirebase`'s `@orns.org` domain enforcement (happy path, restricted-and-signed-back-out, and the sign-in-itself-fails path), `saveConfig`'s signed-out refusal and its real `setDoc` payload/success/failure paths, and - the regression guard for the fix itself - that the config listener is actually torn down across a sign-out/re-sign-in cycle and on unmount. Sanity-checked by reverting the fix and confirming exactly those 2 tests failed with the real symptom (the unsubscribe spy never called), then re-applied. Also hit and fixed a real test-authoring pitfall along the way, unrelated to `ConfigContext.tsx` itself: this file is the first in the suite to read `localStorage` (the bare global, not `window.localStorage`) at module-load time, which exposed that Node 22's own built-in Web Storage global can leave `window.localStorage` undefined in this repo's jsdom test environment unless `NODE_OPTIONS=--no-experimental-webstorage` is set - already the reason `npm run test:quiet` exists (see its own earlier changelog entry) - so this file's tests require that variant, same as the rest of the suite; investigated a setup.ts-level fix but `window.localStorage` itself is unavailable here regardless, so there was nothing to re-point during setup - reverted that attempt rather than leave dead code in a shared test file. Test count: 560 → 573; updated the count everywhere it's asserted. Doesn't touch Apps Script. `tsc --noEmit` clean; full suite 573/573 (via `npm run test:quiet`), `npm run build` clean.

- **Closed `src/lib/bidderAttendeeInfo.ts`'s test-coverage gap and fixed a real dead-code bug it surfaced: `getBidderAttendeeInfo`'s guest match could never actually use its `ownBidderNumber` check.** `Guest.ownBidderNumber` never holds a real bidder ID - per `attendeeParsing.ts`/`syncService.ts`, that sheet column literally holds the "yes"/"no" opt-in flag ("do you want your own bidder number?"), never a numeric ID like `Bidder.id` (e.g. `"105"`) - so `g.ownBidderNumber === bidder.id` was permanently dead code, and worse, a latent false-positive risk (a stray flag value that happens to coincidentally equal some bidder's numeric id as a string would incorrectly match). In practice this meant guest-to-attendee matching relied entirely on exact, freeform full-name matching - fragile against nicknames/typos/two guests sharing a name, with real consequences since the caller (`useBiddersState.ts`'s `sortedBidders`) treats *any* matched guest as automatically paid (`isPaid = isGuest || ...`) and uses the match for the source-filter tabs. Per the user's explicit choice, added email as a second, more reliable match path (both `Bidder` and `Guest` already carry `email`) alongside the existing name fallback, and removed the dead `ownBidderNumber` check entirely. Added `src/lib/bidderAttendeeInfo.test.ts` (13 tests): primary name matching (including case/whitespace-insensitivity, primary-over-guest precedence, and the no-match fallback), guest matching by email (the core fix - including a nickname-mismatch scenario where name-matching alone would have missed it, case/whitespace-insensitivity, and both graceful no-crash paths when either side is missing an email), guest matching by name as the fallback when there's no email overlap, a dedicated regression pin proving the old `ownBidderNumber` check's false-positive risk is gone, and correctly finding the right host attendee among multiple guests/attendees. Sanity-checked by reverting to the old `ownBidderNumber === bidder.id` check and confirming 3 tests failed - the two email-matching tests, and (confirming the false-positive risk was real, not just theoretical) the dead-code regression pin itself, which the old code actually matched incorrectly - then re-applied the fix. Test count: 573 → 586; updated the count everywhere it's asserted. Doesn't touch Apps Script. `tsc --noEmit` clean; full suite 586/586, `npm run build` clean.

- **Closed the last item on the original test-coverage-gap list: bidder check-in.** `Bidders.test.tsx` had 21 tests and none clicked "Check In" - the pending-payment confirm path, the real `/api/auction/check-in-with-bid` POST, its `bidValue: parseFloat(ticketPrice) || 35` fallback, and the failure/rollback path (all in `useBiddersState.ts`'s `handleCheckInAttempt`/`confirmPendingCheckIn`) were all unexercised. Writing these tests required first fixing the exact shallow-mock gap already logged for this file: `AlertDialogCancel`/`AlertDialogAction` were mocked as `({children}) => <button>{children}</button>`, dropping `onClick` entirely, which would have silently no-op'd the "No, Do Not Add" click needed to test the direct-check-in-without-receipt path - fixed to forward `onClick` through. Also made the `AuctionContext`/`ConfigContext` mocks overridable per-test (a `let ... ?? { ...shared default }` pattern, reset in a top-level `afterEach`) since none of the existing 8 mock bidders/attendees hit the one combination that opens the pending-payment dialog (not checked in, not proxy, with a real *pending* attendee record - the closest existing bidder, Jane Smith, is already checked in). Added 6 tests to `Bidders.test.tsx`: immediate check-in with no dialog when there's no pending attendee, the dialog opening instead of an immediate check-in when there is one, "No, Do Not Add" checking in directly without a network call, "Yes, Add to Receipt" posting the real payload (bidderId/spreadsheetId/bidManagementSheet/bidderNumbersSheet/bidValue/idempotencyKey), the `bidValue` fallback when `ticketPrice` doesn't parse, and the failure path showing an error toast while still refreshing the bidder list. Sanity-checked by reintroducing 2 real bugs into `useBiddersState.ts` and confirming the exact relevant tests failed: dropping the `|| 35` fallback (caught by the fallback test, `NaN` serializing to `null` in the request body) and disabling the pending-payment gate entirely (caught by the dialog-opens test). Test count: 586 → 592; updated the count everywhere it's asserted. Doesn't touch Apps Script. `tsc --noEmit` clean; full suite 592/592, `npm run build` clean. This closes every item originally listed on the test-coverage-gap punch list from this session's testing-gaps pass.

- **Closed the last "lower priority" test-coverage items (`useConfigSettingsState.extractId`, `useApiAuthStatus.ts`, `useGoogleAuth.ts`) and fixed 2 already-logged bugs discovered to be live along the way.** `extractId`'s 4 regex branches (Apps Script project URLs, `/d/` doc URLs, Drive `/folders/` URLs, `id=` query params) turned out to be genuinely correct - added `src/hooks/useConfigSettingsState.test.ts` (9 tests) pinning all 4 branches plus the raw-ID/no-match fallback and a case proving the `/d/` branch is checked before `id=` (a Sheets URL's own `#gid=0` fragment coincidentally contains the substring `"id="`). The other two hooks needed real fixes first. (1) **The already-logged missing `event.origin` check** in both `useApiAuthStatus.ts` and `useGoogleAuth.ts`'s `postMessage` listeners. Investigating *why* it had never been an issue in practice turned up something worth recording: there is no `postMessage` **sender** anywhere in the current codebase - `git log` traced it to a real, working sender (a server-side HTML page at `/auth/callback` that checked `window.name === 'oauth_popup'`, matching the popup name `useGoogleAuth.ts` still opens today, then called `window.opener.postMessage(...)`) that existed since the very first commit and was accidentally deleted during a `server.ts` refactor without updating either listener hook. Today, the iframe/popup login path silently degrades to relying entirely on `useGoogleAuth.ts`'s existing 5-second `/auth/status` fallback poll instead of an instant signal - functionally fine, just up to 5s slower - but the listeners themselves were still a live injection surface: with no origin check, any page holding a reference to this window could post a fake `{type: 'OAUTH_AUTH_SUCCESS', tokens: {...}}` and have the app blindly trust and persist it. Fixed both to reject any message whose `event.origin` isn't this app's own before reading `event.data` at all - correct and safe regardless of whether the deleted sender is ever restored, since a restored sender would legitimately target `window.location.origin` anyway. (2) **The already-logged `useGoogleAuth.ts` `setState`-updater bug**: the 5-second fallback poll called `checkAuth()` from *inside* a `setAuthenticated` updater function, which must be pure - React (especially StrictMode) can invoke updaters more than once, doubling `/auth/status` requests. Fixed by mirroring `authenticated` into a `useRef` the interval reads directly, preserving the original "only poll while not yet authenticated" behavior without the impure updater. Added `src/hooks/useApiAuthStatus.test.ts` (7 tests: mount status, failure-is-unauthenticated, same-origin `OAUTH_AUTH_SUCCESS` persisting tokens and re-checking, the origin-check regression guard, an unrelated-type message being ignored, and listener cleanup on unmount) and `src/hooks/useGoogleAuth.test.ts` (14 tests: mount/postMessage/origin-check/`_fallbackTokens` fallback, the 5-second poll's regression guard for the `setState`-updater fix, `handleLogin`'s non-iframe redirect and iframe/popup paths including the popup-blocked and no-URL-returned error toasts, and `handleLogout`). Sanity-checked all 3 fixes by reverting each independently and confirming the exact relevant test failed with the real symptom (an attacker-origin message writing tokens to `localStorage`, for both origin-check tests; the poll firing a second/third `/auth/status` call after already being authenticated, for the `setState`-updater fix) - all caught cleanly, then reverted. Test count: 592 → 622; updated the count everywhere it's asserted. Doesn't touch Apps Script. `tsc --noEmit` clean; full suite 622/622, `npm run build` clean. **Still open, deliberately out of scope here**: `useApiAuthStatus.ts` and `useGoogleAuth.ts` independently duplicating the same auth-status-plus-listener flow (a bigger consolidation, not a bug - see `refactor-opportunities.md`), and whether to restore the deleted `postMessage`-sending `/auth/callback` page (a product decision, not raised with the user in this pass).

Read the real payloads directly from `useCheckoutState.ts`'s `startBatchPrint`/`startBatchEmail` (`{ spreadsheetId, receiptFolderId: folderId }` and `{ spreadsheetId }` respectively) and strengthened both assertions to check the exact parsed body, not just the HTTP method.
