- **Closed the last SHALLOW-rated test, `ConfigSettings.test.tsx`, and discovered `extractId` is genuinely defense-in-depth, not single-point-of-failure.** The existing "clicking Save Configuration calls saveConfig" test only ever changed `ticketPrice`, leaving all 8 ID fields at their already-clean fixture values - `extractId`'s URL-parsing regex branches never actually fired through this component. Added a new test that pastes a real Google Sheets URL (`https://docs.google.com/spreadsheets/d/1AbC-XyZ_123/edit#gid=0`) into the "Bid Management Spreadsheet ID" field and asserts `saveConfig` receives the bare extracted ID, not the raw URL. Sanity-checking this turned up something worth knowing: `AuctionConfigurationCard.tsx`'s `onChange` handlers already call `extractId` on every keystroke, before the value even reaches `config` state - so `useConfigSettingsState.ts`'s `handleSave` calling `extractId` *again* on save is genuinely redundant, not the sole extraction point the original flagged issue assumed. Confirmed by testing all three combinations: breaking only the `onChange`-level extraction left the test passing (the `handleSave`-level one still caught it), breaking only the `handleSave`-level extraction also left it passing (the `onChange`-level one still caught it), and breaking *both* together correctly failed the test - proving this is real, working defense-in-depth on both ends of the flow, not a flaky assertion. Test count: 622 → 623; updated the count everywhere it's asserted. Doesn't touch Apps Script. `tsc --noEmit` clean; full suite 623/623, `npm run build` clean. This closes every SHALLOW/CIRCULAR/SMOKE-rated test and every test-coverage gap originally flagged in `refactor-opportunities.md`'s "Test coverage gaps" section.

- **Fixed `useBiddersData.ts`'s `checkInBidder`/`markBidderAsProxy` - the same whole-snapshot rollback bug already fixed in `useWinnersData.ts`, `useAttendeesData.ts`, and `useAuctionItemsTableState.ts` this session, now in a fifth file.** Both captured `previousBidders` (the entire array) from the render closure and restored it wholesale on failure, so checking in one bidder and marking a second as proxy in quick succession (before either re-renders) - a real scenario at a busy check-in table - could have the second's failure silently erase the first's already-committed, successfully-synced change. Fixed both to capture and restore only the one bidder's own field (`previousCheckedIn`/`previousIsProxy`) via a functional `setBidders` update, scoped by `id`. Added 2 regression tests to the existing `useBiddersData.test.ts` (which already had solid single-operation coverage, just nothing concurrent): two bidders checked in / marked proxy back-to-back, the first succeeds and commits, the second fails (not found in the sheet) - the first's state must survive the second's rollback. Sanity-checked by reverting each fix to a blunt `setBidders([])` and confirming the corresponding regression test failed with the real symptom, then re-applied. Test count: 623 → 625; updated the count everywhere it's asserted. Doesn't touch Apps Script. `tsc --noEmit` clean; full suite 625/625, `npm run build` clean. Still open: `useCheckoutState.ts` and `useTicketAttendeeState.ts`.

- **Fixed `useCheckoutState.ts`'s `confirmMarkPaid` - the same whole-snapshot rollback bug, now in a sixth file.** Captured `previousWinners` (the entire array) from the render closure and restored it wholesale on failure, so marking two winners paid in quick succession (before either re-renders) - a real scenario at a busy checkout table - could have the second-in-flight one's rollback erase the first's already-committed, successfully-synced payment too. Fixed by capturing and restoring only the one winner's own `paid`/`paymentPending` fields (`previousWinnerState`) via a functional `setWinners` update, scoped by `bidderId`. Added `src/hooks/useCheckoutState.test.ts` (2 tests) via `renderHook` - this hook is only ever consumed through `Checkout.tsx`, and `Checkout.test.tsx` mocks `useConfig`/`useAuction` wholesale, so none of its real logic had ever run under the suite before this: a single failed sync rolling back cleanly, and the concurrent-rollback regression guard (mark winner A paid, mark winner B paid while A's sync is still in flight, B's sync commits first, then A's sync fails - A's rollback must not touch B's already-committed paid status). Hit and fixed a real authoring bug while writing the first test: constructing the hook's `items` array as a fresh literal inside the `renderHook` callback (`items: [ITEM1, ITEM2]`) gave it a new identity every re-render, which kept re-triggering `loadWinners`'s mount effect forever ("Maximum update depth exceeded") - fixed by hoisting it to a stable `const ITEMS = [ITEM1, ITEM2]` outside the callback, the same discipline the `bidders` array already followed. Also had to get the concurrent test's operation order right: the actual bug only reproduces when the *first*-acted winner's own sync is the one that fails, since its rollback snapshot was captured before the second winner acted at all and so has no record of the second's later change - the reverse order (second-acted-winner's sync fails) doesn't expose the bug, because that winner's snapshot is captured after the first's optimistic update already landed. Sanity-checked by reverting to the old whole-snapshot version and confirming exactly the concurrent-rollback test failed with the real symptom (winner B's already-committed paid status wiped by winner A's unrelated rollback), then re-applied. Test count: 625 → 627; updated the count everywhere it's asserted. Doesn't touch Apps Script. `tsc --noEmit` clean; full suite 627/627, `npm run build` clean. Still open: `useTicketAttendeeState.ts`.

- **Fixed `useTicketAttendeeState.ts`'s `updateField`/`updatePaymentStatus` - the same whole-snapshot rollback bug, closing out the backlog item across all six affected files.** Both captured `previousAttendees` (the entire array) from the render closure and restored it wholesale on failure, so editing two attendees' fields (or payment statuses) in quick succession (before either re-renders) - a real scenario when reconciling a batch of tickets - could have the second-in-flight one's rollback erase the first's already-committed, successfully-synced edit too. Fixed by capturing and restoring only the one attendee's own prior state - `updateField` keeps a reference to the pre-update `attendee` object itself (cheaper than re-deriving just the touched field/guest slot, and equally correct since it's restored wholesale only for that one id), `updatePaymentStatus` captures just `previousPaymentStatus`- both applied via a functional `setAttendees` update scoped by `id`. Added `src/hooks/useTicketAttendeeState.test.ts` (4 tests) via `renderHook` - this hook is only ever consumed through `TicketAttendeeTab.tsx`, and `TicketAttendeeTab.test.tsx` mocks `useAttendeesData` wholesale, so none of `updateField`/`updatePaymentStatus`'s own logic had ever run under the suite before this: a single failed sync rolling back cleanly for each, and the concurrent-rollback regression guard for each (edit/update attendee A first, attendee B second while A's sync is still in flight, B's sync commits first, then A's sync fails - A's rollback must not touch B's already-committed change). Since this hook takes `attendees`/`setAttendees` as arguments rather than owning the list itself (the real state lives in the parent, via `useAttendeesData.ts`), the tests wire a plain mutable array + a `setAttendees` mock that applies the same updater function a real `useState` setter would, `rerender`-ing with the mutated array between steps to hand the hook a fresh closure - the same wiring problem `useAttendeesData.ts`'s own concurrent test already solved for its rollback fix. Sanity-checked both fixes independently by reverting each to the old whole-snapshot version and confirming exactly the corresponding concurrent-rollback test failed with the real symptom (the first-acted attendee's rollback wiping the second's already-committed edit/status), then re-applied. Test count: 627 → 631; updated the count everywhere it's asserted. Doesn't touch Apps Script. `tsc --noEmit` clean; full suite 631/631, `npm run build` clean. This closes the whole-snapshot-rollback backlog item - every file `refactor-opportunities.md` flagged (`useAuctionItemsTableState.ts`, `useWinnersData.ts`, `useAttendeesData.ts`, `useBiddersData.ts`, `useCheckoutState.ts`, `useTicketAttendeeState.ts`) now scopes its rollback to the one entity a failed sync actually touched.

- **Deleted `Class_ReceiptManager.gs`'s unreachable `createIndividualReceipt()`/`createAllReceipts()` prototype methods and the now-dead `UIManager` instance and UI strings they alone consumed.** Confirmed genuinely unreachable, not just an assumption: grepped every `.gs` file for both method names and for `new ReceiptManager`/`ReceiptManager(` - the only two call sites are `GoogleAIStudio.createAllReceipts`/`.createIndividualReceipt` (a same-named but different, parameterized, non-interactive pair of methods on a different class), and both of those construct their own `ReceiptManager` and call its lower-level methods (`buildReceipt_`, `processBatch_`, `getOrCreateInvoiceUrl`, `handleEmailDispatch_`) directly - never `mgr.createIndividualReceipt()`/`mgr.createAllReceipts()` themselves. These two prototype methods were the last leftover from the manual-menu-driven workflow already disabled in `menu.gs` (see the "Menu disabled" entry above) - UI-prompt-driven (`this.uiManager.promptOkCancel`/`.confirmYesNo`/`.showAlert`), not reachable from any menu item or API entry point. Deleting them also let `this.uiManager = new UIManager()` in the constructor go, since nothing else in the class read `this.uiManager` - confirmed via a targeted grep before removing it. Also removed the entire "── ReceiptManager ──" block of `Class_UIStrings.gs` (12 strings: `receiptGenerationTitle`, `enterBidderNumberPrompt`, `blankBidderNumberMsg`, `bidderNotFoundReceiptMsg`, `statusTitle`, `noBidsMsg`, `emailReceiptQuestion`, `receiptCreatedSuccessMsg`, `receiptErrorMsg`, `massEmailTitle`, `massEmailQuestion`, `batchReceiptsSuccessMsg`), confirmed via the same grep to have zero other consumers anywhere in the project - they existed solely for the two deleted methods. Left the file's separate "Generic / Reused" strings (`successTitle`/`errorTitle`/`invalidInputTitle`) and CheckOutManager's own already-unused `emailReceiptTitle` alone - both are pre-existing, unrelated to this cleanup, and out of scope. Updated the class's own docblock, which previously described these deleted methods as its "entry points for individual and batch receipt generation," to instead point at the real entry points (`GoogleAIStudio.createAllReceipts`/`.createIndividualReceipt`). No Node `vm`-harness test or native GAS test file references either deleted method or `this.uiManager` (confirmed via grep across `server/test/` and the project's own `*TEST*.gs`/`*Test*.gs` files) - nothing to update there. `node --check` on both edited files (via a temporary `.js` copy, since Node won't check a bare `.gs` extension) confirms valid syntax. Doesn't touch any TypeScript - full suite unaffected at 631/631, `npm run build` clean.

- **Fixed Checkout's refresh spinner getting stuck forever.** `handleRefresh` set `fetchingWinners(true)` but had no `finally` of its own, relying entirely on `loadWinners`'s `finally` to reset it - which never ran on two real paths: `loadWinners` early-returns at its own `if (!spreadsheetId) return;` before reaching its `try`/`finally` at all, and `fetchAllData()` (awaited first) can itself throw, which skips the call to `loadWinners` entirely. Either path left the "Refresh" button's spinner spinning forever with no way to recover short of a full page reload. Fixed by wrapping `handleRefresh`'s own `fetchAllData`/`loadWinners` calls in a `try/finally` that unconditionally resets `fetchingWinners`, regardless of which of the two ever actually runs or throws. Added 2 tests to `src/hooks/useCheckoutState.test.ts`: refreshing with no `spreadsheetId` configured (the early-return path) and refreshing when `fetchAllData` itself rejects (the throw-before-`loadWinners` path) - both assert `fetchingWinners` ends up `false`. Sanity-checked by reverting to the old bare `await fetchAllData(); await loadWinners();` and confirming both new tests failed with the real symptom (`fetchingWinners` stuck at `true`), then re-applied. Test count: 631 → 633; updated the count everywhere it's asserted. Doesn't touch Apps Script. `tsc --noEmit` clean; full suite 633/633, `npm run build` clean.

- **Fixed `WinnerCard`'s "Checkout" button's permanently-dead loading condition.** `loading={processingId === \`${winner.bidderId}-pay\`}` could never be true - confirmed by grep that `useCheckoutState.ts` only ever writes a `-email` or `-print` suffix to `processingId`, never `-pay`; the button never showed a spinner regardless of actual in-flight state. Root cause: `handleMarkPaid` doesn't do any async work itself - it just opens `PaymentMethodDialog` synchronously, and the real submission (`confirmMarkPaid`) closes that dialog immediately, before its background sync settles, tracking that in-flight window with its own `isSubmittingPayment`/`winnerToPay` state instead (already surfaced via the dialog's own spinner). So there's no in-flight gap on the WinnerCard button's own click to show a spinner for - but there IS a real, previously-unrepresented window right after: once the dialog closes, this button re-renders for a moment while the sync is still running, before `winner.paid`/`winner.paymentPending` update and this branch stops rendering the button at all. Wired the button to that real window instead of dropping it: added a `payingBidderId: string | null` prop, threaded through `WinnerGrid` from a new `Checkout.tsx` computation (`state.isSubmittingPayment ? state.winnerToPay?.bidderId ?? null : null` - the same two flags `PaymentMethodDialog` already reads), and swapped the dead `processingId === \`${bidderId}-pay\`` check for `payingBidderId === winner.bidderId`. Added `src/components/WinnerCard.test.tsx` (4 tests, this component had zero coverage of its own before this - only exercised indirectly and shallowly through `Checkout.test.tsx`): idle state renders "Checkout" and isn't disabled, the paying winner's button shows "Processing..." and is disabled, a *different* winner's button stays idle while one is paying (confirms the prop is scoped by bidder id, not a single shared boolean), and a `-pay`-suffixed `processingId` no longer renders the loading state (the legitimate `isProcessingThisWinner` cross-action lock still disables the button for that case, which is real and unrelated - only the dead loading condition itself is gone). Needed an explicit `import "@testing-library/jest-dom"` (the shared `server/test/setup.ts` doesn't register it globally - every other component test file that uses jest-dom matchers imports it locally too). Sanity-checked by reverting to the old `loading={processingId === ...-pay}`/`disabled={isProcessingThisWinner}` pair and confirming the "shows loading when paying" test failed with the real symptom (button stuck as static "Checkout"), then re-applied. Test count: 633 → 637; updated the count everywhere it's asserted. Doesn't touch Apps Script. `tsc --noEmit` clean; full suite 637/637, `npm run build` clean.

- **Fixed new staff/sponsor attendee entries writing payment fields to the wrong columns.** Resolved the "which of the two is correct" question the flagged issue left open by investigating both sides directly: `src/lib/attendeeParsing.ts`'s hardcoded `Comp`/`Paid`/`$0` for staff/sponsor rows turned out to be a deliberate business rule, not a bug - a comment right there in its own test file says staff/sponsors get free tickets and their sheets don't carry real payment data. So the write was the actual bug: `handleAddSubmit` wrote `row[16] = "Paid"` (commented "Payment Status") and `row[17] = "$0"` (commented "Total Paid"), but the real column layout for non-general sheets - confirmed two independent ways: `parseAttendeeRows`' own unshifted `pm=row[16], ps=row[17], tp=row[18]` variables, and separately `bidderService.ts`'s `updateAttendeeField` fieldMapping, which maps `paymentStatus` to column R (index 17) for non-"tickets" sheets - is Payment Method (Q/16), Payment Status (R/17), Total Paid (S/18), one column later than the write assumed. Fixed to write all three columns with values matching the parser's own hardcoded ones (`Comp`/`Paid`/`$0`), so the raw sheet stays consistent with what the app displays even though `parseAttendeeRows` doesn't read these columns back for staff/sponsor rows today - worth having correct regardless, for anyone opening the raw sheet directly or if the hardcoding is ever revisited. Updated the two existing `TicketAttendeeTab.test.tsx` tests that had pinned the old, wrong column indices/values (`row[16]`/`row[17]` as Paid/$0) to assert the corrected `row[16]`/`row[17]`/`row[18]` as Comp/Paid/$0 instead. Sanity-checked by reverting to the old two-column write and confirming both updated tests failed with the real symptom (`row[16]` was `'Paid'`, expected `'Comp'`), then re-applied. No test count change (existing tests corrected in place, no new ones added). Doesn't touch Apps Script. `tsc --noEmit` clean; full suite 637/637, `npm run build` clean.

- **Closed the last flagged correctness bug: added test coverage and a real defensive-parsing fix to `server/services/emailService.ts:processEmail`, which had zero tests of any kind.** Investigation narrowed the "no defensive parsing" flag to what was actually reachable: the triple-sheet reads themselves already propagate cleanly to callers with their own try/catch (`receiptRoutes.ts`'s `/email-only` route, `receiptService.ts`'s per-bidder `processAllEmails` loop - see that file's own earlier test/fix), so a thrown Sheets API error was never the real gap. The real gap was in the Bidder-Numbers row read: `firstName`/`lastName`/`email`/`invoiceUrl` were assigned directly from row cells with no fallback, so a malformed row (any of those cells missing or `null`/`undefined` rather than an empty string) would bake the literal string `"undefined"` or `"null"` into the sent email's subject line and greeting (`Receipt for undefined undefined`) instead of degrading to a blank name - genuinely bidder-facing, not just an internal log line. Fixed with an `|| ''` fallback on all four fields. Added `server/test/emailService.test.ts` (5 tests, `http.post` override on the Gmail-send MSW handler to capture and base64-decode the actual sent email for the fallback test, since a bare `{success: true}` check wouldn't have caught the "undefined"-in-subject bug at all): the happy path with real won items, the no-email-on-file/bidder-not-found-in-matrix/no-items-won clean-error paths (all pre-existing behavior, newly pinned), and the malformed-row fallback itself - asserting the decoded sent email contains neither `"undefined"` nor `"null"`. Sanity-checked by reverting the `|| ''` fallback and confirming exactly the fallback test failed with the real symptom (decoded email contained `"null null"` in its subject/greeting - MSW's JSON serialization turns array `undefined` slots into `null`), then re-applied. Test count: 637 → 642; updated the count everywhere it's asserted. Doesn't touch Apps Script. `tsc --noEmit` clean; full suite 642/642, `npm run build` clean. This closes every item in the 2026-09-02 deep audit's "Correctness bugs" section - none remain open.

- **Extracted the ~200 duplicated lines shared by `DocumentCreationTab.tsx` and `TicketDocumentCreationTab.tsx`** into three new, reusable pieces: `src/hooks/useForPrintFolderFiles.ts` (the folder-files fetch/refresh/invalid-folder-check logic, previously hand-copied verbatim in both files' `fetchFiles`), `src/components/FolderFilesCard.tsx` (the entire "For Print Folder Contents" card - refresh button, loading/invalid/empty states, and the files table - byte-for-byte identical between the two originals), and `src/components/DocumentCreationPanel.tsx` (the "Creation Tools" card chrome: icon/title, description, closed-auction note, and "documents go to your For Print folder" footer, wrapping each consumer's own distinct button list as `children`). Matches the same pattern `SpecialWinnerField.tsx` already established for the analogous Dashboard duplication - plain presentational components/hook with no dedicated test file of their own, verified instead through the two existing consumer test suites. Each consumer keeps its own `runScript` (the actual per-tab difference: item-entry's 8-endpoint map vs. tickets' single bidder-labels endpoint and extra `bidManagementSpreadsheetId` validation) and its own button list, passed as `DocumentCreationPanel`'s children - nothing about the generation flow itself changed, only the wrapping chrome and file-fetch plumbing moved. `DocumentCreationTab.tsx` shrank from 281 to 145 lines and `TicketDocumentCreationTab.tsx` from 207 to 72, with the three new shared files totaling 202 lines - net reduction of ~69 lines, and the duplicated logic now lives in exactly one place. Ran both existing test suites (`DocumentCreationTab.test.tsx`'s 15 tests, `TicketDocumentCreationTab.test.tsx`'s 6) against the refactored components unchanged - all 21 passed without a single assertion edit, confirming behavior parity (rendered text, button disabled states, POST payloads, toast messages, and the closed-auction note are all pixel-for-pixel the same). Standardized one small, inconsequential cosmetic inconsistency the copy-paste had introduced: `TicketDocumentCreationTab.tsx`'s closed-auction note was missing the `mt-4` top-margin class `DocumentCreationTab.tsx`'s otherwise-identical note had - now both get it via the shared component. No test count change (existing tests transferred as-is, no new ones needed for pure presentational extraction already covered by the consumers). Doesn't touch Apps Script. `tsc --noEmit` clean; full suite 642/642, `npm run build` clean (bundle size dropped slightly, consistent with removing duplicated code). This closes the first item in `refactor-opportunities.md`'s "Redundant/duplicated logic" section.

- **Replaced the 27 hand-copied `if (!req.session?.tokens) return res.status(401).json({ error: "Unauthorized" });` guards with a single `server/middleware/requireAuth.ts`, applied via `router.use(requireAuth)` at the top of each of the six routers where every route requires auth** (`setupRoutes.ts`, `bundleRoutes.ts`, `dataRoutes.ts`, `receiptRoutes.ts`, `bidRoutes.ts`, and `productionRoutes.ts`'s single shared `handleAppsScriptCall` helper, which already deduplicated its own 9 routes down to 1 copy). `authRoutes.ts`'s pre-auth routes (`/auth/url`, `/auth/callback`, `/auth/status`, `/auth/logout`) correctly don't get this middleware. Confirmed every route in all six files genuinely required auth (no public route accidentally getting swept in) before applying the router-wide guard, by counting route registrations against guard occurrences per file first. Added 4 unit tests to `server/test/middleware.test.ts` for `requireAuth` itself, matching the existing convention there for `cacheControl`/`cookiePolicy`/`tokenRestorer`/`errorHandler`. While verifying the refactor, discovered and pinned a real, load-bearing subtlety with a dedicated `server/test/requireAuth.integration.test.ts` (6 tests, one router mounted alone per test): Express's `router.use(middleware)` with no path applies to *every* request that enters that router, regardless of whether a specific route inside it matches - and since `app.ts` mounts both `bidRoutes.ts` and `dataRoutes.ts` at the same `/api` base path with `bidRoutes.ts` first, `bidRoutes.ts`'s guard was found (while sanity-checking this fix) to silently intercept and mask requests actually destined for `dataRoutes.ts`. That meant `routes.test.ts`'s existing `/api/sheets/:spreadsheetId/:range` 401 test kept passing even after deliberately deleting `dataRoutes.ts`'s own `router.use(requireAuth)` line entirely - `bidRoutes.ts` returned 401 first either way, so the existing integration test alone couldn't have caught a future accidental removal of `dataRoutes.ts`'s own guard. The new isolated-mount tests remove that masking and pin each router's own guard directly (verified each one really is load-bearing: temporarily stripped `router.use(requireAuth)` from all six files at once and confirmed all 6 isolated tests failed with the real symptom - 500s from `req.session.tokens` being accessed on a session with no tokens, or a 400 from a later check running unauthenticated - then restored). Sanity-checked the `requireAuth` unit tests themselves too, by temporarily reducing the middleware to a bare `next()` and confirming exactly the 3 rejection-path tests failed, then restored. Test count: 642 → 652 (320 → 330 backend); updated the count everywhere it's asserted. Ran the full existing suite (not just the new tests) against the refactored routers unchanged - all 320 pre-existing backend tests passed with zero assertion edits, confirming behavior parity. Doesn't touch Apps Script. `tsc --noEmit` clean; full suite 652/652, `npm run build` clean (server bundle shrank ~2kb, consistent with removing duplicated code). This closes the last item in `refactor-opportunities.md`'s "Redundant/duplicated logic" section that didn't need a design decision first.

- **Had `useGoogleAuth.ts` build on `useApiAuthStatus.ts` instead of hand-copying its auth-status-check + `OAUTH_AUTH_SUCCESS` postMessage-listener logic, per the flagged issue's own prescribed fix.** Moved the token-persist step into a shared `persistTokens` helper inside `useApiAuthStatus.ts`, merging in the one thing it was missing that `useGoogleAuth.ts` had: the `_fallbackTokens` escape hatch for when `localStorage.setItem` throws (e.g. a blocked cross-origin iframe) - this is a real behavioral improvement for `useApiAuthStatus.ts`'s own existing consumer (`AuctionContext.tsx`), not just a code move: previously, a thrown `localStorage.setItem` there was uncaught and skipped the following `checkProviderAuth()` call entirely, so a real login could go unnoticed until the next unrelated re-render. Exposed `checkProviderAuth` from `useApiAuthStatus.ts`'s return value (changing its shape from a bare `boolean | null` to `{ isApiAuthenticated, checkProviderAuth }`) so `useGoogleAuth.ts` can call it directly - from its own 5-second fallback poll (unchanged: still only polls while not yet authenticated, still reads a ref mirror rather than the state directly, for the same StrictMode-safety reason as before) and from `handleLogout` (`await logout(); await checkProviderAuth();`, replacing its own local `checkAuth`). `useGoogleAuth.ts` itself now only owns `handleLogin`/`handleLogout` and the poll - the mount-time status check and postMessage listener are gone from it entirely, sourced from `useApiAuthStatus.ts` alone. Updated `AuctionContext.tsx`'s one call site for the new return shape (`const { isApiAuthenticated } = useApiAuthStatus();`) and `useApiAuthStatus.test.ts`'s 7 pre-existing assertions to read `result.current.isApiAuthenticated` instead of the bare `result.current`. Deliberately did NOT change any of `useGoogleAuth.test.ts`'s 14 tests - they exercise the hook's public behavior only (`result.current.authenticated`, `handleLogin`, `handleLogout`, the postMessage/fallback-token/polling behavior), and all 14 passed completely unedited against the refactored implementation, which is the real proof this is a behavior-preserving consolidation rather than a rewrite. Sanity-checked by temporarily dropping the `_fallbackTokens` line from the shared `persistTokens` helper and confirming `useGoogleAuth.test.ts`'s own "falls back to window._fallbackTokens..." test failed with the real symptom (a second, unrelated test failure in the same run turned out to be pre-existing test-order pollution - `Storage.prototype.setItem`'s override not getting restored because the first test's assertion threw before reaching its own cleanup line - confirmed by re-running both files together with the fix restored, where both pass cleanly), then restored. One thing this does *not* fix, worth being explicit about: `main.tsx` mounts `AuctionProvider` around `<App />`, so `useApiAuthStatus.ts` (via `AuctionContext.tsx`) and `useGoogleAuth.ts` (via `App.tsx`) are still two separate live hook instances with no shared state - every OAuth completion still fires two `/auth/status` requests, since code-sharing alone can't collapse two independent component-subtree mounts into one network call. That would need lifting auth-status state into one shared context both read from, a real architectural change - split out as its own, still-open `refactor-opportunities.md` item rather than silently dropped. No test count change (existing tests updated/verified in place, no new ones needed - both hooks already had solid coverage from earlier this session). Doesn't touch Apps Script. `tsc --noEmit` clean; full suite 652/652, `npm run build` clean.

- **Closed the follow-up split out from the previous entry: actually deduplicated the two independent `/auth/status` requests per OAuth completion, not just the duplicated source code.** Added `src/context/AuthContext.tsx` (`AuthProvider`/`useAuth`), a thin wrapper mounting exactly one instance of `useGoogleAuth.ts` (itself built on `useApiAuthStatus.ts`, per the prior entry) and sharing it via React context. Mounted `<AuthProvider>` once in `main.tsx`, positioned around both `<AuctionProvider>` and `<App />` (`ConfigProvider > AuthProvider > AuctionProvider > App`) so both can read the same live instance. `App.tsx` (the login gate) now calls `useAuth()` instead of `useGoogleAuth()` directly; `AuctionContext.tsx` now calls `useAuth()` instead of `useApiAuthStatus()` directly (`const { authenticated: isApiAuthenticated } = useAuth();` - kept the existing local variable name so nothing downstream in that file needed to change). `useApiAuthStatus.ts` and `useGoogleAuth.ts` themselves are unchanged - they're still real, independently-testable hooks; only *who calls them, and how many times* changed, from two call sites (`App.tsx` and `AuctionContext.tsx`) down to one (`AuthProvider`). Added `src/context/AuthContext.test.tsx` (4 tests, mocking `useGoogleAuth.ts` itself since its own real behavior is already covered by `useGoogleAuth.test.ts` - this file only pins the Provider/`useAuth` wiring): `useAuth()` throws a clear error outside a Provider, the hook's return value passes through unchanged, `authenticated: null`/`false`/`true` all reflect correctly across a rerender, and - the test that actually matters for this fix - two separate consumer components under one `AuthProvider` see the identical `authenticated` value while the underlying `useGoogleAuth` mock is called exactly once, not once per consumer. Sanity-checked both non-trivial assertions by deliberately breaking them: removed the `useAuth()` throw and confirmed the "throws outside Provider" test failed; then simulated the exact regression this whole fix targets - `useAuth()` calling `useGoogleAuth()` directly instead of reading from context (i.e. what the code looked like before this session) - and confirmed the "single shared instance" test failed with `useGoogleAuth` called 3 times instead of 1, then reverted both. `tsc --noEmit` clean; ran the full existing suite (not just the new file) against the rewired `App.tsx`/`AuctionContext.tsx` - all 322 pre-existing frontend tests passed unchanged, since every consumer test already mocks `@/context/AuctionContext` (or `@/context/AuthContext` transitively) wholesale rather than rendering the real providers, so none of them exercise this wiring directly. Could not do a live-browser smoke test of the real login-gate flow - `npm run dev`'s port was already held by an unrelated process on this machine, and killing an unrelated process wasn't warranted for a check the automated suite already covers; relied on the full test suite, a clean `tsc --noEmit`, and a manual line-by-line read of the final provider nesting in `main.tsx` instead. Test count: 652 → 656; updated the count everywhere it's asserted. Doesn't touch Apps Script. `tsc --noEmit` clean; full suite 656/656, `npm run build` clean. This closes the item split out from the previous entry - `refactor-opportunities.md`'s "Redundant/duplicated logic" section now has no auth-related items left.

- **Collapsed `useBiddersData.ts`'s `checkInBidder` and `markBidderAsProxy` - identical apart from one field name, one sheet column, and their toast text - into a single `setBidderFlag(bidder, field, column, successMessage)` helper.** Both did the same optimistic-update/background-sync/scoped-rollback flow (this hook's rollback was already scoped-per-bidder from an earlier fix this session, not the whole-array bug); `checkInBidder` and `markBidderAsProxy` are now 2-line wrappers calling `setBidderFlag` with `('checkedIn', 'E', ...)` and `('isProxy', 'F', ...)` respectively. Also unified a small, purely-internal inconsistency the duplication had let drift: `checkInBidder`'s not-found error said "Bidder not found in management sheet.", `markBidderAsProxy`'s said "Bidder not found in sheet." - confirmed neither message is ever actually shown to the user (both are caught and replaced with the generic "Failed to sync updates—retrying..." toast; the thrown message only controls which `.then()` gets skipped), so standardizing on one wording changes nothing user-visible. Ran the existing `useBiddersData.test.ts` (9 tests, already covered both mutations' happy/rollback/concurrent-rollback paths from earlier session fixes) against the refactored implementation unchanged - all 9 passed with zero assertion edits, confirming behavior parity. Sanity-checked the collapse itself (the actual risk in merging two near-identical blocks: a copy-paste slip swapping which field/column goes with which caller) by deliberately swapping `checkInBidder`'s and `markBidderAsProxy`'s column arguments (and their field arguments) and confirming both of the two column-write assertions failed with the real symptom (`checkInBidder` writing column F instead of E, `markBidderAsProxy` writing E instead of F), then reverted. `src/hooks/useBiddersData.ts` shrank from 96 to 80 lines. No test count change (existing tests exercise both mutations already; a shared-helper refactor doesn't need new ones). Doesn't touch Apps Script. `tsc --noEmit` clean; full suite 656/656, `npm run build` clean (bundle size dropped slightly). This closes the first item in `refactor-opportunities.md`'s "Redundant/duplicated logic" section.

- **Standardized the optimistic-rollback error-toast wording that had drifted into three variants, and corrected all of them - not just merged the wrong two.** Added `src/lib/toastMessages.ts`'s `ROLLBACK_SYNC_ERROR_MESSAGE` ("Failed to sync updates. Your local change has been reverted.") and switched all 11 genuine optimistic-rollback call sites to it: `useBiddersState.ts`, `useCheckoutState.ts`, `useAuctionItemsTableState.ts` (×2), `useAttendeesData.ts` (×2), `useBiddersData.ts`, `useWinnersData.ts`, and `useTicketAttendeeState.ts` (×2, previously the odd "...rolling back..." variant). The new wording deliberately drops "retrying" from both merged variants, not just picks one - confirmed nothing anywhere in this app actually retries a failed sync automatically; the user has to manually redo the action, so the old "retrying..." claim was inaccurate everywhere it appeared, not only in the one case the flagged issue called out. That one case - `useBundling.ts`'s `executeBundle` - reused the same "local state reverted" wording despite having no optimistic update to revert at all (the bundle form only closes on success); rather than folding it into the shared constant it doesn't belong to, gave it its own accurate message: `toast.error(e.message)`, since the thrown error is already a complete, self-describing message (either the server's real error or the `"Failed to bundle items (status N)"` fallback) - no generic prefix needed, unlike this file's other three catches, whose thrown messages are bare network/JSON errors with no context of their own. Updated the 5 test assertions that pinned the old wording verbatim (`Bidders.test.tsx`, `useAttendeesData.test.ts` ×2, `useBiddersData.test.ts`, `useWinnersData.test.ts`) to the new constant text, and `useBundling.test.ts`'s one assertion to the corrected, distinct message. Sanity-checked the shared constant is genuinely wired through (not just present but unused) by temporarily changing its text to an unrelated string and confirming all 5 of those direct-assertion tests failed with the real symptom, then reverted. Ran the full existing suite for every touched hook (`useBiddersState`/`useCheckoutState`/`useAuctionItemsTableState`/`useTicketAttendeeState` have no toast-text assertions of their own but were re-run anyway to confirm nothing else broke) - all passed. No test count change (existing tests updated in place, no new ones needed - this is a wording fix, not new behavior). Doesn't touch Apps Script. `tsc --noEmit` clean; full suite 656/656, `npm run build` clean. This closes the last item in `refactor-opportunities.md`'s "Redundant/duplicated logic" section.

- **Fixed `Class_AuctionItem.gs`'s constructor: no length guard meant a genuinely short `rowData` row silently broke its own display-fallback logic.** `this.bundleID = rowData[10]`-style direct indexing assumed all 12 columns are always present, but the Sheets API omits trailing empty cells from a row - a row with no bundle/timestamp data (or any other blank trailing columns) can arrive shorter than 12 elements, leaving those fields `undefined` instead of `''`. That silently broke `getDisplayValue()`/`getDisplayOpeningBid()`/`getDisplayMinRaise()`'s `=== ''` fallback checks (returning `undefined` instead of falling back to `'500'`/`'5'`/`'5'`) and `hasOpeningBid()`'s `!== ''` check (reading a missing bid as "set" instead of "not set"). Fixed with a small `cell(i)` helper in the constructor that defaults any `undefined`/`null` cell to `''`, matching the `row[idx]?.toString() || ""` defaulting pattern already established in this codebase's Node-side parsers (e.g. `src/lib/itemParsing.ts`). Added a regression test in both places this class is tested: `AuctionItemManager_UnitTests.gs` (the native GAS suite, registered in `runItemsSpreadsheetUnitTests()`'s runner) and `server/test/gs-items-auction-item-manager.test.ts` (the Node `vm`-harness suite that loads this exact source file) - both construct `new AuctionItem(['101'])`, a single-element row, and assert every trailing field defaults to `''` (not `undefined`) and all three display-fallback methods plus `hasOpeningBid()` still behave correctly. Sanity-checked by reverting the constructor to its old direct-indexing form and confirming the Node harness's new test failed with the real symptom (`category` was `undefined`, expected `''`), then re-applied. Confirmed both edited `.gs` files still parse (`node --check` via a temporary `.js` copy, since Node won't check a bare `.gs` extension). Test count: 656 → 657 (330 → 331 backend); updated the count everywhere it's asserted. `tsc --noEmit` clean; full suite 657/657, `npm run build` clean. This closes the last item in `refactor-opportunities.md`'s "Redundant/duplicated logic" section - nothing remains open there.

- **Gave `useAttendeesData.ts` its own `loading` state, so `TicketAttendeeTab.tsx`'s Refresh button and table spinner finally reflect the actual attendee fetch.** Previously this hook tracked no loading state of its own at all - `TicketAttendeeTab.tsx` was wired to `useBiddersData.ts`'s `loading` from `AuctionContext` instead (that flag exists specifically to gate the app's *initial-load* spinner and reflects the bidders fetch, per its own docblock), so refreshing just the attendee list never showed a spinner unless a bidders fetch happened to be in flight at the same moment. Added a standard `loading` `useState` to `useAttendeesData.ts`, set around `fetchAttendees` exactly like its sibling `useBiddersData.ts` does. Exposed it through `AuctionContext.tsx` as a **new, separate** `attendeesLoading` field rather than repurposing the existing `loading` field - `Bidders.tsx`, `Checkout.tsx`, and `Items.tsx` all still read `loading` for its original bidders-fetch meaning, so overloading it would have silently changed their behavior. `TicketAttendeeTab.tsx`'s Refresh button and `AttendeesTable`'s `loading` prop now read `attendeesLoading` instead. Added 2 tests to `useAttendeesData.test.ts`: `loading` goes `false` → `true` → `false` across a real fetch (using a deferred promise to observe the in-flight state, the same pattern established for `useCheckoutState.ts`'s equivalent tests earlier this session), and the no-`ticketsSpreadsheetId` early-return never sets `loading` at all. Updated `TicketAttendeeTab.test.tsx`'s `useAuction()` mock to include `attendeesLoading: false` alongside the existing `loading: false`. Sanity-checked by reverting the `loading` state entirely and confirming both new tests failed with the real symptom (`result.current.loading` was `undefined`), then re-applied. Test count: 657 → 659 (326 → 328 frontend; backend unchanged at 331); updated the count everywhere it's asserted. Doesn't touch Apps Script. `tsc --noEmit` clean; full suite 659/659, `npm run build` clean. This closes the first item in `refactor-opportunities.md`'s "Code quality" section.

- **Split `useCheckoutState.ts` (416 lines) and `useAuctionItemsTableState.ts` (437 lines) - both past this codebase's own "over 400 is almost certainly doing too much" line - into the same container-hook-split pattern already applied to every other oversized file in this app (see `AuctionContext.tsx`).** `useCheckoutState.ts` became a 105-line container composing `useWinnersList.ts` (loading/parsing/filtering the winners sheet, search/tab state, refresh), `useCheckoutReceiptActions.ts` (per-winner email/print), `useCheckoutBatchActions.ts` (batch print/email + their confirm dialogs), and `useCheckoutPaymentFlow.ts` (the high-value-alert gate, payment dialog, and the mark-paid optimistic-update/rollback mutation). `useAuctionItemsTableState.ts` became an 85-line container composing `useAuctionItemsFilterSort.ts` (filter/sort/search + row selection), `useAuctionItemUpdate.ts` (single-cell edits, including the value-edit auto-bid side effects), `useAuctionItemsBulkEdit.ts` (bulk category/type edits), `useAddAuctionItem.ts`, and `useDeleteAuctionItem.ts`. Both containers keep their exact original external return shape, so `Checkout.tsx`/`AuctionItemsTab.tsx` and every component reading `WinnerRecord` (`PaymentMethodDialog.tsx`, `WinnerGrid.tsx`, `WinnerCard.tsx`) needed zero changes - `WinnerRecord` still lives in and is exported from `useCheckoutState.ts`, with the split-out files importing it back via `import type` (a type-only import, so no runtime circular-dependency issue despite the container importing *from* the pieces that import *from* the container). Each split necessarily introduces new cross-hook wiring where one piece needs another's state - `useCheckoutPaymentFlow` needs `useWinnersList`'s `setWinners`/`specialWinners`, `useAuctionItemsBulkEdit` needs `useAuctionItemsFilterSort`'s `selectedRows`/`clearSelection` - the same one-hook's-setter-passed-into-another composition `AuctionContext.tsx` already uses for its own child hooks. Ran every existing consumer test suite (`useCheckoutState.test.ts`, `Checkout.test.tsx`, `AuctionItemsTab.test.tsx`, `AuctionItemsTab.bundling.test.tsx` - 32 tests total) against both refactored containers unchanged - all passed with zero assertion edits, confirming behavior parity. Sanity-checked both new wiring points are genuinely load-bearing by temporarily severing each (a bare `() => {}`/`{}` in place of the real setter/state) and confirming a test failed with the real symptom, then restored: `useCheckoutState.ts`'s existing concurrent-rollback test caught the `setWinners` break immediately, but severing `useAuctionItemsBulkEdit`'s `selectedRows`/`clearSelection` wiring exposed a genuine, pre-existing test-coverage gap - neither `AuctionItemsTab.test.tsx` nor the bundling test file exercises the bulk-edit flow at all, so both kept passing against the broken wiring. Closed that gap directly rather than leave the new coupling unverified: added `src/hooks/useAuctionItemsTableState.test.ts` (1 focused test, not a full hook suite) asserting the real selected row indices reach the bulk-update request and get cleared on success - confirmed it fails the same way against the same deliberately-broken wiring, then restored the fix and reconfirmed it passes. `useCheckoutState.ts` shrank 415 → 105 lines (4 new files, 105-176 lines each); `useAuctionItemsTableState.ts` shrank 477 → 85 lines (5 new files, 50-142 lines each) - every file involved is now well under the 400-line guideline. Updated `refactor-opportunities.md`'s stale `useCheckoutState.ts:117` line reference (an unrelated already-logged `any`-typing item) to its new location, `useWinnersList.ts:38`, since the code it points at moved. Test count: 659 → 660 (328 → 329 frontend; backend unchanged at 331); updated the count everywhere it's asserted. Doesn't touch Apps Script. `tsc --noEmit` clean; full suite 660/660, `npm run build` clean. This closes the second item in `refactor-opportunities.md`'s "Code quality" section.

- **Narrowed the 5 flagged `any` types to concrete shapes.** `useAttendeesData.ts`'s `addAttendee(sheetName, rowData: any[])` → `rowData: string[]` (its one real call site, `useTicketAttendeeState.ts`'s `handleAddSubmit`, already builds and passes a genuine `string[]`; `AuctionContext.tsx`'s `AuctionContextType.addAttendee` interface field updated to match). `useAuctionItems.ts`'s two `.map((row: any[]) => ...)` callbacks (fetching Value-Calculations and Categories tiers) → `row: string[]`, matching the `string[][]` convention every other sheet-row parser in this codebase already uses (`itemParsing.ts`, `attendeeParsing.ts`, `bidGridParsing.ts`) - also dropped the now-inferrable `: any` annotations on their `.filter()`/`.map()` callback params (`t`/`c`), letting TS infer them from the narrowed `.map()`'s own return type instead. `useWinnersList.ts`'s Special-Winners row parse (`row: any`, `name: row[0]` with no fallback despite being typed `string`) → `row: string[]` with an explicit `row[0]?.toString() || ""` fallback, matching `bidderId`'s existing pattern on the very next line - investigated whether this was a live, user-visible bug (a blank/missing name cell producing the literal string `"undefined"` in the high-value-alert toast) before claiming one: it isn't - `useCheckoutPaymentFlow.ts`'s only consumer joins these names via `.join(" and ")`, which already coerces a genuinely `undefined` array element to an empty string, so there's no reachable behavior difference through today's one call site. Kept the fix anyway (type honesty, defense-in-depth for any future consumer that reads `.name` directly instead of through `.join()`), but did not write a test claiming to catch a bug that provably isn't there - a first draft of exactly that test was caught failing to fail against the reverted code during sanity-checking, which is what surfaced the `.join()` behavior in the first place. `useBiddersState.ts`'s sort comparator (`valA`/`valB` typed `any`) - this one *is* a real (if currently unreachable) gap: `handleSort`/`sortConfig.key` accepted any `string`, and the comparator's `if/else if` chain only handles `"id"`/`"name"`/`"status"`, silently leaving `valA`/`valB` `undefined` (sorting nothing) for any other key with no compiler signal. Added a `type BidderSortKey = "id" | "name" | "status"` and used it for both `sortConfig.key` and `handleSort`'s parameter, which lets `valA`/`valB` be typed `string | number` instead of `any` and makes the "only these three keys are ever handled" invariant checked at compile time instead of merely true by inspection (confirmed by writing a throwaway call site passing an invalid key string and watching `tsc` reject it, then removing that file - `BiddersTable.tsx`'s three `SortableHead` columns are still the only real callers, and they already only ever pass these three keys, so this doesn't change any runtime behavior either). Ran the full existing suite for every touched file (`useAttendeesData.test.ts`, `Bidders.test.tsx`, `useCheckoutState.test.ts`, `AuctionItemsTab.test.tsx`) - all passed unedited, confirming these are genuinely non-behavioral narrowings apart from the two real gaps described above (neither of which was reachable through any existing test, by design - one is provably unreachable today, the other has no live symptom to reproduce). No test count change. Doesn't touch Apps Script. `tsc --noEmit` clean; full suite 660/660, `npm run build` clean. This closes the third item in `refactor-opportunities.md`'s "Code quality" section.

- Added `src/components/FilterToolbar.tsx`, a shared search/filter bar (`searchValue`/`onSearchChange`/`searchPlaceholder`, optional `filters` and `trailing` slots) consolidating the near-identical hand-rolled search bars in `Items.tsx`, `Checkout.tsx`, and `Bidders.tsx`. Adopted in all three; `AuctionItemsTab`'s distinct labeled-field variant (`AuctionItemsFilterBar.tsx`) was left as-is since its layout genuinely differs. All 32 tests across the three affected files pass unmodified (including `Bidders.test.tsx`'s exact-select-order assertions); full suite 321/321, `npm run build` clean.
- Added `src/components/DataTableWrapper.tsx`, a shared scrollable table shell (consistent header styling, animated skeleton rows while loading, a stylized empty-state row) with `header`/`renderRow` render props for the genuinely per-screen parts (column widths, sort-click handlers, row content) and a `wrapItemRows` hook for row-animation wrappers like `AnimatePresence`. Adopted in `Items.tsx`, `Bidders.tsx`, and `Receipts.tsx`. `Checkout.tsx` was dropped from this item's scope - a previous audit claimed it needed this too, but it turns out to render its winners list as a responsive card grid, not a `<Table>` at all; that claim was never independently re-verified until now. `Receipts.tsx`'s loading/empty states were normalized from a one-off "replace the whole card" treatment (centered spinner text, dashed-border box) to the same skeleton-rows/empty-row-in-table pattern as the other two, and its `RefreshCw` spin class was switched from a manual template literal to `cn()` while in there. All tests pass unmodified; full suite 321/321, `npm run build` clean.
- Added `npm run test:quiet` (`NODE_OPTIONS=--no-experimental-webstorage vitest run`) to suppress Node's own `ExperimentalWarning: localStorage is not available...` noise during test runs - a built-in Node 22+ Web Storage feature unrelated to and not used by jsdom's `window.localStorage`, which is what the tests actually run against. Deliberately a separate script from `test` rather than a change to it: that Node flag doesn't exist on older Node versions and would hard-error there instead of just warning, so it isn't safe to assume for AI Studio's own Node runtime.
- Added `src/components/ui/StatusStatePlaceholder.tsx`, a shared "nothing to show" block (centered muted icon, optional heading, description, optional action, optional dashed-border treatment) - no Card wrapper of its own, composed inside `<Card>`/`<CardContent>` the same way the rest of the app composes Card content. Adopted in `AuctionItemsTab.tsx` ("Not Configured"), `Receipts.tsx` ("No Folder Configured"), `Checkout.tsx` (`EmptyWinnersState`, ×2 usages), and `DocumentCreationTab.tsx`/`TicketDocumentCreationTab.tsx` (folder-not-configured and no-files-found states, ×2 each - these two files are near-exact duplicates of each other). The original scope (`Bidders.tsx`'s and `AuctionItemsTab.tsx`'s in-table empty rows, `Items.tsx`'s and `Receipts.tsx`'s file-row empty message) had since moved *inside* `DataTableWrapper`'s single-cell `emptyMessage` slot from the prior session's work, so those were correctly left alone - a full icon/heading/description block doesn't fit inside a table row. Also fixed while touching these files: 2 more missing `aria-label`s and 3 more manual-template-literal classNames from the standing audit list (both counts updated in this doc). All tests pass unmodified (including `Checkout.test.tsx`'s exact `"No winners found in this category."` assertion); full suite 321/321, `npm run build` clean.
- Added `src/components/ui/LoadingButton.tsx`, a shared busy-button wrapper (`loading`/`loadingText`/`icon`/`loaderClassName` props, wraps `Button`, auto-disables while loading) consolidating hand-rolled `{loading ? <Loader2 .../> : ...}` pairs. Adopted in `BidEntry.tsx` (1 button), `Bidders.tsx` (2), and `Checkout.tsx` (7, including 3 per-winner-row buttons keyed off a shared `processingId` string). `Receipts.tsx` was dropped from this item's scope - a previous audit claimed it needed this too, but its refresh button already uses a different, correct "spin the same icon in place" pattern rather than a `Loader2` swap, and no longer imports `Loader2` at all; that claim was stale. Found the same duplicated pattern in five more files beyond the doc's original scope (`BundlingDialog.tsx`, `ConfigSettings.tsx`, `Dashboard.tsx`, `TicketAttendeeTab.tsx`, `AddAuctionItemDialog.tsx`) - logged as a separate follow-up in `refactor-opportunities.md` rather than folded into this pass. Also fixed while touching `Checkout.tsx`: 2 more `bg-[#e31b23]`/`text-[#e31b23]` → `bg-orns-red`/`text-orns-red` (already-logged finding, count updated 6→2 remaining). All tests pass unmodified; full suite 321/321, `npm run build` clean.
- Added `src/components/ui/IconButton.tsx`, a shared icon-only button wrapper with `size` locked to the icon variants and `aria-label` required at the type level (verified with a throwaway compile check that omitting it is a real `tsc` error, not just documented intent). Adopted at all 7 real icon-only `<Button size="icon">` call sites across 6 files: `AuctionAssistant.tsx`, `AuctionItemRow.tsx`, `ui/EditableCell.tsx`, `DocumentCreationTab.tsx`, `TicketDocumentCreationTab.tsx`, and `TicketAttendeeTab.tsx` (×2) - including the 4 that were previously missing `aria-label` entirely and the 3 that already had one (migrated anyway for consistency and future type-safety). `dialog.tsx`/`sheet.tsx`'s own icon-only close buttons were left alone - shadcn-generated framework internals using a different, already-correct `sr-only` span pattern, not app-level duplication. All tests pass unmodified; full suite 321/321, `npm run build` clean.
- Created a modular documentation system in `/docs/agents/` to break down the monolithic `AGENTS.md` and `README.md` files into organized subfiles.
- Added `/docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md` to track feature updates, bug fixes, and system improvements.

- Added `docs/agents/component-standards.md`, `tailwind-style-guide.md`, `react-patterns.md`, `performance.md`, and `refactor-opportunities.md` - app-specific standards docs verified directly against this codebase (the previously-uploaded generic versions of these described a different, unrelated app).
- Ran a full code audit (2026-09-02) against all 7 standards docs (`component-standards.md`, `patterns.md`, `performance.md`, `react-patterns.md`, `style_guide.md`, `tailwind-style-guide.md`, `testing-philosophy.md`) against the real `src/`/`server/` code, and recorded findings into `refactor-opportunities.md` sorted into "Tasks To Refactor," "Passed Audit Inspection," and "Out of Scope, but Should be Fixed." Every code-level finding from that audit has since been resolved (see the other entries in this file); `refactor-opportunities.md` was reorganized into terse actionable bullets afterward, with this narrative context moved here per the file's own paper-trail-lives-in-CHANGELOG policy.
- Added `src/components/ui/ConfirmationDialog.tsx`, a shared confirmation dialog (`open`/`onOpenChange`/`title`/`description`/`confirmLabel`/`cancelLabel`/`onConfirm`/`isLoading`/`variant`/`icon` props), first adopted by `AuctionItemsTab.tsx`'s two confirmation dialogs.
- Added PWA support via `vite-plugin-pwa`: web manifest, service worker (`registerType: 'autoUpdate'`, `devOptions.enabled: true`), and a placeholder icon set in `public/`.
- Added local dev support for a configurable `PORT` env var (`server.ts`, `.env.example`) so the app can run on a port other than 3000 when that's already taken.
- Added `npm run dev:watch`, a local-only script (`tsx watch`, excluding `src/**`) that auto-restarts the backend on `server/**` changes. Deliberately a *separate* script from `dev` - `dev` itself is what AI Studio's own live-editing preview runs under the hood (`vite.config.ts`'s `DISABLE_HMR` comment), and an earlier version of this change mistakenly edited `dev` directly, which would have added an ungated restart-on-change watcher to AI Studio's own edit loop and reintroduced the exact flickering/instability `DISABLE_HMR` exists to prevent. Caught before being ported back; `dev` is untouched.
- Started a new hardening pass on every remaining app-to-Bid-Management-spreadsheet API call, now that the checkout/PayPal payment workflow's own hardening is complete - logged as a 5-item ordered plan in `refactor-opportunities.md`'s "Test coverage gaps" section so it doesn't get lost between sessions. Item 1 done: `server/services/auctionSetupService.ts`'s year-end master-spreadsheet reset (`/api/auction/setup-new`'s step 6 - deletes the `Auction-Items`/`Bid-Management`/`Bidder-Numbers` tabs, resets `Auction-Status!A2`, clears `Special-Winners!B2:B3` and `Follow-Up!A2:Z`), the highest-risk item on the list since it's destructive and irreversible against live data. Found and fixed a real gap while writing the tests: steps 4 (template copy) and 5 (spreadsheet backup) already surface failures to the operator via `onLog` (the streamed setup-wizard log), but every failure in step 6 - the actually destructive step - only reached the server's own `console.error`, invisible in the UI; a failed delete/clear/update (quota, permissions, transient API error) would report `success: true` to the operator while silently leaving stale data mixed into what they were told was a clean reset. Fixed by routing all of step 6's `.catch()` handlers (and the outer try/catch) through `onLog` too, matching the pattern already used one step earlier in the same function. Added `server/test/handlers.ts` capture arrays (`clearedRanges`, `sheetBatchUpdateRequests`) since the existing MSW mocks for `values:clear`/`spreadsheets:batchUpdate` didn't record what was actually sent; also added a missing `beforeEach(() => resetHandlersState())` to `auction-setup.test.ts`; which imported the reset helper but never called it, so the file's existing 4 tests ran with unreset shared mock state. Added 2 new tests: one pins the exact `batchUpdate` delete-request payload (asserting `Auction-Status`/`tickets` tabs survive while `Auction-Items`/`Bid-Management`/`Bidder-Numbers` don't, by sheetId, not just tab name) plus the `Auction-Status` reset value and both `Special-Winners`/`Follow-Up` clears; the other simulates a 429 on the delete step and asserts the failure now appears in the streamed log while the run still completes (`success: true`) - confirming the existing "one failed sub-step doesn't abort the whole reset" resilience is intentional and now actually visible when it kicks in. Sanity-checked both new tests catch real regressions before trusting them: temporarily added `Auction-Status` to the delete list and confirmed the sheetId assertion failed, then reverted. Test count: 377 → 379 (105 → 105 frontend, 272 → 274 backend); updated the count everywhere it's asserted (`AGENTS.md`, `docs/agents/README.md`, `docs/agents/testing-batches.md`). This step doesn't touch Apps Script - `setupNewAuction` reads/writes the Bid-Management spreadsheet directly via the Sheets API, no `.gs` bridge function involved, so there's no live-script file to copy over. `tsc --noEmit` clean; full suite 379/379, `npm run build` clean.
- Item 2 of the Bid-Management hardening plan done: `src/hooks/useBiddersData.ts`'s `checkInBidder`/`markBidderAsProxy` - the real optimistic-update-with-rollback Sheets-write logic for bidder check-in. This hook is only ever consumed through `AuctionContext.tsx`, and every existing component test mocks that context wholesale via `vi.mock()`, so there was no component-test path that could reach this hook's real code at all - not even a shallow one. Added `src/hooks/useBiddersData.test.ts` (7 tests) using `renderHook` from `@testing-library/react` v16 - already installed but never used anywhere in this codebase before (the established convention is integration-style component tests only); confirmed the approach with the user before introducing a new testing pattern, since it sets a precedent for how other context-hidden hooks get tested going forward. Found and fixed 3 real issues on the read-through, plus applied the same menu-disabling policy already in place for Bid-Management: (1) **Critical, now-fixed breakage**: `Class_GlobalStyles.gs` was completely empty (0 bytes), but `Class_DriveService.gs`'s `DriveService extends GlobalStyles` - since `DocService`/`SlidesService`/`TemplateManager` all extend `DriveService` transitively, and every document generator extends one of those, this would have thrown `ReferenceError: GlobalStyles is not defined` the instant any function in the script ran, breaking every menu item and all 8 `api*` bridge functions. The user pasted in the real content (colors, fonts, spacing primitives, `DocumentApp` style objects - same shape as the sibling `BidManagementSpreadsheet/Class_GlobalStyles.gs`) before the rest of this work continued. (2) `LiveAuctionSlides.writeLiveAuctionItems_()` and `BiddingGuideDoc.loopThroughAllLiveAuctionItems_()` both looped over raw sheet rows via `getAuctionItemAtIndex_(i)` instead of calling `getValidSortedItems()` like every other document generator (`BundlingMeetingDoc`, `PackagingLabelsDoc`, `BidSheetDoc`, `CatalogDoc`, `TableTentDoc` all correctly filter+sort) - meaning a Live-category item marked inactive/deleted in the app would still print in the live-auction slideshow and the bidding guide's inner pages. Fixed both to build their item list via `getValidSortedItems().filter(item => item.isLive())`. (3) `GlobalVariables`'s `_Settings`-missing path called `SpreadsheetApp.getUi().alert(...)` unconditionally, but `getUi()` throws when there's no UI context - exactly the situation every `api*` call runs in (invoked via the Apps Script API, no spreadsheet window open) - so a missing `_Settings` tab would have surfaced as a confusing internal UI-context exception instead of the intended "Missing _Settings configuration" message. Wrapped the `getUi().alert(...)` call in a try/catch; the deliberate `throw new Error(...)` right after it still surfaces the real message either way. (4) Disabled the Items spreadsheet's custom "Auction" menu (`onOpen`/`onInstall` now no-ops, `createCustomMenu()` left defined but unreachable) per the user's explicit confirmation that the same "app is the single source of use" policy applied to Bid-Management also applies here - orphaning ~12 menu-only document-generation wrapper functions, logged as a new `refactor-opportunities.md` follow-up (same deferred delete-vs-keep decision as Bid-Management's now-deleted manager classes, to be revisited after the testing phase). This surface never touches Apps Script at all, so no `.gs` unit tests apply to it. Working from findings an earlier deep audit had already logged in `refactor-opportunities.md` but never sequenced into a plan. Fixed 2 real bugs: (1) Bundle creation silently dropped a manually-entered min raise - `minRaise` was destructured out of `req.body` in `bundleRoutes.ts` and typed on `ProcessBundleParams`, but `processBundle`'s own destructure never listed it, so `cleanMinRaise` was always recomputed from the tier table regardless of an admin's override. Fixed to `minRaise || calculateMinRaise(...)`, matching how `openingBid` is already handled one line above. Found while fixing: `bundle.test.ts`'s happy-path test already sent `minRaise: "20"` (and `value`/`openingBid`/`donor`/`itemType`) in its payload but never asserted any of them landed correctly in the written row - added those assertions, plus a new test for the no-`minRaise`-provided fallback path. That new test itself first failed with the wrong value, exposing a second, independent bug: the same test file's own MSW mock checked `range.includes("Value%20Calculations")` against an already-`decodeURIComponent`'d string, so that branch could never match - the "Value Calculations" tier fixture had been silently unreachable in every bundle test, including the original happy path, this whole time. Fixed the match condition. (2) Divergent minimum-raise fallback tables - `itemService.ts`'s `calculateBidAmounts` fell back to a flat `$5` when no "Value Calculations" tab was configured, while `bundleService.ts`'s `calculateMinRaise` used a hardcoded 7-tier ladder instead, so adding an item vs. bundling one at the same value produced different minimum raises. Extracted the shared logic into a new `server/utils/minRaiseHelpers.ts::calculateMinRaiseAmount()` (kept the 7-tier ladder as the single fallback - it matches a real "Value Calculations" tab's own default rows); both services now delegate to it. Added a test to `items.test.ts` proving a $500 item with no Value Calculations tab now gets the same `$20.00` a $500 bundle would (previously `$5.00`), and strengthened its happy-path test with a `minRaise` assertion it was missing. - Continued the Items-spreadsheet direct-Sheets-API hardening pass: fixed 3 more real bugs in `useBundling.ts`/`useAuctionItemsTableState.ts`, plus a duplication cleanup. (1) Bundle creation could be double-submitted - `executeBundle` fired its `fetchWithRetry(...)` without `await`, so the `finally { setIsExecutingBundle(false) }` ran in the same tick, re-enabling the "Confirm Bundle & Merge Rows" button immediately (and the idempotency key regenerates per call, so the server can't dedupe a rapid double-click). Fixed by awaiting the fetch inline, matching the sibling `handleRestoreBundle`'s already-correct shape. (2) Both `handleRestoreBundle` and `executeBundle` assumed the response shape without checking `res.ok` - `data.ui_history_log.original_item_names.join(...)` (and the equivalent for bundling) threw on any non-200 response missing that field, turning a real failure into a confusing "Cannot read properties of undefined" toast. Both now check `res.ok`/`data.error` first, with a status-code fallback message and optional-chained field access. (3) `useAuctionItemsTableState.ts`'s selection wasn't reconciled with active filters - `selectedRows` survived search/category/type filter changes, while `useBundling.ts` re-derives the selected-items list against the *unfiltered* `items` array, so selecting rows then narrowing the filter could silently include items no longer visible in the table when bundling. Fixed with a `useEffect` keyed on the filtered `sortedItems` that prunes any selected `rowIndex` no longer in view - deliberately general rather than special-casing `resetFilters`, since widening the view (including via `resetFilters`) never removes anything from `sortedItems`, so nothing gets pruned in that direction. Also fixed the closely-related "whole-snapshot rollback" bug in this same file's `handleUpdate` (part of a broader, 5-file finding logged earlier but only this file was in scope for this pass): its rollback used to restore the entire captured `items` array on failure, so editing two different cells in quick succession could have the second edit's failure silently wipe out the first edit's already-committed change; replaced with a `rollbackThisRow()` closure scoped to just the one row's changed field(s) by `rowIndex`. `executeBulkUpdate`'s own whole-array rollback in the same file was deliberately left alone - it's a genuinely atomic multi-row batch operation, so restoring the whole pre-batch snapshot on failure is correct there, not an instance of this bug. (4) Deleted `useBundling.ts`'s byte-for-byte duplicate of `parseDollar`/`calculateAutoBids` (already flagged, `useAuctionItemsTableState.ts` already imported the real one) - now imports both from `@/lib/auctionItemPricing`, with the one call site updated to pass `valueCalculations` as the shared function's second parameter instead of closing over it. Added test coverage across all of this: `src/lib/auctionItemPricing.test.ts` (20 tests - `parseDollar` plus every one of `calculateAutoBids`'s 7 tier boundaries, both with and without a configured Value-Calculations table, closing an already-flagged gap) and `src/hooks/useBundling.test.ts` (9 tests, via `renderHook` since this hook is only ever consumed through `AuctionItemsTab.tsx` and both `AuctionItemsTab.test.tsx`/`BundlingDialog.test.tsx` mock it wholesale) - covering the double-submit fix (a deferred-promise test proving `isExecutingBundle` stays `true` for the whole request, not just until the call is issued), both `res.ok` fixes, `openBundlingModal`'s <2-items guard and its description/donor-merge and auto-bid calculations, and both handlers' happy paths. Every fix sanity-checked against a deliberately reintroduced bug before being trusted - all caught cleanly, then reverted.
