This closes the entire 9-item Items-spreadsheet direct-Sheets-API hardening plan (item entry, bundling logic, item-list parsing, and now bundle UI wiring), on top of the 6-item Apps Script bridge plan closed earlier - every originally-scoped gap in how this app talks to the Items spreadsheet has now been reviewed and, where a real issue existed, fixed and tested. `tsc --noEmit` clean; full suite 482/482, `npm run build` clean.
- Deleted the ~12 now-orphaned menu-only wrapper functions in `scripts/GoogleAppsScriptsCode/ItemsSpreadsheet/`, per the user's go-ahead - the same call made for Bid-Management's now-deleted manager classes, and the deferred decision this exact backlog item had been holding on since the Apps Script bridge plan closed: `printAuctionItemsForBundling`, `requestSequentialBundles`, `printLabelsForAuctionItems`, `setAuctionIDsOnImportSheet`, `createNewBiddingGuideCoverPage`, `createNewBiddingGuideInnerPages`, `createBidSheets` (the global menu-only function - distinct from `GoogleAIStudio.createBidSheets(folderID)`, the live class method `apiCreateBidSheets` actually calls, which is untouched), `createNewCatalog`, `createTableTents` (same distinction), `createNewPresentation`, `updateFileOrFolderLocations`, `fixEmptyCategorySheet`. Several were the *only* caller of their parent class, so - matching how Bid-Management's cleanup deleted the whole orphaned class rather than just its wrapper - also deleted `Class_BundlingWorkflowManager.gs`, `Class_ValidationManager.gs`, `Class_AuctionCategory.gs` (only ever used by `ValidationManager`), and `Class_SettingsManager.gs` in full, after confirming via grep that every remaining reference to each was a stale doc comment, not real code. This cascaded one level further: after those four deletions, `Class_UIManager.gs` had exactly one real caller left - `DriveService.giveUserHeadsUpAboutLoadingTime_()`, itself already unreachable (every call site in the doc generators was commented out) - so deleted `Class_UIManager.gs` and that dead method too. Also deleted `menu.gs`'s `createCustomMenu()` and its menu-label constants, since every function it referenced was now gone and a dead function full of dangling string references is worse than not having it (same reasoning already applied to Bid-Management's `menu.gs`); `onOpen`/`onInstall` stay as documented no-ops. Cleaned up two now-stale doc comments that referenced the deleted classes by name (`Class_BundlingMeetingDoc.gs`, `Class_GlobalVariables.gs`) while in the area. Also recorded, for completeness, two minor findings noticed during the original `.gs` read-through but never actually written down until now: `ValidationManager.findMatchingCategoryValue_()`'s Categories-sheet-row-order coupling (now moot - that class is deleted) and `AuctionItem`'s missing length guard on `rowData` (still live, low-probability, flagged for awareness). Sanity-checked the whole cleanup by loading the full remaining 18-file `.gs` folder into a sandboxed Node `vm` context (all parse and resolve cleanly, file count matches disk exactly) and re-running `AuctionItemManager_UnitTests.gs` against the real combined source (still 10/10). **Reference-copy deletions only - none of this was ever live-critical (the manual menu it served was already disabled), so nothing needs to change in the live script for correctness, though the user may want to delete the same files there too for consistency.** No Node-side code touched, so `tsc --noEmit`/full suite/`npm run build` are unaffected (482/482 still).

- **Fixed `Checkout.tsx`'s two `TabsContent` panels binding the same `state.filteredWinners`**, flagged in `refactor-opportunities.md`: `useWinnersList.ts`'s tab-scoped filtering was keyed off `activeTab`, so both the "pending" and "completed" `WinnerGrid` instances read the identical, single-tab-scoped list - it only ever rendered correctly because `Tabs` unmounts the inactive panel, and would have silently shown the wrong tab's winners (or the wrong tab's search results) the moment either panel rendered while not "active" (e.g. `forceMount`). Fixed by splitting `useWinnersList.ts`'s single `filteredWinners` memo into two independently search-filtered lists, `pendingFilteredWinners`/`completedFilteredWinners`, each derived straight from its own `pendingWinners`/`completedWinners` set rather than gated by `activeTab`; `useCheckoutState.ts` passes both straight through, and `Checkout.tsx`'s two `TabsContent` panels now each bind their own. Added 2 tests to `src/hooks/useCheckoutState.test.ts`: both lists staying correctly and independently populated at the same time regardless of `activeTab` (the exact `forceMount` scenario), and a search term scoping to only the active tab's own list, leaving the other tab's list untouched. Sanity-checked by reverting to a single `activeTab`-gated list shared by both fields and confirming both new tests failed with the real symptom (the wrong tab's winners appearing, and a search term leaking into the other tab's results), then re-applied the fix. Test count: 660 → 662; updated the count everywhere it's asserted. Doesn't touch Apps Script. `tsc --noEmit` clean; full suite 662/662, `npm run build` clean.

- **Confirmed and fixed the medium-confidence `SelectValue` display-label bug flagged in `refactor-opportunities.md`**: `AddAttendeeDialog.tsx`'s Source select and all three of `BiddersFilterControls.tsx`'s selects rendered bare `<SelectValue />`/`<SelectValue placeholder="..." />` with no explicit `children`. The backlog item itself flagged this as needing a manual UI check first, since `@base-ui/react`'s `SelectValue` actually *does* resolve a matching item's label from its internal item registry when one exists - it only falls back to the raw stored value when nothing in that registry matches yet. No browser tool was available in this environment, so verified empirically instead: wrote a throwaway RTL test rendering the real (unmocked) `AddAttendeeDialog` with `source: "sponsor tickets"` and read the trigger's actual DOM text - it showed the literal string `"sponsor tickets"`, not "Sponsor", confirming the bug is real (`SelectContent`'s items aren't registered until the dropdown has been opened at least once, so the initial closed-trigger render always falls back to the raw value). Fixed both files using the exact pattern `component-standards.md` documents: `<SelectValue>{(value) => LABELS[value] ?? value}</SelectValue>`, with a `Record<string, string>` label map per select next to its `SelectItem` list. Added `src/components/AddAttendeeDialog.test.tsx` (3 tests) and `src/components/BiddersFilterControls.test.tsx` (4 tests) - both components' existing test coverage (`TicketAttendeeTab.test.tsx`, `Bidders.test.tsx`) mocks `@/components/ui/select` wholesale, so neither could ever have caught this; the real `@base-ui/react` Select is deliberately left unmocked in both new files, rendering each select with a non-default stored value and asserting the trigger shows the friendly label, not the raw value. Sanity-checked by reverting both fixes back to bare `<SelectValue />` and confirming all 7 new tests failed with the real symptom (the raw stored value, e.g. `"checked-in"`, `"sponsors"`, rendered instead of the label), then re-applied. While confirming this, found the identical gap in a third, unflagged file - `TicketAttendeeTab.tsx`'s own Source filter select - out of scope for this pass since it wasn't one of the two files the backlog item named; logged as a new `refactor-opportunities.md` entry rather than fixed as a drive-by. Test count: 662 → 669 (2 new test files); updated the count everywhere it's asserted. Doesn't touch Apps Script. `tsc --noEmit` clean; full suite 669/669, `npm run build` clean.

- **Fixed the keyboard-focus-reveal gap flagged in `refactor-opportunities.md`**: `EditableCell.tsx`'s edit-trigger IconButton (used in nearly every editable table cell app-wide) and `AuctionItemRow.tsx`'s delete IconButton were both hidden via `opacity-0 group-hover(/row):opacity-100` with no focus-based reveal - `opacity-0` doesn't remove a `<button>` from the tab order, so a keyboard user tabbing to either button landed on a control that was fully invisible, with no focus ring visible either, violating `component-standards.md`'s "never remove a focus outline without replacement" rule. Fixed both by adding `group-focus-within:opacity-100` (`EditableCell.tsx`) / `group-focus-within/row:opacity-100` (`AuctionItemRow.tsx`, matching its existing named `group/row`) alongside the existing hover variant - CSS `:focus-within` on the wrapping `group` fires whenever any descendant, including the button itself, has focus, so tabbing to either button now reveals it the same way hovering does. Added `src/components/ui/EditableCell.test.tsx` (2 tests) and `src/components/AuctionItemRow.test.tsx` (2 tests): jsdom doesn't apply real CSS, so there's no computed `opacity` to read from a test - the tests instead assert the `group-focus-within(/row):opacity-100` class itself is present on the button alongside the hover class (documented in each file's own header comment as the reason for testing the class directly here, an exception to this codebase's normal preference for user-visible-outcome assertions over implementation details), plus a check that the button stays a real, non-disabled, tab-reachable `<button>` regardless of its opacity. Sanity-checked by reverting both classes and confirming all 4 new tests failed (the class was simply absent), then re-applied. Test count: 669 → 673 (2 new test files); updated the count everywhere it's asserted. Doesn't touch Apps Script. `tsc --noEmit` clean; full suite 673/673, `npm run build` clean.

- **Fixed `useConfigSettingsState.ts`'s `syncSettingToCloud` never checking `res.ok`**, flagged in `refactor-opportunities.md`: `fetchWithRetry` only throws for a 429/5xx transient response, so a 4xx from `POST /api/settings/update` (e.g. `dataRoutes.ts`'s own 400 "Missing required fields" or 404 "Spreadsheet or Settings tab not found") resolved normally and was silently treated as success by all six of `handleSave`'s `syncSettingToCloud` call sites - the `try/catch` wrapping them only logged to `console.error` and swallowed, so `handleSave` fell through unconditionally to `toast.success("Settings saved locally and synced to cloud")` even when the cloud sync genuinely failed. Fixed `syncSettingToCloud` to check `res.ok` and throw with the server's real error message, matching the exact pattern every other function in `google-api.ts` already uses (`if (!res.ok) { const errorData = await res.json().catch(() => ({})); throw new Error(errorData.error || "..."); }`) - this function was the one outlier missing it. Also fixed `handleSave` itself: the existing catch now sets a `cloudSyncFailed` flag instead of just logging, and the final toast branches on it - a real failure now shows `toast.error("Settings saved locally, but failed to sync to the Bid-Management spreadsheet's cloud settings. Check your Bid Management Spreadsheet ID.")` instead of the misleading blanket success toast (local `localStorage` writes and the separate Firebase `saveConfig` sync are unaffected either way - both already save/fail independently of this legacy per-setting cloud sync). Added 2 tests to `src/components/ConfigSettings.test.tsx` (the real, unmocked `useConfigSettingsState` hook runs under this component's existing tests, just with `fetchWithRetry` mocked at the network boundary): the plain success-toast path when every sync call succeeds, and the new failure-toast path (mocking `/api/settings/update` to return `{ ok: false, status: 400 }`) asserting the failure toast fires and the success toast does not. Sanity-checked by reverting `syncSettingToCloud`'s `res.ok` check and confirming the new failure-toast test failed with the real symptom (the success toast fired instead, since the 4xx response was never treated as an error), then re-applied. Test count: 673 → 675; updated the count everywhere it's asserted. Doesn't touch Apps Script. `tsc --noEmit` clean; full suite 675/675, `npm run build` clean.

- **Fixed `google-api.ts`'s `fetchWithRetry` mutating the caller's `options` object in place**, flagged in `refactor-opportunities.md`: when injecting the `x-google-tokens` auth header, it wrote straight into the caller's own `options.headers` (a plain object got a key assigned directly onto it, a `Headers` instance got `.set()` called on it directly) - a caller reusing an options literal or a shared `Headers`/headers-object reference across multiple `fetchWithRetry` calls would see that mutation leak forward into every later call, and the mutated object would remain permanently altered even after the function returned. Checked every current call site in this codebase first (32 total): every one already passes a fresh options literal per call, so this wasn't yet an observed symptom anywhere - but it's a directly provable, currently-reachable contract violation of the exported function itself, not a hypothetical. Fixed by shallow-cloning `options` into a local `requestOptions` before any mutation, and cloning `Headers` instances via `new Headers(...)` before calling `.set()` on the clone (the plain-object and array branches already build a new object/array before assigning, so only needed to stop writing back onto the caller's own reference). Added `src/services/google-api.test.ts` (4 tests - this module had zero direct coverage before this, scoped deliberately to just this fix rather than full coverage of every exported function, a separate larger gap): a plain-object headers literal stays untouched after a call, a `Headers` instance stays untouched, an array-form headers list stays untouched, and the regression guard itself - two calls sharing the same options object, confirming both real `fetch()` calls received the injected token header while the caller's own shared object was never mutated. Sanity-checked by reverting the clone and confirming 3 of the 4 new tests failed with the real symptom (the caller's own object gaining an `x-google-tokens` key/entry it never had) - the array-form test alone still passed even reverted, since `.filter()` already returns a new array regardless of the surrounding clone, confirming that branch was never the bug - then re-applied the full fix. Test count: 675 → 679 (1 new test file); updated the count everywhere it's asserted. Doesn't touch Apps Script. `tsc --noEmit` clean; full suite 679/679, `npm run build` clean.

- **Fixed `TicketAttendeeTab.tsx`'s own Source filter `Select` having the same bare-`<SelectValue />` display-label gap** already fixed in `AddAttendeeDialog.tsx`/`BiddersFilterControls.tsx` - found while confirming that sibling bug via a real (unmocked) RTL render, logged separately since it wasn't one of the two files that backlog item named, and now closed the same way: a `SOURCE_LABELS` map and `<SelectValue placeholder="Source">{(value) => SOURCE_LABELS[value] ?? value}</SelectValue>`. Added `src/components/TicketAttendeeTab.sourceFilterLabel.test.tsx` (1 test) - the existing `TicketAttendeeTab.test.tsx` mocks `@/components/ui/select` wholesale as a native `<select>` (deliberately, for reliable `userEvent` testing of the filtering logic itself), so it could never have caught this; this new file mocks everything else needed to render the component (`AttendeesTable`, `AddAttendeeDialog`, `ConfirmationDialog`, the contexts) but leaves the real `@base-ui/react` Select unmocked, and asserts the default-state trigger shows "All Sources", not the raw stored value `"all"`. Sanity-checked by reverting to bare `<SelectValue placeholder="Source" />` and confirming the new test failed with the real symptom (the literal string `"all"` rendered instead of "All Sources"), then re-applied. Test count: 679 → 680 (1 new test file); updated the count everywhere it's asserted. Doesn't touch Apps Script. `tsc --noEmit` clean; full suite 680/680, `npm run build` clean. **This closes every item in the 2026-09-02 deep audit's "Code quality" section** - `refactor-opportunities.md`'s "Code quality" heading now reads "Nothing outstanding right now," matching the file's own convention for a fully-closed section.

- **Built an in-app bug-reporting tool**, per the user's explicit spec: a small, low-contrast floating button (bottom-left - `App.tsx`'s existing `BackToTop` already claims bottom-right) plus a global Ctrl/Cmd+Shift+B shortcut open a form dialog (title, description, a Bug/UI Issue/Workflow Issue type select, an "include a screenshot" checkbox defaulted on). Submitting auto-captures, with no user input needed: the current route (`App.tsx` has no router - it's `activeWorkflow`/`activeBidTab` state already mirrored into `localStorage` on every change, which is the real, already-in-sync signal read directly rather than threading new state through the provider tree), a compact app-state snapshot (bidder/item/attendee/winner counts, auction-closed flag, which spreadsheets are configured, from the real `AuctionContext`/`ConfigContext`), browser info (user-agent/platform/viewport), and a best-effort DOM screenshot. Storage architecture was a deliberate, user-confirmed decision (asked before building, since it has real tradeoffs): rather than one-file-per-report on local disk (this app's own reference precedent) or a browser download, reports are saved as rows in a dedicated **Bug-Reports Google Sheet** (`Config.bugReportsSpreadsheetId`, new "Bug Reports" section in `AuctionConfigurationCard.tsx`) - a central, multi-machine catalog, consistent with every other piece of this app's data already living in Sheets - with screenshots uploaded to a companion Drive folder (`Config.bugReportsFolderId`, both optional/independently configurable). If the Sheets write fails for any reason (no spreadsheet configured yet, an expired/missing OAuth session, a Sheets API error), the server transparently falls back to writing the report as a self-contained `.md` file (+ sibling `.png` if a screenshot was captured) under a new gitignored `bug-reports/` directory at the repo root - so a report is never silently lost regardless of auth state, matching the spec's "submitting a valid report always succeeds" requirement. Documented the sheet's exact column layout in `schema.md` and the report lifecycle (delete/mark-Fixed the row + screenshot once the real fix is shipped and written up here in `CHANGELOG.md`, since a report is a working note, not permanent history) in a new `AGENTS.md` section.

  **Recent-error capture** (`src/lib/errorBuffer.ts`, new): a 20-entry ring buffer that patches `console.error`/`console.warn` once at startup (`main.tsx`, before the app renders) to also record into the buffer while still calling through to the original, listens for `window.onerror`/`unhandledrejection` to catch errors outside the normal render path, and exposes a manual `recordError()` for any future error boundary to call (this app has none yet, so nothing else calls it today, but the hook is in place) - idempotent via a module-level guard, with the window listeners themselves torn down by a test-only reset helper to avoid leaking across test runs.

  **Screenshot capture** (`src/lib/screenshotCapture.ts`, new): added `html2canvas` as a new dependency (confirmed with the user first, since it's a new dependency) - dynamically imported so it never lands in the eager initial-load bundle, only fetched when someone actually submits a report with a screenshot included (`vite.config.ts` got a dedicated `vendor-html2canvas` `manualChunks` branch for this specifically - without it, html2canvas would have been silently swept into the already-eager `vendor-utils` chunk alongside `date-fns`/`clsx`, defeating the lazy-load entirely; confirmed via `npm run build`, it lands in its own ~202 kB chunk, separate from the ~264 kB `vendor-utils`). Passes `{ foreignObjectRendering: true }` from the start, a known, pre-solved gotcha for this exact stack: html2canvas 1.4.x's own manual CSS-color parser throws on `oklch()`, which `src/index.css`'s entire base theme uses (confirmed - this app is Tailwind v4/shadcn); that option delegates painting to the browser's native SVG renderer instead, sidestepping the parser. Captures `#root` specifically, not `document.body` - this app's `Dialog` (`@base-ui/react/dialog`) portals its content to `document.body`, so the bug-report dialog itself is a sibling of `#root`, not a descendant; capturing `document.body` would have screenshotted the report dialog covering the page instead of the page behind it. Wrapped in try/catch, returns `null` on any failure - a screenshot is best-effort and must never block the report itself from saving.

  **Route registration ordering bug found and fixed while wiring this up**: `bugReportRoutes.ts` was initially mounted after `createBidRouter()`/`createDataRouter()` in `app.ts`, both mounted at the generic `/api` prefix with `router.use(requireAuth)` - since Express matches that as a path *prefix*, any request to `/api/bug-reports` hit `bidRoutes.ts`'s auth guard first and 401'd before ever reaching the new router, exactly the masking risk `requireAuth.integration.test.ts`'s own docblock already documents for this codebase. The new bug-report route is deliberately *not* gated by `requireAuth` at all, unlike every other router here - a report about a broken/expired Google session must still be savable, and the route's own try/catch already falls back to the local file in exactly that case - so it has to be mounted before those two generic routers to actually be reachable; caught by the route's own integration test failing with 401 instead of the expected 400/200, not by inspection.

  Added `src/lib/errorBuffer.test.ts` (7 tests: patch-and-call-through for both console methods, idempotent init, the 20-entry ring cap dropping oldest-first, `window` error/`unhandledrejection` capture, manual `recordError()`), `src/lib/bugReportContext.test.ts` (7 tests, the pure route/app-state/browser-info/console-activity formatters), `src/lib/screenshotCapture.test.ts` (4 tests: `#root` + `foreignObjectRendering: true`, returns `null` on a thrown error or a missing `#root` rather than throwing), `src/hooks/useBugReportState.test.ts` (6 tests via `renderHook`: the keyboard shortcut, required-field validation, the full auto-captured payload shape POSTed, screenshot-included vs. not, best-effort screenshot failure still saving, a failed save keeping the dialog/form intact), `src/components/BugReportButton.test.tsx` (4 tests, real unmocked `Select`/`Dialog`: click and keyboard-shortcut both open it, a real end-to-end fill-and-save posts the expected payload, Cancel closes without saving), `server/test/bugReportService.test.ts` (10 tests: filename collision-avoidance via the random suffix - pins the exact "two reports submitted close together silently overwrite each other" gotcha the spec called out - markdown formatting including the relative-not-embedded screenshot link, the local-fallback file/screenshot write, the exact Drive upload args, the exact Sheet row order/Status default), and `server/test/bugReportRoutes.test.ts` (4 tests via `supertest`+MSW: 400 validation, the Sheets-success path, and both local-fallback triggers - no spreadsheet configured, and no session at all). Every fix/behavior sanity-checked against a deliberately broken version before being trusted - reverted the mount-order fix and confirmed the route test failed with 401 as predicted, reverted the new `AuctionConfigurationCard.tsx` fields and confirmed the two dependent `ConfigSettings.test.tsx` tests failed - then re-applied. Test count: 680 → 723 (331 → 345 backend, 349 → 378 frontend; 9 new test files); updated the count everywhere it's asserted. Manually verified end-to-end against the real running dev server (no browser tool available in this environment): booted clean, `POST /api/bug-reports` correctly 400s on missing fields and correctly falls back to a real local `.md` file when unauthenticated with no spreadsheet configured, every touched frontend module transforms through Vite with no compile error. Doesn't touch Apps Script - this is a purely Node/Sheets/Drive-API integration, no bridge functions involved. `tsc --noEmit` clean; full suite 723/723, `npm run build` clean.

- **Fixed two real bugs the user hit immediately after the bug-reporting tool above shipped**, both caused by a process miss on my part: I added `bugReportsSpreadsheetId`/`bugReportsFolderId` to `Config` and edited `src/context/ConfigContext.tsx` to default them, without first checking `AGENTS.md`'s own "DO NOT VIOLATE" critical rules, which explicitly lists `ConfigContext.tsx` and `firestore.rules` as do-not-edit files. (1) **Every config save started failing with "Missing or insufficient permissions."** `firestore.rules`'s `isValidConfig()` does a strict `data.keys().hasAll([...])` + `data.keys().size() == 15` check on the `settings/global` document - my two new fields weren't in that list, so Firestore's own security rules silently rejected every write, not just ones touching the new fields. Confirmed with the user before touching this protected file (their explicit call, since the alternative was dropping Firestore sync for these two IDs entirely): added both fields to `isValidConfig()`'s `hasAll`/type-check list (bumping the strict count to 17) and to the `update` rule's `affectedKeys().hasOnly([...])` list. **This requires `firebase deploy --only firestore:rules` (or pushing the updated rules through the Firebase console) to actually take effect** - editing the local `firestore.rules` file alone does nothing until deployed. While fixing this, also hardened `ConfigContext.tsx`'s Firestore snapshot handler: a `const data = docSnap.data() as Config` blind cast left the two new fields `undefined` (not `""`) for any already-saved config document written before this change, which was the direct cause of the Base UI "uncontrolled to controlled" console warning the user saw - fixed by merging the fetched data over `defaultConfig` (`{ ...defaultConfig, ...docSnap.data() }`) so any field missing from an older document falls back to its real default instead of `undefined`, the same self-healing pattern that already protects every other Config field here. (2) **The floating bug-report button was invisible** - placed `fixed bottom-6 left-6`, directly underneath `App.tsx`'s persistent left sidebar (`fixed md:sticky inset-y-0 left-0 ... z-50`, opaque background, spanning the full height including that exact corner, at a higher z-index than the button's `z-40`). The original reasoning only checked for a collision with `BackToTop` (bottom-right) and missed the sidebar entirely - moved the button to `bottom-20 right-6`, stacked above `BackToTop` so the two coexist without overlapping once both are visible. Added a regression test to `src/context/ConfigContext.test.tsx` (1 test: an old-shaped Firestore doc missing the two new keys still resolves `config.bugReportsSpreadsheetId`/`bugReportsFolderId` to `""`, not `undefined`, while every field the old doc did have passes through untouched) and updated `src/components/BugReportButton.test.tsx`'s existing render/click assertions to match. Sanity-checked the `ConfigContext.tsx` fix by reverting to the blind cast and confirming the new test failed with the real symptom (`undefined` instead of `""`), then re-applied; the `firestore.rules` fix and button reposition were verified directly (rules changes aren't unit-testable without a Firestore emulator this environment doesn't have; the button fix was re-verified via the existing render test plus the documented layout-collision reasoning). Test count: 723 → 724; updated the count everywhere it's asserted. `tsc --noEmit` clean; full suite 724/724, `npm run build` clean.

- **Deployed `firestore.rules` and found/fixed a related deploy-config bug, plus fixed a live oklch screenshot-capture crash the user caught via their own first real bug report through the tool.** Deploying the rules change above required `firebase.json` to specify which Firestore database to target - this project uses a non-default named database (`ai-studio-8ae64d57-94f3-4e97-b086-1f1495b79131`, confirmed against `firebase-applet-config.json`'s `firestoreDatabaseId` and cross-checked with `firebase firestore:databases:list`), and `firebase.json` had no `database` field at all, meaning `firebase deploy --only firestore:rules` would have silently deployed to the empty, unused `(default)` database instead - added `"database": "ai-studio-8ae64d57-94f3-4e97-b086-1f1495b79131"` to `firebase.json`'s `firestore` block and confirmed the fix via `firebase deploy --only firestore:rules --dry-run --debug`, checking the raw API log for the correct `databases/ai-studio-...` path before the user ran the real deploy. Also added `VITE_BUG_REPORTS_SPREADSHEET_ID`/`VITE_BUG_REPORTS_FOLDER_ID` to `.env`/`.env.example` (the user's call, discussed first) so these two IDs don't need to be re-pasted into Settings on this machine - the code already supported this env-var fallback from the original build, just needed the values set. **Separately, the user's own first real bug report through the tool (submitted 2026-09-04) surfaced a live crash in the tool itself**: `src/lib/screenshotCapture.ts`'s "Recent Console Activity" field showed `Attempting to parse an unsupported color function "oklch"` - the exact crash `foreignObjectRendering: true` was supposed to prevent, still happening. Traced it in `node_modules/html2canvas/dist/html2canvas.js`: `renderElement()` unconditionally calls `parseBackgroundColor()` *before* it ever branches into the `foreignObjectRendering` path, and that function reads `getComputedStyle(document.body / documentElement).backgroundColor` through html2canvas's own manual color parser regardless of the option - `foreignObjectRendering` only bypasses that parser for the actual paint step, not this one pre-step. Modern Chrome now serializes that computed value in whatever color space it was declared in (oklch, for this app's Tailwind v4 theme) instead of always downgrading to `rgb()`, which is why this wasn't caught by any earlier testing (the KNOWN GOTCHA note from the original spec was accurate for the paint path, just incomplete for this pre-step). Fixed by passing `onclone` to force a plain, parseable `#f8fafc` (Tailwind's `slate-50`, matching this app's real background) onto the *cloned* document's `body`/`documentElement` before html2canvas ever reads it - the clone happens before `parseBackgroundColor` runs, so mutating it is safe and doesn't touch the live page. Added a regression test to `src/lib/screenshotCapture.test.ts` (1 test: asserts `backgroundColor`/`onclone` are passed to `html2canvas`, then invokes the captured `onclone` callback against a fake cloned document and asserts both elements' `style.backgroundColor` end up set) - sanity-checked by reverting the fix and confirming the new test failed with the real missing-option symptom, then re-applied. Hit and fixed one incidental test-environment leak while verifying this: adding the two `VITE_BUG_REPORTS_*` vars to the real `.env` (used above) made `src/context/ConfigContext.test.tsx`'s existing Firestore-merge regression test (added in the previous entry) start failing, since `defaultConfig`'s env-var fallback now resolves to a real ID during test runs instead of `""` - the test's own assertion was too strict for what it actually needed to prove; loosened it from a literal `.toBe("")` to `typeof ... === "string"` (the real invariant being guarded is `undefined`, not a specific default value), confirmed it still fails against the original bug before re-applying. **Also investigated, but could not fully verify, a second real bug the user found independently while testing the tool** (not the tool's own fault - a pre-existing UI issue on `/BidManagement`'s dashboard tab): `AttendanceChart.tsx`'s checked-in/not-present/proxy donut chart clipping at the top, inside a `Card` with `overflow-hidden`. Traced the likely cause (the original `cy="42%"`/`height={250}`/`outerRadius={95}` left only ~10px of top clearance) and applied a best-effort fix increasing clearance to ~31px, but this genuinely could not be visually confirmed - no browser tool is available in this environment. **Logged as an open, unconfirmed item in `refactor-opportunities.md` rather than claimed as fixed here** - needs the user to check in a real browser before being considered closed. Test count: 724 → 725; updated the count everywhere it's asserted. Doesn't touch Apps Script. `tsc --noEmit` clean; full suite 725/725, `npm run build` clean.

- **User visually confirmed the `AttendanceChart.tsx` clipping fix from the previous entry actually works** - the donut chart's checked-in/not-present/proxy circle no longer clips at the top of its `overflow-hidden` `Card` on `/BidManagement`'s dashboard tab. That entry's best-effort `height={280}`/`cy="45%"` change (~31px of top clearance, up from ~10px) is now a confirmed fix rather than an open item; removed from `refactor-opportunities.md`. No code change in this entry - documentation only.

- **Fixed the same `SelectValue` display-label gap, now in `AuctionItemsFilterBar.tsx`, plus the dropdown-width bug it was compounding with.** User-reported via screenshots: the Category/Item Type filters on the Auction Items table showed the literal raw value `"all"` in the trigger instead of "All Categories"/"All Types" (same pattern already fixed in `AddAttendeeDialog.tsx`/`BiddersFilterControls.tsx`/`TicketAttendeeTab.tsx` this session, never caught here since `AuctionItemsTab.test.tsx` mocks `@/components/ui/select` wholesale) - and, opened, the Category list showed long names ("Arts, Crafts and Jewelry", "Entertainment & Experiences") visibly cut off mid-word with no ellipsis. Traced the second bug to two compounding facts: `SelectTrigger` is `width: fit-content` by default (`select.tsx`'s base classes), so with the trigger showing only the 3-character "all" instead of "All Categories" it was rendering far narrower than its wrapping `w-[240px]` div actually allowed (that wrapper's width does nothing to constrain a `w-fit` child); and `SelectContent`'s popup is bound to the trigger's width via `w-(--anchor-width)`, with each `SelectItem`'s text rendered `whitespace-nowrap` inside an `overflow-x-hidden` popup - so once the trigger was narrow, anything in the list wider than that got silently clipped with no ellipsis. Fixed by (1) adding the label-resolving `SelectValue` children (`categoryLabel`/`itemTypeLabel`, mapping only the `"all"` sentinel - every real category/item-type value already equals its own label) and (2) adding `w-full` to both `SelectTrigger`s so they reliably fill their intended 240px/180px width regardless of which value is selected, plus `min-w-75` on the Category `SelectContent` so the popup has real room for long category names independent of the trigger's own width. Checked `AuctionItemsBulkActionBar.tsx`'s sibling "Set Category"/"Set Type" selects for the same bug while in the area - not affected, since those are uncontrolled (no `value` prop) and every item's `value` already equals its own display label exactly, so there's no value/label mismatch for the bug to ever surface through. Added `src/components/AuctionItemsFilterBar.test.tsx` (3 tests, real unmocked `@base-ui/react` Select): the friendly "All Categories"/"All Types" labels render instead of the raw `"all"`, a real selected category shows its own name, and every category's full untruncated text is present in the DOM once the dropdown is opened (jsdom has no real layout engine, so this proves the text isn't string-truncated at the React level - it can't prove pixel-level visual clipping is gone, which needs a real browser). Sanity-checked by reverting both `SelectValue` fixes back to bare and confirming the label test failed with the real symptom, then re-applied. Test count: 725 → 728 (1 new test file); updated the count everywhere it's asserted. Doesn't touch Apps Script. `tsc --noEmit` clean; full suite 728/728, `npm run build` clean.

- **2026-09-04 standards audit, per the user's explicit request**: measured the codebase against all six standards docs (`component-standards.md`, `patterns.md`, `react-patterns.md`, `style_guide.md`, `tailwind-style-guide.md`, `testing-philosophy.md`) via three parallel research passes, then personally spot-verified every finding against real code before writing anything up - one claimed finding (an `h-[650px]` "duplication" between `DataTableWrapper.tsx` and `Items.tsx`) turned out on inspection to be a docblock comment illustrating the prop next to its one real call site, not an actual second usage, and was dropped rather than logged. **Biggest finding: two of the standards docs were themselves stale**, describing gaps that real feature work in this session had already closed - `component-standards.md` claimed "no dedicated `IconButton`," "no `ConfirmationDialog` wrapper," "no shared `LoadingButton`," and "not yet solved by an existing `DataTableWrapper`-style component," when all four now exist and are in real, multi-file use; `component-standards.md`'s "One Responsibility" section and `react-patterns.md`'s "Component Size" section both cited six specific files by name with line counts from before this session's own container-hook-split work (`AuctionItemsTab.tsx` 969 → 181, `Bidders.tsx` 644 → 107, `Checkout.tsx` 644 → 143, `ConfigSettings.tsx` 572 → 64, `Dashboard.tsx` 517 → 57, `TicketAttendeeTab.tsx` 446 → 120, plus `AuctionContext.tsx` 532 → 118) - no file under `src/components/` (excluding `ui/`) exceeds 250 lines today. Corrected both docs' prose directly rather than just logging the staleness. Also corrected `refactor-opportunities.md`'s own "Passed Audit Inspection" list, which claimed "no derived-state duplication anti-pattern" - false: `useConfigSettingsState.ts:43-46` does exactly that (`useState(remoteConfig)` synced via a `useEffect` on every `remoteConfig` change) - moved to an open "Code quality" item instead of left as an incorrect "passed" claim, with a note that it's not a mechanical `useMemo` fix since `config` is a mutable local draft, not pure derived data. **Real, new findings logged to `refactor-opportunities.md`'s "Code quality" section** (not fixed in this entry - logged per the established one-item-at-a-time workflow, awaiting explicit direction): the derived-state item above; `AuctionItemsTable.tsx:64-85`'s 8 static inline `style={{width}}` attributes on `<TableHead>` cells, a real violation of `style_guide.md`'s "inline styles are forbidden" rule and the only such hit anywhere in `src/` outside test files; and ~20 arbitrary Tailwind width/height values across a dozen files that have a clean default-scale equivalent already available (e.g. `w-[240px]`→`w-60`, `h-[320px]`→`h-80`), including a self-inconsistency in this session's own recent `AuctionItemsFilterBar.tsx` edit (used `min-w-75` in one spot, left `w-[240px]`/`w-[180px]` untouched two lines away). Logged a lower-priority, decision-needed item to "Out of Scope, but Should be Fixed": the widespread `text-[10px]`/`text-[11px]` pattern (8 files) sits below Tailwind's smallest default step and reads as an already-adopted convention that was never promoted to a named token - flagged for a design decision, not auto-fixed. **Everything else passed clean**: no hardcoded hex colors beyond the sanctioned `orns-red` token, no native `<select>` outside test mocks, no manual string-concatenated classNames bypassing `cn()`, no stale font-family references, no direct `fetch()` calls bypassing `google-api.ts`, `useEffect`/memoization discipline both still hold (1-3 effects per file, no gratuitous or missing memoization found), no remaining `SelectValue` bare-value mismatches, all `patterns.md` business rules (winner-notification toast, bundle re-sequencing, PayPal surcharge server-only, `orns_session` cookie name, functional `manualChunks`) reconfirmed correct, and the test suite came back clean against `testing-philosophy.md`'s SHALLOW/CIRCULAR/SMOKE criteria (sampled broadly across bare `toHaveBeenCalled()` call sites, newer test files for circularity, generically-titled tests for smoke-only assertions, and a sample for forbidden self-mocking - all held up on inspection). Also corrected a real doc drift found during this pass: `docs/agents/README.md` claimed "76 focused files" for the test suite; a direct recount (`find server/test src -iname "*.test.ts" -o -iname "*.test.tsx"`) came back 72 (27 backend + 45 frontend) - the 728 test-count figure itself was already accurate, only the file count had drifted. No code changes in this entry - `component-standards.md`, `react-patterns.md`, `refactor-opportunities.md`, and `README.md` only. `tsc --noEmit` clean (unaffected, doc-only).

- **Fixed a real, user-hit `413 PayloadTooLargeError` in the bug-reporting tool**: `server/app.ts`'s `app.use(express.json())` had no `limit` option, defaulting to Express's built-in 100kb cap - a real submitted bug report with a screenshot (base64-encoded PNG, ~390KB in the user's actual case) blew straight through that on the very first real-world attempt to include one. Raised the limit to `5mb` - comfortable headroom for a realistic full-page screenshot while still bounding worst-case request size, not removed entirely. While investigating, also found and fixed a related correctness bug in `server/middleware/errorHandler.ts`: it unconditionally forced every error to a `500` response, discarding the real status code body-parser (and other middleware) had already attached to the error object (`err.status`/`err.statusCode`) - so the user's terminal showed a generic "Internal Server Error" for what was actually a client-side 413, and (discovered while fixing this) a real Google API 429 rate-limit error from `/api/auction/close-and-cleanup` was also being silently flattened to 500 instead of surfacing as 429. Fixed `errorHandler` to respect `err.status || err.statusCode`, falling back to 500 only when neither is present. This is a pure improvement, not just a cosmetic status-code fix: `google-api.ts`'s `fetchWithRetry` specifically retries on 429 or 5xx - a Google-API 429 was already landing in that retry-eligible range via the old 500 fallback, so client-side retry behavior is unchanged, but the status now honestly reflects what actually happened. Updated the one existing test that had encoded the old (wrong) behavior as expected (`server/test/bid-management.test.ts`'s 429-rate-limit test asserted `response.status).toBe(500)` with a comment describing it as intentional "graceful" handling - it wasn't a deliberate design choice, just what the buggy code happened to do) - confirmed via `fetchWithRetry`'s own retry-eligibility check that 429 is the more correct status here, not a regression. Added 2 tests to `server/test/middleware.test.ts`'s `errorHandler` suite (respects `err.status`; respects `err.statusCode` as a fallback for libraries that set that property name instead) and 2 to `server/test/bugReportRoutes.test.ts` (a realistic ~390KB payload, matching the user's exact reported size, now succeeds; a genuinely oversized 6MB payload still correctly 413s, proving the limit was raised, not removed). Sanity-checked both fixes together by reverting and confirming all 4 new tests failed, then re-applied - this also correctly failed the pre-existing `bid-management.test.ts` 429 test in the same revert, confirming that test's dependency on the fix is real, not coincidental. Manually verified end-to-end against a real running dev server: POSTed the exact ~390KB payload size from the user's original bug report and confirmed a real `200` instead of the original `413`. Test count: 728 → 732; updated the count everywhere it's asserted. Doesn't touch Apps Script. `tsc --noEmit` clean; full suite 732/732, `npm run build` clean.

- **Added a "Bug Reports" admin page** (left sidebar, System section, next to Configuration - `src/components/BugReportsAdmin.tsx`), per the user's request to be able to work the open bug-report queue without opening the raw spreadsheet. Lists every row in the Bug-Reports sheet whose `Status` is (case-insensitively) `"open"`, via a new `GET /api/bug-reports/list` route (`listOpenBugReports` in `server/services/bugReportService.ts`, reading `Bug-Reports!A:K` and dropping the header row and any row with a blank Report ID). Each row shows its Type badge, title, route, and reported time, with a Screenshot button (only rendered when the row has a link) and a **Resolve** button. Two follow-up requirements were added by the user mid-build and both are included: (1) each row also has a **View** button opening `BugReportDetailDialog.tsx`, a dialog rendering every field of the report (description, app state, browser info, recent console activity, screenshot link) plus a "Copy as Markdown" button (`navigator.clipboard.writeText`, a new pattern for this codebase - no prior clipboard-copy precedent existed) that copies the report formatted via a new `formatReportAsMarkdown` in `src/lib/bugReportContext.ts`, specifically so the text can be pasted directly into a Claude Code chat to request the fix. (2) Resolving a report (`POST /api/bug-reports/resolve`) now does two things server-side, not just flipping `Status` to `Fixed`: `markReportResolved` writes `"Fixed"` to column C of the report's row (found via `findReportRow`, which reads the full row - not just column A - in one request so it can hand back both the 1-indexed row and the screenshot link together), and if the row had a screenshot, `deleteScreenshotFromDrive` parses the file ID out of the stored `https://drive.google.com/file/d/<id>/view` link and deletes it from the Bug Screenshots Drive folder, so resolved reports don't leave orphaned images behind. The Drive delete is deliberately best-effort/non-blocking (try/catch, `console.error` and swallow, matching the existing screenshot-upload side effect's pattern) - a resolved report must not fail to save just because Drive cleanup didn't work. `bugReportRoutes.ts`'s `/list` and `/resolve` routes are gated by `requireAuth` per-route (not `router.use`), since the router also mounts the pre-existing, deliberately-unauthenticated `POST /` bug-submission route. Added `BugReportListItem` to `src/types.ts` and wired a new `bugReports` workflow into `App.tsx` (sidebar button, `WORKFLOW_PATHS`/`getWorkflowFromPath`, lazy-loaded component, mobile header label). Added `parseReportRow`/`listOpenBugReports`/`findReportRow`/`markReportResolved`/`deleteScreenshotFromDrive` tests to `server/test/bugReportService.test.ts`, list/resolve route tests (including the Drive-delete-on-resolve behavior, via an MSW handler on `DELETE .../drive/v3/files/:fileId`) to `server/test/bugReportRoutes.test.ts`, `formatReportAsMarkdown` tests to `src/lib/bugReportContext.test.ts`, and two new test files: `src/components/BugReportsAdmin.test.tsx` (list rendering, the real fetch URL, empty state, Screenshot button only shown when a link exists, Resolve posting the real payload and removing the row, Refresh re-fetching, the not-configured placeholder, and the View button opening the dialog) and `src/components/BugReportDetailDialog.test.tsx` (all fields render, no Screenshot button without a link, and the copy-to-Markdown button calling `navigator.clipboard.writeText` with the real formatted content and showing a "Copied!" confirmation). Sanity-checked the Drive-delete route logic, the row-removal-on-resolve-success client behavior, and the dialog's copy behavior by reverting each independently and confirming the corresponding test failed with the real symptom, then restored. Live-verified the new routes against a real running dev server (temporary port, no real OAuth session): `GET /list` and `POST /resolve` both correctly 401 without a session, and the pre-existing `POST /` submission route still correctly 400s on a missing-fields payload - confirming the router's mixed auth-gating (per-route `requireAuth`, not `router.use`) is wired correctly. Test count: 732 → 767 (369 backend, 398 frontend; 74 test files, 2 new); updated in `docs/agents/testing-batches.md`, the sole place these totals are tracked (per the user's explicit request this session to stop duplicating live test counts across `AGENTS.md`/`docs/agents/README.md` and only maintain them in one file). Also updated `AGENTS.md`'s "In-App Bug Reports & Their Lifecycle" section to describe this admin UI as the primary way to work the queue, since it now automates the manual Sheet-edit/Drive-delete steps that section used to describe by hand. Doesn't touch Apps Script. `tsc --noEmit` clean; full suite 767/767, `npm run build` clean.

- **Closed `refactor-opportunities.md`'s "Several arbitrary Tailwind width/height values have a clean default-scale equivalent" item.** Replaced every flagged arbitrary-bracket value with this app's Tailwind v4 dynamic-spacing-scale equivalent (`w-<n>` etc. resolve to `n * 0.25rem`, so e.g. `w-37.5` = 150px, `h-100` = 400px - not literal named tokens, but the canonical non-bracket form the style guide asks for) across `AuctionItemsFilterBar.tsx` (`w-[240px]`→`w-60`, `w-[180px]`→`w-45` - also closing the internal inconsistency the backlog item called out, where this same file's `SelectContent` already used the proper scale form two lines away), `BiddersFilterControls.tsx` (`w-[150px]`×3→`w-37.5`), `TicketAttendeeTab.tsx` (`w-[180px]`→`w-45`), `AttendanceChart.tsx`/`ProxyBiddersCard.tsx` (`h-[320px]`→`h-80`), `Receipts.tsx` (`h-[400px]`→`h-100`, `w-[300px]`→`w-75`, `max-w-[300px]`→`max-w-75`), `FollowUpListPanel.tsx` (`h-[300px]`→`h-75`), `WinnerGrid.tsx` (`h-[250px]`→`h-62.5`), `AuctionItemsBulkActionBar.tsx` (`w-[140px]`→`w-35`), `BidderRow.tsx` (`min-w-[140px]`→`min-w-35`, `max-w-[200px]`→`max-w-50`), `BugReportDialog.tsx` (`min-h-[120px]`→`min-h-30`), `EditableCell.tsx` (`min-h-[80px]`→`min-h-20`), `WinnerCard.tsx` (`min-w-[100px]`×2→`min-w-25`), and `FolderFilesCard.tsx` (`max-w-[200px]`→`max-w-50`) - every instance named in the backlog entry, none left behind. Also fixed several unflagged instances of the identical pattern surfaced along the way (by the IDE's own `suggestCanonicalClasses` lint hook while editing `TicketAttendeeTab.tsx`/`BidderRow.tsx`/`EditableCell.tsx` for their flagged fixes, and by a full-codebase `grep` sweep afterward to confirm nothing else was missed): `TicketAttendeeTab.tsx`'s `min-h-[400px]`→`min-h-100`, `EditableCell.tsx`'s `min-w-[9rem]`→`min-w-36`, `BidderRow.tsx`'s second `min-w-[140px]`→`min-w-35`, `App.tsx`'s mobile-header `max-w-[120px]`→`max-w-30`, `BundlingDialog.tsx`'s `min-h-[150px]`→`min-h-37.5`, and `AuctionItemsBulkActionBar.tsx`'s second select's `w-[130px]`→`w-32.5`. Left untouched, confirmed still legitimate one-offs on a final sweep: `AddAuctionItemDialog.tsx`/`BundlingDialog.tsx`'s dialog `max-w-[600px]`/`max-w-[700px]`/`max-h-[90vh]` sizing, `Items.tsx`'s `h-[650px]` (650 isn't scale-clean and it's a single real usage site), and `AuctionItemsBulkActionBar.tsx`'s `w-[1px]` hairline divider (no positive scale value can represent 1px, since the smallest spacing unit is 4px). This was a pure mechanical class-rename pass with no value changes (every replacement resolves to the exact same pixel size as the bracket value it replaced) - not a candidate for new tests, and confirmed no existing test asserts any of these exact class strings before starting (`grep` across all `.test.tsx` files came back empty). No new tests; existing full suite (767) re-run clean, `tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run build` clean. Removed the now-fully-closed item from `refactor-opportunities.md`.

- **Closed `refactor-opportunities.md`'s "Out of Scope, but Should be Fixed" `text-[10px]`/`text-[11px]` item** by naming the convention, per the user's explicit design decision after reviewing a live visual specimen page of all 21 real occurrences across 8 files. Added `--text-xxs: 0.625rem` (10px) with a paired `--text-xxs--line-height: 0.875rem` (14px) to `src/index.css`'s `@theme inline` block - the same one-off-token pattern already used for `--color-orns-red` - giving this app a real step below Tailwind's smallest default, `text-xs` (12px). Replaced all 19 `text-[10px]` call sites with `text-xxs` across `AuctionItemsFilterBar.tsx` (the 3 field-label eyebrows), `DocumentCreationPanel.tsx`/`FollowUpListPanel.tsx` (italic helper notes), `AuctionItemRow.tsx` (the restore-bundle button), `BidderRow.tsx` (12 instances - the sheet-source label, both status-section eyebrows, and all 6 badge-status variants), `BundlingDialog.tsx` ("Suggested by Gemini:"), and `AuctionItemsBulkActionBar.tsx` ("Bulk Actions"). Also fixed the one real inconsistency the review surfaced: `AttendeeRow.tsx`'s "Own #" badge was the only call site using `text-[11px]` instead of `text-[10px]` - per the user's explicit call that this was genuine drift, not an intentional second size, standardized it to `text-xxs` (10px) too, so there's now exactly one sub-`text-xs` size in the app, not two. Documented the new token in `tailwind-style-guide.md`'s new "Type Scale" section, explicitly noting there's no sanctioned 11px step. Confirmed no test asserts any of these exact class strings before starting (`grep` across all `.test.tsx` files came back empty), so this was a value-preserving rename plus one deliberate 11px→10px correction - not a candidate for new tests. Verified the token itself compiles correctly post-build: `grep`ping the built CSS confirms `.text-xxs{font-size:.625rem;line-height:var(--tw-leading,.875rem)}`. `tsc --noEmit` clean, full suite 767/767 clean, `npm run build` clean. Removed the now-resolved item from `refactor-opportunities.md`'s "Out of Scope, but Should be Fixed" section (now empty).

- **Closed `refactor-opportunities.md`'s inline-`style` item: `AuctionItemsTable.tsx`'s 8 `<TableHead>` cells.** Every cell had a `style={{ width: "..." }}` attribute alongside its own `className` - `style_guide.md`'s "inline style attributes are forbidden" rule, the only real hit anywhere in `src/` outside test files. All 8 values were static literals (`"40px"`, `"7%"`, `"30%"`, `"15%"` ×3, `"18%"`, `"60px"`), never computed at runtime, so there was no reason they couldn't fold into `className`. Moved the two px values onto the default Tailwind scale (`40px`→`w-10`, `60px`→`w-15`) and kept the four percentages as arbitrary-bracket `className` values (`w-[7%]`, `w-[30%]`, `w-[15%]`, `w-[18%]`) since percentages have no clean default-scale equivalent - exactly the plan the backlog entry itself already spelled out. This is a pure attribute-relocation with no value change: the table uses `table-fixed` layout, so these widths are load-bearing for column sizing, and every one resolves to the identical computed width it had before. Confirmed no test renders `AuctionItemsTable.tsx` directly (only `AuctionItemsTab.tsx` does, indirectly) and none asserts a `style` attribute or these width values, so no test changes were needed. Verified the four percentage classes actually compiled (Tailwind escapes `%` as `\%` in the selector): `grep`ping the built CSS confirms `.w-\[7\%\]{width:7%}`, `.w-\[30\%\]{width:30%}`, `.w-\[15\%\]{width:15%}`, `.w-\[18\%\]{width:18%}`, plus `.w-10{width:calc(var(--spacing) * 10)}` (40px) and `.w-15{width:calc(var(--spacing) * 15)}` (60px) - all six exactly matching their original inline-style values. `tsc --noEmit` clean, full suite 767/767 clean, `npm run build` clean. Removed the now-resolved item from `refactor-opportunities.md`.

- **Closed `refactor-opportunities.md`'s last "Code quality" item: `useConfigSettingsState.ts`'s derived-state anti-pattern - a real bug, not just a style violation.** `const [config, setConfig] = useState<Config>(remoteConfig)` followed by an unconditional `useEffect(() => setConfig(remoteConfig), [remoteConfig])` is the exact shape `react-patterns.md`'s "Derived State" rule warns against, but this one wasn't a drop-in `useMemo` fix - `config` is a mutable local *draft* the Settings form edits directly (every field's `onChange` calls `setConfig({...config, field: value})`), not pure derived data. Traced the real consequence: `ConfigContext.tsx`'s `remoteConfig` is a fresh object literal built on every Firestore `onSnapshot` fire (`{ ...defaultConfig, ...docSnap.data() }`), which can re-fire for reasons unrelated to any field this user is editing - another admin's device saving a change, or Firestore's own local-then-server double-fire for a write this same session made - and the unconditional effect would silently overwrite the entire local draft with whatever `remoteConfig` most recently held, discarding any unsaved edits with no warning. Fixed by tracking whether the draft is "clean" via reference equality against the last-synced `remoteConfig` (`lastSyncedRemoteConfigRef`) - since every field edit always spreads a fresh object (verified: every `setConfig` call site in `AuctionConfigurationCard.tsx`/`TicketPricingCard.tsx` uses `{...config, field: value}`, never in-place mutation), `config !== lastSyncedRemoteConfigRef.current` reliably means "the user has an unsaved edit in progress." The effect now only accepts a new `remoteConfig` while the draft is still clean - covering the real cases that need it (the initial async Firestore load arriving after mount, signing into Firebase mid-session, a genuine external change while the user hasn't touched anything yet) - and silently skips the sync otherwise, so an in-progress edit is never clobbered. Considered the backlog entry's other suggested option (remount the form via a `key` prop keyed on load/auth state, dropping the effect entirely) but it would've required restructuring `ConfigSettings.tsx` to gate on `useConfig()`'s `loading` flag (which it doesn't currently consume) and had a real race: `firebaseUser` and the Firestore snapshot resolve at different times on sign-in, so keying a remount purely on identity risked capturing a stale pre-snapshot `remoteConfig`; the dirty-check stays self-contained in the one file already named in the backlog item and has no such race. Added 4 tests to `useConfigSettingsState.test.ts` via `renderHook` (a new `vi.mock("@/context/ConfigContext")` returning a controllable `vi.fn()`, reconfigured per test via `mockReturnValue` and picked up on `rerender()`): the draft initializes from `remoteConfig`; a real `remoteConfig` change is picked up while the draft is clean; the regression guard itself - an in-progress local edit survives a subsequent `remoteConfig` change; and syncing keeps working across multiple consecutive clean `remoteConfig` changes (not a sync-exactly-once bug). Sanity-checked by reverting to the old unconditional `setConfig(remoteConfig)` and confirming exactly the regression-guard test failed with the real symptom (`ticketPrice` reverted from the user's `"99"` edit back to `"35"`), then restored. Test count: 767 → 771 (398 → 402 frontend); updated in `docs/agents/testing-batches.md`. Doesn't touch Apps Script. `tsc --noEmit` clean, full suite 771/771 clean, `npm run build` clean. Removed the now-resolved item from `refactor-opportunities.md`'s "Code quality" section (now empty) - this closes every item across every section of the backlog file.

- **Fixed a real, user-reported bug filed as 3 near-duplicate bug reports** ("Select Dropdown Menu Width", "Dropdown Menu Width", "Category Dropdown Bug" - all filed within 3 minutes of each other against the Auction Items Create-Item dialog, the filter bar, and the bulk "Set Category" action) - a long category name (e.g. "Arts, Crafts and Jewelry") was silently clipped with no ellipsis wherever the Category dropdown appeared. Root-caused to one shared component, `src/components/ui/select.tsx`'s `SelectContent`: the popup's width was hard-bound to its trigger's own width via `w-(--anchor-width)` (with only a flat `min-w-36`/144px floor), and `SelectItem` text is `whitespace-nowrap` inside an `overflow-x-hidden` popup - so any option text wider than the (often narrow) trigger just vanished past the edge, with no scrollbar or truncation indicator to hint anything was cut off. Fixed at the shared-component level, not per call site: swapped `w-(--anchor-width) min-w-36` for `w-max min-w-(--anchor-width)`, so the popup now sizes to its own widest item's actual content, with a floor of "at least as wide as the trigger it opens from" instead of an arbitrary constant. Chose the systemic fix deliberately over patching the 3 reported files individually - a `grep` across the whole app found only one other place a `SelectContent` had ever needed a custom width override (`AuctionItemsFilterBar.tsx`'s pre-existing `min-w-75`, which stays as a harmless, still-useful higher floor for short category lists) and, more importantly, surfaced a 4th, unreported instance of the identical bug: `src/components/ui/EditableCell.tsx`'s inline category-editing select in the Auction Items table row, which a per-file patch would have missed entirely. Added `src/components/ui/select.test.tsx` (2 tests, the first coverage for this shared primitive) rendering a real, unmocked `Select`/`SelectContent` with a long option and a narrow trigger, opening it, and pinning the popup's actual className - `jsdom` has no real layout engine so it can't prove pixel-level clipping is gone (`AuctionItemsFilterBar.test.tsx` already covers that the full untruncated text is present in the DOM once open), so this follows the same "assert on the class string itself" exception already used elsewhere in this codebase for identical jsdom limitations: the popup carries `w-max` and `min-w-(--anchor-width)`, not the old `w-(--anchor-width)`/`min-w-36`. Sanity-checked by reverting to the old classes and confirming both new tests failed with the real symptom, then restored. Verified the compiled CSS post-build: `grep`ping `dist/assets/index-*.css` confirms `.w-max{width:max-content}` and `.min-w-\(--anchor-width\){min-width:var(--anchor-width)}` both compiled correctly. Test count: 771 → 773 (404 frontend, 1 new test file). Doesn't touch Apps Script. `tsc --noEmit` clean, full suite 773/773 clean, `npm run build` clean.

- **Fixed a real, user-reported bug: resolving a bug report through the Bug Reports admin page stopped deleting its screenshot from Drive.** Root-caused to a missing `supportsAllDrives: true` on the delete call itself: `uploadScreenshotToDrive`'s `create` call already passes it, but `deleteScreenshotFromDrive`'s `drive.files.delete({ fileId })` didn't - and the Bug Screenshots folder lives in a Shared Drive, where the Drive API rejects (403/404) *any* operation against a file inside it that omits this flag, delete included. The failure was completely invisible: `deleteScreenshotFromDrive` is deliberately best-effort (see its own docblock - a resolved report must not fail to save just because Drive cleanup didn't work), so the rejected delete was silently swallowed by its `try/catch`, the report still correctly flipped to `Fixed` in the Sheet, and nothing in the UI or server logs pointed at Drive specifically - the user only noticed because they went and looked at the actual Bug Screenshots folder and found every resolved report's screenshot still sitting there. Fixed by adding `supportsAllDrives: true` to the delete call, matching the create call's own already-correct pattern. Strengthened both the existing unit test (`bugReportService.test.ts`) and the existing route-level integration test (`bugReportRoutes.test.ts`) to assert this directly rather than just checking `deletedFileId`: the route-level test reads the *real* HTTP request MSW intercepted (`new URL(request.url).searchParams.get("supportsAllDrives")`), confirming `googleapis`'s client actually puts the flag on the wire as a query parameter, not just that a JS object literal contains the key - the strongest verification available without live Drive credentials. Sanity-checked at both levels by reverting the fix and confirming both tests failed with the real symptom (`fileId`-only object at the unit level, a `null` query param at the HTTP level), then restored. No new tests - both existing tests strengthened in place. Doesn't touch Apps Script. `tsc --noEmit` clean, full suite 773/773 clean, `npm run build` clean.

- **Fixed a real, user-reported workflow gap: guest first/last names in the Auction Tickets attendee table couldn't be edited at all, unlike the primary bidder's** ("I was expecting to be able to edit the first and last names of guests in the auction tickets table, like you can for the primary bidder, but you can't"). Root-caused to the data model losing information at the parsing stage, not the underlying sheet: `attendeeParsing.ts` already reads a guest's first/last name from two separate columns (matching the primary attendee's own `firstName`/`lastName` split), but immediately collapsed them into one combined `Guest.name` string - so `AttendeeRow.tsx` had nothing but a read-only `<span>{g.name}</span>` to render, with only the guest's `email` ever wired to an `EditableCell`. Fixed by keeping the split all the way through: added `firstName`/`lastName` to the `Guest` type (kept `name` too, since `bidderAttendeeInfo.ts`'s guest-to-bidder name matching still reads it), populated both in `attendeeParsing.ts` alongside the existing combined `name`, and replaced `AttendeeRow.tsx`'s static guest-name span with two `EditableCell`s mirroring the primary attendee's own First/Last layout exactly. The real column-mapping work was in `useTicketAttendeeState.ts`'s `updateField`, which had a working guest-column mapping for `email` only (`J`/`N`/`R` on the general "tickets" sheet vs. `J`/`M`/`P` on staff/sponsor, accounting for the same guest-column shift already handled in `attendeeParsing.ts`/`syncService.ts`) and no mapping at all for guest `firstName`/`lastName` - calling it with those fields and a `guestIndex` would have silently written to the *primary* attendee's own columns A/B using the guest-editing row index, a real data-corruption risk that only wasn't visible yet because nothing had ever called it that way before. Added the missing guest first/last column triples (derived from `attendeeParsing.ts`'s own read-side layout: `H`/`I`/`J`, `L`/`M`/`N`, `P`/`Q`/`R` on the general sheet; `H`/`I`/`J`, `K`/`L`/`M`, `N`/`O`/`P` on staff/sponsor). Also fixed a second, latent bug in the same function's optimistic update while touching it: the guest branch was hardcoded to `{ ...g, email: newValue }` regardless of which field was actually being edited - harmless while email was the only editable guest field, but would have silently written a firstName/lastName edit into the wrong local field (email) the moment guest name editing existed at all; fixed to `{ ...g, [field]: newValue }`, plus recomputing the combined `name` whenever firstName/lastName changes so `bidderAttendeeInfo.ts`'s matching stays correct. Added `src/components/AttendeeRow.test.tsx` (6 tests, first coverage for this component - real, unmocked `EditableCell`/`Badge`, driven via `userEvent`): guest firstName/lastName render as real editable text; editing each calls `onUpdateField` with the field, new value, and the guest's own `guestIndex`; the primary attendee's own firstName editing is unaffected; guest email editing still works; the "Own #" badge still renders correctly. Added 10 tests to `useTicketAttendeeState.test.ts`'s existing `updateField` coverage: the primary attendee's A/B/C/D columns are unaffected; every guest 1/2/3 firstName/lastName/email column on both the general "tickets" sheet (shifted) and staff/sponsor sheets (unshifted), parametrized via `it.each`; the optimistic-update regression itself - editing a guest's firstName only touches that field, not email, and doesn't bleed into a second guest on the same attendee; the combined `name` stays in sync. Updated 3 existing test fixtures whose `Guest`/`Attendee` literals predated the type change (`bidderAttendeeInfo.test.ts`, `attendeeParsing.test.ts`'s 6 `toEqual` assertions, `TicketAttendeeTab.test.tsx`) to include the new required fields. Sanity-checked both fixes independently by reverting each and confirming the real regression tests failed with the real symptom (guest edits falling back to the primary attendee's own columns/local state), then restored. Test count: 773 → 789 (420 frontend; 1 new test file, 16 new tests in 2 existing files). Doesn't touch Apps Script - this is a client-side Sheets write (`updateSheetData`) with no Apps Script bridge involved. `tsc --noEmit` clean, full suite 789/789 clean, `npm run build` clean.

- **Fixed a real, user-reported data-quality gap: a bidder with more guests listed than tickets purchased (e.g. 1 ticket, 3 guests) went completely unflagged in the Auction Tickets attendee table.** The user's exact framing: "The app, which should be smarter than the spreadsheet, shouldn't allow a bidder to have less tickets purchased than guests... We should add in logic that doesn't allow this to happen and throws an error - or at least ... warns the user." Investigated whether a blocking validation was even possible today: it isn't - `ticketsPurchased` is rendered as a plain, read-only badge in `AttendeeRow.tsx` (never an `EditableCell`), and `AddAttendeeDialog.tsx`'s "Add Entry" form collects tickets but no guests at all, so every new attendee starts with `guestsInfo: []`. There is currently no in-app write path that can *create* a guests-exceed-tickets mismatch - the only source is data that arrived already mismatched from the original ticket-purchase import (a real-world data-entry mistake at the point of purchase, e.g. someone said "1 ticket" but listed 3 names on the form). Given that, implemented the report's own explicit fallback: surface a visible warning for existing mismatched data rather than inventing a blocking check with no write path to attach to. Added a small, focused pure function, `src/lib/attendeeValidation.ts`'s `hasMoreGuestsThanTicketsPurchased` (guest count vs. `parseInt(ticketsPurchased) || 0`, so a blank/non-numeric ticket count is treated as 0 and flags any guest at all), and wired it into `AttendeeRow.tsx`: the ticket-count badge turns red with a ring and a `title` tooltip naming the real numbers, and a red `AlertTriangle` banner appears above the guest list itself ("3 guests listed but only 1 ticket(s) purchased - please correct in the spreadsheet.") - both only when the mismatch is real, invisible otherwise. Documented directly in the new function's own docblock that if a future feature ever lets someone add a guest or make `ticketsPurchased` editable, that feature should call this function to *block* the save, not just warn - the warning-only behavior here is a description of what's possible today, not a design opinion that blocking is wrong in general. Added `src/lib/attendeeValidation.test.ts` (4 tests: the exact reported 1-ticket/3-guest case, guest count within tickets purchased, zero guests regardless of ticket count, and blank/non-numeric ticket counts) and 3 tests to `AttendeeRow.test.tsx` (the warning text renders with the real numbers; no warning when guests are within the ticket count; no warning for a guest-less row even at 0 tickets). Sanity-checked by stubbing the validation function to always return `false` and confirming all 3 component-level warning tests failed with the real symptom (the mismatch silently rendering with no indication anything was wrong), then restored. Test count: 789 → 796 (427 frontend; 1 new test file, 3 new tests in an existing file). Doesn't touch Apps Script. `tsc --noEmit` clean, full suite 796/796 clean, `npm run build` clean.

- **Fixed a real, user-reported bug affecting the printed bidder-number labels: guests who explicitly opted in for their own bidder number were silently getting no label at all.** ("When I ran the create labels workflow button, the app successfully creates the document... but it fails to create labels for the guests of bidders who have requested to have their own bidder numbers.") Root-caused to a duplicated bug in **both** places that populate the Bid-Management app's `Bidder-Numbers` sheet - exactly the two places the user suspected ("this one may need to be fixed on both the app's end and also via the GAS code"): `server/services/syncService.ts`'s `collectGuestInfo` (the Node "Set Up Bid Management App" sync) and `scripts/GoogleAppsScriptsCode/BidManagementSpreadsheet/Class_BidderManager.gs`'s `_collectGuestInfo_` (the equivalent Apps-Script-native sync, `copyBiddersToBidderSheet()`). Both required a *separate* "guest marker" checkbox (column index 6, distinct from the guest's own name/email/opt-in columns) to be non-blank before a guest was even considered - a column `src/lib/attendeeParsing.ts`'s own guest-inclusion check (the thing that actually drives what the live app shows, including the "Own #" badge) never reads at all. A guest with a real name and an explicit "yes" opt-in, but whose row simply never had that separate checkbox ticked, was silently dropped from `Bidder-Numbers` entirely - and since `TicketsSpreadsheet/Class_BidderManager.gs`'s `getAllBidders()` (the function that actually builds the label document) already correctly treats "not found in Bidder-Numbers" as "not opted in, the normal case" and skips them with no error, the label workflow reported full success while quietly never generating that guest's label. Fixed both `collectGuestInfo` and `_collectGuestInfo_` to drop the guest-marker-column check entirely, gating inclusion on just the two facts that actually matter and that the rest of the app already treats as authoritative: the guest's own name is present, and they explicitly answered "yes". While touching the `.gs` file, also added the same `overrides`-injection testability pattern already used by its `TicketsSpreadsheet` sibling and by `GoogleAIStudio`/`PayPalSweeper` this session (`BidderManager`'s constructor now accepts `activeSpreadSheet`/`config`/`ticketsSpreadsheet` overrides, forwarded to `GlobalVariables`'s own constructor, replacing `SpreadsheetApp.openById(...)` in `getAuctionTicketsSheet_` when given) - this class had zero test coverage of any kind before this fix, on either side, precisely because it had no way to construct one without touching a real spreadsheet. Per the user's explicit follow-up request for regression coverage on both the Node side and "the GAS unit test that lives in the GAS codebase," added three layers: (1) a new test in `server/test/tickets.test.ts`'s existing "Guest Bidder Number Assignment workflow" suite, exercising the real `/api/bidders/sync` route with a guest-marker-blank fixture; (2) a new `server/test/gs-bidmanagement-bidder-manager.test.ts` (6 tests) loading the real `.gs` source via `loadGasClasses` (not a reimplementation) and exercising `_collectGuestTickets_` directly, including the user's exact reported row shape (two guests opted in, one not); (3) a new native Apps Script test file, `scripts/GoogleAppsScriptsCode/BidManagementSpreadsheet/BidderManager_UnitTests.gs` (`runBidderManagerUnitTests`, 5 test groups, reusing `PayPalService_UnitTests.gs`'s assert helper) with its own from-scratch fake Spreadsheet/Sheet supporting the 4-arg `getRange`/`getLastRow`/`getLastColumn` surface this class needs (the existing fakes in `GoogleAIStudioAndSweeper_UnitTests.gs` only supported a narrower 2-arg form built for a different class) - verified for real by loading this exact new file into a throwaway Node `vm` sandbox alongside the real source and running it end-to-end before trusting it, the same discipline already applied to every other native test file in this project, not just a syntax check. Sanity-checked every layer independently by reverting the `.gs`/TS fixes and confirming the real, relevant tests failed with the real symptom (the guest missing from `collectedBidders`/the sync payload) at each of the three layers, then restored. Test count: 796 → 803 (376 backend, 427 frontend; 1 new test file, 1 new test in an existing file) - plus 5 native-tier GAS tests not counted in either total, matching this project's existing convention for native test files. `tsc --noEmit` clean, full suite 803/803 clean, `npm run build` clean. **Reference-copy work only - `Class_BidderManager.gs` and the new `BidderManager_UnitTests.gs` both need to be manually copied into the live Bid-Management Apps Script editor before this fix (or the native tests) take effect there.**

- **Correction to the entry directly above, per the user's own suspicion after re-testing and still seeing the bug**: the `BidManagementSpreadsheet/Class_BidderManager.gs` fix was **not necessary** - `copyBiddersToBidderSheet()` (and therefore `_collectGuestInfo_`) has zero live callers anywhere in this codebase. `menu.gs` in both `BidManagementSpreadsheet/` and `TicketsSpreadsheet/` explicitly disables their custom menus with the same docblock ("the ORNS Auction Manager web app is now the single source of use... manager classes it drove were deleted outright once confirmed to have no other callers"), and a `grep` across the whole repo turns up `copyBiddersToBidderSheet` only inside `Class_BidderManager.gs` itself and test files - the live web app's "Set Up Bid Management App" button only ever calls `server/services/syncService.ts`'s `performSheetSync()` (Node), never this `.gs` method. The fix and its 3 layers of tests are harmless and stay (the class itself is genuinely used elsewhere, via `Class_ReceiptManager.gs`'s `biddersFullData`/`sortByLastName_`, just not this particular method chain), but they were not what fixed - or could have fixed - the reported bug. Double-checked whether `TicketsSpreadsheet/Class_BidderManager.gs`'s `getAllBidders()` (the function that actually builds the label document) has an analogous bug, since that's where the user correctly pointed out the real label-generation code lives (`Class_BidderLabelsDoc.gs`): it does not - `tryAddGuest` was already correct, gating purely on the guest's own name plus a `Bidder-Numbers` lookup, no guest-marker-column dependency at all, and `gs-tickets-bidder-manager.test.ts`'s existing "uses the CORRECTED guest column offsets for guest 2 and guest 3" test already proves a guest present in `Bidder-Numbers` gets correctly matched and included. Confirmed this directly with a full pipeline check: fed the corrected Node `collectGuestInfo` logic's output for the user's exact reported row (Jimmy Smith and Amy Sedaris both opted in) into the real, unmodified `TicketsSpreadsheet/Class_BidderManager.gs` via a throwaway Node `vm` script - both guests came back with real bidder numbers from `getAllBidders()`, proving the two real fixes (the `syncService.ts` one from the entry above, plus this correction) fully close the loop with no code left to fix in `TicketsSpreadsheet/`. No new test added there since there is no bug to guard against - the existing test already covers it. **The most likely reason the user still didn't see guest labels after the first fix: `Bidder-Numbers` was already populated (missing the guests) from a sync that ran *before* the fix, and a code fix doesn't retroactively repair already-written sheet data - "Set Up Bid Management App" needs to be re-run to regenerate it.** Also worth checking: `performSheetSync()` has a guard (`server/services/syncService.ts`, "Sync Guard") that hard-blocks re-syncing once any walk-in bidder has been registered directly (to avoid reassigning a bidder number already in use) - if the test auction has any walk-in registered, re-sync will fail outright until that's addressed, which would independently explain being unable to verify the fix by re-syncing. No code changes in this entry - documentation-only correction.
