- **Fixed a real, user-reported workflow gap, surfaced by testing the "Bidder-Numbers is stale" theory from the entry above to its logical conclusion: bidder labels couldn't be generated at all until "Set Up Bid Management App" had been run first.** ("The app is assuming that the Bid Management sheet needs to be set up before we create the labels for the bidders. But in reality... labels get printed 2-3 days before the auction... the Bidder Management page is never even used until the day of the Auction, one of the last things done.") `TicketsSpreadsheet/Class_BidderManager.gs`'s `getAllBidders()` deliberately hard-errors when `Bidder-Numbers` doesn't exist yet - that's correct and intentional (an earlier fix, so labels are never generated with locally-invented numbers that could drift from the real ones), but it assumed Bid-Management setup always happens *before* label printing, which is backwards from how this auction actually runs. Landed on the user's own proposed fix over two more complex alternatives that were considered and rejected: reimplementing the numbering algorithm a *third* time directly in `TicketsSpreadsheet/Class_BidderManager.gs` (duplicates logic that's already independently drifted apart twice this session), or running the *full* `performSheetSync()` as a hidden side effect of "Create Labels" (would also prematurely lock in `Auction-Items` and `Bid-Management` weeks before the director's own team touches them, while the item catalog is still being actively edited). Instead: extracted the pure `Bidder-Numbers`-building logic out of `performSheetSync()` in `server/services/syncService.ts` into two reusable pieces - `computeBidderNumbersRows` (the deterministic staff→primaries→guest1s→guest2s→guest3s→sponsors numbering transform, now independently unit-tested for the first time) and `writeBidderNumbersSheet` (the actual sheet write + checkbox formatting + header freeze) - and `performSheetSync` itself now just calls them, a pure behavior-preserving refactor (confirmed via the full existing `tickets.test.ts` suite passing unchanged). Built a new, narrower `ensureBidderNumbersSheet(client, spreadsheetId, auctionTicketSheetsID)` on top of those same two shared pieces: if `Bidder-Numbers` already has real data, it's a no-op (**deliberately idempotent** - re-running "Create Labels" later, or the real "Set Up Bid Management App" on the actual auction day, must never reassign a number already printed on a physical label); otherwise it creates the sheet if needed and builds it fresh, using the exact same numbering logic `performSheetSync` uses, so numbers assigned at label-print time are byte-for-byte what a later real sync would independently compute (as long as rows are only appended between the two, never reordered - the same assumption the whole numbering system already rests on). Wired this into `server/routes/productionRoutes.ts`'s `/tickets/bidder-labels` route, called before the existing Apps Script bridge call to `apiCreateBidderLabels` - **zero changes needed to any `.gs` file for this fix**, since `TicketsSpreadsheet/Class_BidderManager.gs` already correctly consumes `Bidder-Numbers` once it exists (verified by the "Correction" entry directly above). `TicketDocumentCreationTab.tsx` now also sends `auctionTicketsSpreadsheetId` (already in scope, just not previously forwarded) alongside the existing `folderId`/`bidManagementSpreadsheetId`, since the route needs it to read the ticket-purchase sheets when it has to build `Bidder-Numbers` from scratch. Added `server/test/syncService.test.ts` (6 tests, first direct unit coverage for this file - previously only exercised indirectly through route tests): the deterministic ordering/ID-sequencing, the user's exact reported row (2 guests opted in, 1 not), exclusion of nameless/non-opted-in guests, dedup, and name/email formatting; plus 4 new tests in `production.test.ts`'s new "`/api/production/tickets/bidder-labels` - builds Bidder-Numbers first when it's missing" block covering the route-level integration: builds from staff/tickets/sponsor rows and still proceeds to call the label Apps Script function, stays a no-op when `Bidder-Numbers` already has data, a real 400 error when the Tickets spreadsheet ID is missing, and the pre-step is skipped entirely when no `bidManagementSpreadsheetId` is given. Sanity-checked the full route wiring by reverting it and confirming the 2 relevant new route tests failed with the real symptom (labels "succeeding" with `Bidder-Numbers` never touched, and a request that should 400 instead returning 200), then restored; separately sanity-checked the new unit tests aren't vacuous by breaking `computeBidderNumbersRows`' starting ID and confirming exactly the 3 ID-asserting tests failed, then restored. Test count: 803 → 813 (386 backend, 427 frontend; 1 new test file, 4 new tests in an existing file). Doesn't touch Apps Script. `tsc --noEmit` clean, full suite 813/813 clean, `npm run build` clean.

- **Closed `docs/agents/ROADMAP.md`'s "Concurrency Safety for Simultaneous Bidder Operations" milestone: audited every bidder-mutating write path against real concurrent-access risk and fixed the two that had it.** Prompted directly by the user's own stated fear - "this always relied on my ability to manage this workflow without making a mistake" from the pre-app, 3-disconnected-spreadsheets era - and their explicit goal: more than one staff member, on separate computers, both working check-in and checkout at once, for an auction that's this preschool's primary annual revenue source. Documented the full plan (with pseudocode) in `ROADMAP.md` *before* writing any code, per the user's explicit request, so nothing got lost partway through a 3-part fix; removed from `ROADMAP.md` now that it's done, per this repo's existing convention of keeping that file to only outstanding milestones.

  Audited three functions: **`registerWalkInBidder`** (`server/services/bidderService.ts` - the originally-reported gap) computes `newId = maxId + 1` from a fresh read of `Bidder-Numbers`, and separately computes the next open `Bid-Management` column pair from a fresh read - two walk-ins registered within the same read/write window (realistic the moment more than one person works the door) could compute the *same* ID and the *same* columns, with the second write silently clobbering the first's name and pre-recorded ticket-price bid. **`recordWinningBid`** (`server/services/auctionService.ts` - found during this audit, not originally reported) has the identical shape one level in: finds the winning bidder's own column (safe, identity-based), then scans down it for the first empty row from a fresh read - if the *same bidder* wins two items and two different staff record them within the same window (a normal, good outcome at a live auction, not an edge case), the second recorded win silently overwrites the first, a direct, silent revenue loss with no error surfaced anywhere. **`checkoutMarkPaid`** (`server/services/auctionService.ts`) was audited and found already safe on its own against both scenarios the user asked about directly (two computers checking out the same bidder; two computers checking out different bidders) - `Class_GoogleAIStudio.gs`'s `recordPayment` finds its target row by scanning for the bidder's own ID, not a computed slot, and every payment-method branch writes a specific final value, so concurrent calls are naturally idempotent.

  Fixed with one new, reusable primitive: `server/utils/asyncLock.ts`'s `withLock(key, fn)` - a promise-chain mutex, since Google Sheets has no row/range locking of its own and this app is a single Node process (not horizontally scaled), so no new infrastructure is needed. Wrapped all three functions' *entire* bodies (renamed to `*Locked` inner functions) behind `withLock(`bidder-numbers:${spreadsheetId}`, ...)` - one lock per auction's Bid-Management spreadsheet, shared across all three, so none of them can interleave with each other either, not just with themselves. `checkoutMarkPaid` got the same wrapper purely as defense-in-depth (cheap insurance, keeps every bidder-mutating path protected by the identical rule) even though the audit found it didn't need it. Because the lock is held across `checkoutMarkPaid`'s *entire* Apps Script bridge round-trip (`script.scripts.run`), it also fully serializes the underlying `.gs` execution - confirmed no `LockService` addition was needed on that side.

  Caught and fixed a real bug in the lock implementation itself while writing its own test suite: the first version stored `previousTail.then(() => thisTail)` in the lock map instead of `thisTail` directly - functionally harmless for serialization ordering (the wrapped promise still resolves at the same time via promise-chain flattening), but it broke cleanup, since `locks.get(key) === thisTail` could never be true, silently leaking one Map entry per key forever. Caught only because a dedicated test asserted the map's actual size via a new test-only `_getLockMapSizeForTesting` export, not just the outwardly-observable behavior (which was identical either way) - a good reminder that "the test passes" isn't the same as "the test would catch the bug."

  Testing, three layers: (1) `server/test/asyncLock.test.ts` (6 tests) for the primitive itself - same-key calls serialize, different keys never block each other, a thrown error still releases the lock for the next caller, return values propagate, and the map cleanup bug described above. (2) `server/test/bidderService.test.ts` and a new `describe` in `server/test/auctionService.test.ts`, each with a hand-rolled fake Sheets client carrying *genuinely shared, mutable* state (not per-call canned responses, which couldn't expose a real race) - `registerWalkInBidder`'s test fires two concurrent walk-ins and asserts they land on two different real IDs and two different `Bid-Management` column ranges; `recordWinningBid`'s test fires two concurrent wins for the *same* bidder and asserts both items end up recorded, neither overwriting the other. (3) `checkoutMarkPaid`'s concurrency test needed a different approach - it bridges to Apps Script via `google.script().scripts.run()` rather than taking a Sheets client directly - so it mocks the `googleapis` module itself and asserts call *ordering* (bidder 99's Apps Script call must not start until bidder 42's, held open on a controllable gate, fully finishes) rather than a business-state outcome, which was already proven safe by the audit. A first attempt at this same ordering test at the HTTP/route level via `supertest` was abandoned after it passed even with the lock removed - supertest's request dispatch against an unlistened `app` didn't reliably expose real interleaving, so this needed genuine service-level control instead; the abandoned route-level test was deleted rather than left in as a false-confidence check.

  Sanity-checked every one of the 4 real fixes (the lock's own cleanup bug plus the 3 service wrappers) independently, the same way: reverted just that one fix, confirmed the corresponding test failed with the real symptom (a duplicate bidder ID; an overwritten winning bid; bidder 99's checkout call starting before bidder 42's finished; the lock map never shrinking), then restored. Test count: 813 → 822 (395 backend, 427 frontend; 4 new test files - `asyncLock.test.ts`, `bidderService.test.ts`, plus new `describe` blocks in `auctionService.test.ts` - no prior file existed for either service). Purely backend/data-integrity - no new frontend components, no change to `src/`; per the user's explicit reminder to follow `performance.md`/`react-patterns.md`/`component-standards.md`, confirmed there's nothing in those docs this touches, since none of it is UI. Doesn't touch Apps Script. `tsc --noEmit` clean, full suite 822/822 clean, `npm run build` clean.

### Changed

- Moved `README.md` to `/docs/agents/README.md` to consolidate the overall system architecture guidance.
- Moved `USER_GUIDE.md` to `/docs/agents/USER_GUIDE.md` and updated structural references to point staff and operators directly to the new directory.
- Merged all modular directory lists and metadata links from the redundant `/docs/agents/AGENTS.md` file back into the primary root `/AGENTS.md` index file, and safely deleted `/docs/agents/AGENTS.md` to eliminate configuration duplication.
- Strengthened mock-assertion validation for Gemini genAI endpoints inside `/server/test/bundle.test.ts`. Converted three CIRCULAR test cases ("AI name suggestions — happy path", "AI description suggestion — happy path", and "Happy path — returns AI response text") into GOOD test cases asserting deep prompt checks.
- Cleaned up obsolete tests in `/server/test/items.test.ts` to maintain high-quality testing standards (removed UI-only canceled action checking and empty single-item selection checks).
- Updated documentation across `AGENTS.md` to accurately reflect a test count of 321 passing tests (233 backend, 88 frontend).
- Split `src/components/AuctionItemsTab.tsx` (969 lines) into `AuctionItemsFilterBar.tsx`, `AuctionItemsTable.tsx`, `AuctionItemRow.tsx`, `AuctionItemsBulkActionBar.tsx`, `AddAuctionItemDialog.tsx`, `hooks/useAuctionItemsTableState.ts`, and `lib/auctionItemPricing.ts` (177 lines remaining in the container). All 10 existing `AuctionItemsTab.test.tsx` tests pass unmodified; full suite 321/321, `npm run build` clean.
- Split `src/components/Bidders.tsx` (622 lines) into `BiddersTable.tsx`, `BidderRow.tsx`, `BiddersFilterControls.tsx`, `WalkInTicketDialog.tsx`, `PendingPaymentDialog.tsx`, `hooks/useBiddersState.ts`, and `lib/bidderAttendeeInfo.ts` (107 lines remaining in the container). The pending-payment `AlertDialog` was deliberately kept as its own component rather than migrated to the shared `ConfirmationDialog` - "No, Do Not Add" still checks the bidder in (just without the receipt addition), while closing the dialog does neither, and `ConfirmationDialog`'s cancel-just-closes API can't express that distinction safely. Also added a real `useMemo` around the filter/sort (previously recomputed on every render, including a per-row `getBidderAttendeeInfo()` array scan over `attendees` - a genuine O(n×m) cost, unlike the lighter unmemoized filters elsewhere in the app this doc's audit had noted as correctly-not-memoized). All 21 existing `Bidders.test.tsx` tests pass unmodified; full suite 321/321, `npm run build` clean.
- Split `src/components/Checkout.tsx` (648 lines) into `WinnerCard.tsx`, `WinnerGrid.tsx` (also de-duplicated the verbatim-identical loading/empty/card-map block that used to be hand-copied once per tab), `PaymentMethodDialog.tsx`, and `hooks/useCheckoutState.ts` (141 lines remaining in the container). Migrated the "Print All Receipts" and "Email All Winners" confirmations to the shared `ConfirmationDialog` - a real fix, not just deduplication: their confirm buttons had no loading state at all despite `isPrintingAll`/`isEmailingAll` already existing, so `ConfirmationDialog` gained an optional `confirmClassName` prop (to preserve the "Yes, Print" button's brand-red styling) and both dialogs now show a spinner while the batch job is in flight. Also added a `useMemo` around the pending/completed/search-filtered winners derivation (previously three `.filter()` passes over the full winners list on every render regardless of cause). Fixed the last 2 remaining `bg-[#e31b23]`/`text-[#e31b23]` occurrences in the app while touching those exact lines - the "hardcoded hex instead of the `orns-red` token" finding is now fully resolved codebase-wide and removed from this file. All 5 existing `Checkout.test.tsx` tests pass unmodified, including against the real (unmocked) `Dialog` component; full suite 321/321, `npm run build` clean.
- Split `src/components/Dashboard.tsx` (517 lines) into `PostAuctionFollowUp.tsx` with a new `FollowUpListPanel.tsx` (de-duplicated three near-identical hand-copied list panels - Pending Checkouts, Follow-Up Records, No Shows - into one reusable header/count-badge/scroll-list/empty-message wrapper), `DashboardStatsRow.tsx`, `AttendanceChart.tsx`, `ProxyBiddersCard.tsx`, a new `SpecialWinnerField.tsx` (de-duplicated the gift-card and 50/50-raffle prize fields, previously two near-identical hand-copied blocks) plus `SpecialWinnersCard.tsx`, and `hooks/useDashboardState.ts` (57 lines remaining in the container). Also: migrated the two Special Winners "Save" buttons to `LoadingButton` (part of that item's already-logged broader follow-up); fixed the `text-[#10b981]` hardcodes to Tailwind's built-in `emerald-500` (an exact color match, no new token needed) and the `text-[#0c63d5]`/`bg-[#0c63d5]` hardcodes by adding a new `--color-accent-blue` token to `src/index.css` (following the same pattern as the existing `orns-red` token) - the "hardcoded hex with no token" finding is now fully resolved and removed from `refactor-opportunities.md`; and dropped an unused `loading` destructure from `useAuction()` that was never referenced anywhere in the original file. All 6 existing `Dashboard.test.tsx` tests pass unmodified, including the one that exercises `PostAuctionFollowUp`'s real content; full suite 321/321, `npm run build` clean.
- Split `src/components/TicketAttendeeTab.tsx` (451 lines) into `AttendeeRow.tsx`, `AttendeesTable.tsx` (migrated to `DataTableWrapper`, replacing a full centered loading spinner with skeleton rows, matching the `Items.tsx`/`Bidders.tsx` precedent), `AddAttendeeDialog.tsx`, `AttendeeEditableCell.tsx` (the file's local, file-scoped inline editor, renamed and extracted - not merged with the shared `ui/EditableCell.tsx`, since the two have genuinely different Cancel/Escape behavior; logged as its own follow-up in `refactor-opportunities.md`), and `hooks/useTicketAttendeeState.ts` (107 lines remaining in the container). The toolbar was migrated to `FilterToolbar` and the delete confirmation to `ConfirmationDialog`. Also fixed while splitting: a raw unstyled `<button>` (the inline edit-pencil trigger) that was the only one of its kind anywhere in `src/components/` - had no `aria-label` at all and wasn't caught by the earlier icon-button audit because that audit specifically searched for `<Button size="icon">`, not raw `<button>` elements; migrated to `IconButton`. Added a `useMemo` around the search/source filter (previously recomputed on every render). Also fixed 2 more manual-template-literal classNames (this file's own `EditableCell` and the Refresh button), closing that finding down to 3 remaining files. Found (but didn't fix, logged instead): `AddAttendeeDialog`'s "Save Entry" button has no loading state at all - unlike `Checkout.tsx`'s confirm buttons, there was no existing state to wire up here, so it's a real gap, not just a migration. All 16 existing `TicketAttendeeTab.test.tsx` tests pass unmodified, including the ones asserting exact `mock-select` DOM order between the toolbar's filter select and the dialog's source select; full suite 321/321, `npm run build` clean.
- Split `src/context/AuctionContext.tsx` (532 lines) into domain-scoped hooks - `hooks/useApiAuthStatus.ts` (OAuth status polling and `postMessage` listener), `hooks/useBiddersData.ts` (bidders list, check-in/proxy mutations), `hooks/useItemsData.ts`, `hooks/useAttendeesData.ts`, `hooks/useWinnersData.ts` (takes bidders as an argument, since winner names are resolved from the bidder list), and `hooks/useAuctionStatusData.ts` (auction-closed flag and the close-and-cleanup request) - plus `lib/bidderParsing.ts`, `lib/itemParsing.ts`, and `lib/attendeeParsing.ts` for the three sheet-row parsers, each verified field-by-field against `src/types.ts`. The provider itself now only composes these hooks and assembles the public `AuctionContextType` value, which is unchanged and was not touched. `useWinnersData.fetchWinners` was left circularly-dependent-free by having `closeAuction`'s cleanup hook (`requestCloseAuction`) return a `Promise<boolean>` instead of calling `fetchAllData` itself, since `fetchAllData` needs `fetchAuctionStatus` from the same hook `closeAuction` lives in; the provider composes the two by calling `fetchAllData()` only after a successful `requestCloseAuction()`, matching the original's success-only refetch behavior exactly. The single shared `loading` boolean (originally flipped by both `fetchBidders`'s try/finally and `fetchAllData`'s own upfront `setLoading(true)`) was preserved structurally rather than simplified: `useBiddersData` exports its internal `setLoading` alongside `loading`, so the provider's `fetchAllData` still calls it directly before the `Promise.all`, exactly as the original did. This file has zero existing automated test coverage - every consumer test (`Dashboard.test.tsx`, `Checkout.test.tsx`, `Bidders.test.tsx`, etc.) mocks `@/context/AuctionContext` via `vi.mock()` at the module path, so none of this file's real code has ever run under the 321-test suite, before or after this split; extra manual verification (a full read-through of every new hook against the original file, line by line, plus a `tsc --noEmit` pass) was applied in place of that missing coverage. Full suite 321/321 (unaffected, as expected), `npm run build` clean.
- Split `src/components/ConfigSettings.tsx` (572 lines) into `CloudSyncStatusCard.tsx`, `OAuthRedirectHelperCard.tsx`, `SetupNewAuctionCard.tsx`, `AuctionConfigurationCard.tsx`, `TicketPricingCard.tsx`, `ManualCopyDialog.tsx`, and `hooks/useConfigSettingsState.ts` (67 lines remaining in the container). `OAuthRedirectHelperCard` was kept as-is even though its parent `Card` renders with a permanent `hidden` class - it's still present in the DOM (just visually hidden), unlike the two functions below, so removing it would have been a product decision, not a refactor. Found and removed while splitting: the `initializing` state and the `syncSettingsFromCloud`/`initializeSheets` functions were genuinely dead code - declared but never referenced by any JSX element anywhere in the original file (no button called them, and `initializing` was never read after being set), unlike `OAuthRedirectHelperCard`'s handlers which are at least wired to a real (hidden) button. This is now the last remaining oversized-component entry resolved; the "Component size" section of `refactor-opportunities.md` is now empty and has been removed. All 5 existing `ConfigSettings.test.tsx` tests pass unmodified; full suite 321/321, `npm run build` clean.
- Converted every remaining manual template-literal `className` to `cn()` in `BidEntry.tsx`, `AuctionAssistant.tsx` (4 occurrences, including a multi-line conditional), and `CloudSyncStatusCard.tsx` (the file `ConfigSettings.tsx`'s original occurrence moved into during the prior `ConfigSettings.tsx` split). Also fixed `App.tsx`, which the doc's original 3-file list missed entirely - re-grepping turned up 8 more occurrences there (the sidebar `<aside>` and its collapse/expand toggles, 4 near-identical nav-button className strings, and the sign-out button), all now using `cn()`. The "Manual template-literal classNames instead of `cn()`" finding is now fully resolved codebase-wide and removed from `refactor-opportunities.md`. All tests pass unmodified; full suite 321/321, `npm run build` clean.
- Moved `src/lib/google-api.ts` and `src/lib/firebase.ts` to a new `src/services/` directory, formalizing the `lib → services → hooks → components` layering `react-patterns.md` describes (previously only true on the Express backend). `src/lib/` now holds only genuinely pure code (`categoryHelpers.ts`, `utils.ts`, and the `*Parsing.ts`/`*Pricing.ts`/`*Info.ts` helpers); `google-api.ts` (network calls, `localStorage`, retry/backoff) and `firebase.ts` (live SDK client init) are impure integration clients and now live where the layering says they should. Updated all 28 real import sites (21 components/hooks + `App.tsx`) and 7 test files' path-based `vi.mock("@/lib/google-api", ...)` mocks to `@/services/google-api` (one file, `ConfigContext.tsx`, similarly updated for `@/services/firebase`) - a pure path rename, no code changes to either moved file. All tests pass unmodified against the new mock paths; full suite 321/321, `npm run build` clean (identical output chunk sizes).
- Split the catch-all `vendor-utils` build chunk (`vite.config.ts`'s `manualChunks`) by giving `firebase`/`@firebase/*` and `@base-ui/react` their own named chunks (`vendor-firebase`, `vendor-base-ui`), the same pattern already used for `vendor-react`/`vendor-recharts`/`vendor-motion`/`vendor-lucide`. `vendor-utils` dropped from 851 kB raw / 238 kB gzipped to 264 kB / 86 kB; the two split-out chunks are 450 kB / 106 kB (`vendor-firebase`) and 137 kB / 46 kB (`vendor-base-ui`). This makes per-dependency bundle growth attributable in future builds and lets the browser cache `vendor-firebase`/`vendor-base-ui` independently of app-code changes between deploys - it does not reduce the actual initial JS payload (~424 kB gzipped, essentially unchanged from ~421 kB), since chunk splitting alone doesn't remove bytes from the critical path. Updated `style_guide.md`'s Bundle Optimization table with the new real measured numbers, and logged the real next optimization (lazy-loading `firebase`'s init, now the single largest chunk, behind the cloud-sync UI that's its only consumer) as a new item in `refactor-opportunities.md`, replacing the now-resolved "vendor-utils is oversized" finding. `tsc --noEmit` clean; full suite 321/321, `npm run build` clean.
- Merged `src/components/AttendeeEditableCell.tsx` into the shared `src/components/ui/EditableCell.tsx`, resolving the "two different EditableCell implementations" finding per an explicit decision to merge rather than keep both. `EditableCell` now accepts `onSave: (val) => void | Promise<void>` (shows a saving spinner and disables the input/Select/Textarea while an async save is in flight - previously only synchronous saves existed), skips calling `onSave` entirely if the value is unchanged (a real improvement: avoids no-op sheet writes, previously only `AttendeeEditableCell` did this), and gained two new opt-in props to preserve `AttendeeEditableCell`'s exact collapsed-state look for its narrow table-cell use: `placeholder` (custom empty-state text, e.g. "Guest Email" instead of the default "Empty") and `singleLine` (truncate-with-title-tooltip display and a smaller `icon-xs` edit button, instead of the default wrap-and-line-clamp display sized for wider cells like item descriptions). `AttendeeRow.tsx`'s 5 call sites were migrated to `EditableCell value={...} ... singleLine`; `AttendeeEditableCell.tsx` was deleted. One deliberate behavior change from the merge: `AttendeeRow.tsx`'s inline editors now show an explicit Cancel button during edit (previously Escape-to-revert only, no visible Cancel) - the two implementations' edit-mode UI is now identical everywhere, per the chosen "merge into ui/EditableCell" approach. `tsc --noEmit` clean; full suite 321/321 (no test exercises the inline-edit interaction directly, but `AttendeesTable`/`AttendeeRow` render correctly against real data in `TicketAttendeeTab.test.tsx`); `npm run build` clean.
- Removed the "migrate `BundlingDialog.tsx`'s ad hoc confirmation dialog(s)" item from `refactor-opportunities.md` without changing code - it didn't hold up on inspection. `BundlingDialog.tsx` is a full data-entry form dialog (bundle name/category/value/description fields, an AI-suggestion button, a Cancel/Confirm footer), not a confirmation dialog; its `ConfirmDialogFooter` shape has no room in `ConfirmationDialog`'s `title`/`description`/`confirmLabel`/`cancelLabel` API for arbitrary form content, and it's structurally identical to `AddAttendeeDialog.tsx` and `AddAuctionItemDialog.tsx`, neither of which use `ConfirmationDialog` either - this app's established pattern is `ConfirmationDialog` for lightweight yes/no confirms, plain `Dialog`/`DialogFooter` for form-entry dialogs. Also checked `useBundling.ts`'s bundle-restore flow (`handleRestoreBundle`) for a hand-rolled confirm step that might have been the real target of this finding - there isn't one; it fires immediately on click, no dialog at all. No code changes needed.
- Migrated the last 6 hand-rolled `{loading ? <Loader2 /> : ...}` busy buttons to `LoadingButton`, closing out that finding codebase-wide: `SetupNewAuctionCard.tsx`'s "Run New Auction Setup", `ConfigSettings.tsx`'s "Save Configuration", `AddAuctionItemDialog.tsx`'s "Save Item", and `BundlingDialog.tsx`'s 3 buttons ("Ask Gemini for Ideas", "Ask Gemini for Description", "Confirm Bundle & Merge Rows"). Also removed an already-dead `Check` icon import from `BundlingDialog.tsx` while touching that same import line. `tsc --noEmit` clean; full suite 321/321, `npm run build` clean.
- Lazy-loaded Firebase's init, removing it from the app's initial JS bundle. `src/services/firebase.ts` now exposes a single memoized `getFirebase()` that dynamically imports `firebase/app`, `firebase/auth`, `firebase/firestore`, and the local `firebase-applet-config.json` on first call, instead of the old top-level synchronous `initializeApp()`/`getAuth()`/`getFirestore()` calls; `ConfigContext.tsx`'s three real Firebase call sites (`onAuthStateChanged`/`onSnapshot` in the mount effect, `loginToFirebase`, `saveConfig`) now `await getFirebase()` and destructure the specific named functions they need (kept as named bindings rather than re-exporting the whole `firebase/auth`/`firebase/firestore` module namespaces, to give Rollup the best remaining chance to tree-shake). Measured with a temporary `rollup-plugin-visualizer` pass (installed with `--no-save`, removed after): the app's critical-path JS dropped from ~424 kB to ~319 kB gzipped (`vendor-firebase`'s 106 kB gzipped is no longer part of the initial bundle), moving it much closer to `performance.md`'s <200 kB target, though still above the 300 kB warning threshold. Real trade-off found and kept, not hidden: `vendor-firebase` itself grew once deferred (106 kB → 160 kB gzipped, 450 kB → 691 kB raw) - neither `@firebase/auth` nor `@firebase/firestore` declares `"sideEffects": false`, so Rollup loses some export-level tree-shaking across the async `import()` boundary that it had when everything was one static module graph. Checked `firebase/firestore/lite` as an alternative; not viable, since it's REST-only and this app's config sync depends on `onSnapshot`'s real-time listener. Net effect: users who never touch cloud sync pay zero Firebase cost (down from ~106 kB gzipped unconditionally); users who do trigger it pay more than before, but only then. Also dropped `handleFirestoreError`/`OperationType`/`FirestoreErrorInfo` from `firebase.ts` - fully dead code (zero callers anywhere in the codebase) that would otherwise have needed an awkward async rewrite to keep working with no benefit - and an already-unused `getDoc` import from `ConfigContext.tsx`. `tsc --noEmit` clean; full suite 321/321 (this file has zero direct test coverage - every consumer test mocks `@/context/ConfigContext` - so the async control flow was manually re-verified line-by-line against the original instead); `npm run build` clean, `git status` confirms only `src/services/firebase.ts` and `src/context/ConfigContext.tsx` changed.
- Added a submitting/loading state to `AddAttendeeDialog`'s "Save Entry" button. `hooks/useTicketAttendeeState.ts`'s `handleAddSubmit` now tracks a new `isSubmitting` boolean (set around the existing `addAttendee` call, following the same `try`/`finally` shape as this same hook's `handleDeleteConfirm`/`isDeleting`) and returns it; `AddAttendeeDialog.tsx` takes a new `isSubmitting` prop and its "Save Entry" button is now a `LoadingButton` instead of a plain `Button`, disabling and showing a spinner while the add is in flight - matching the loading-state pattern this dialog was the last one missing. All 16 existing `TicketAttendeeTab.test.tsx` tests pass unmodified, including the 6 that click "Save Entry"; full suite 321/321, `npm run build` clean.
- Deleted the orphaned `/api/assistant` backend route: `server/services/assistantService.ts` (its only usage), the route handler and its now-unused `genAI`/`handleAssistantRequest` imports in `server/routes/dataRoutes.ts`, and the 5-test "Gemini Assistant workflow" `describe` block in `server/test/bundle.test.ts`. User's call after weighing the tradeoff (small, isolated, tested code with no real cost to keep vs. a genuinely dead endpoint with no caller since `AuctionAssistant.tsx` was deleted) - decided to delete rather than keep "just in case," on the reasoning that resurrecting ~30 lines later would be trivial if a real chat feature ever gets scoped. Also cleaned up the now-inaccurate "Gemini Assistant" framing in that test file's header comment and its `genai` mock's default-case comment (the mock's array-shaped-`contents` fallback branch was specifically for the assistant's message-history format), and removed `assistantService.ts` from `file-reference.md`'s services listing, and a stale `src/lib/firebase.ts` reference in `AGENTS.md` (that file moved to `src/services/firebase.ts` in an earlier refactor - fixed while touching that same line). Test count dropped 321 → 316 (233 → 228 backend); updated the count everywhere it's asserted: `AGENTS.md`, `docs/agents/README.md`, `docs/agents/testing-batches.md`. `tsc --noEmit` clean; full suite 316/316, `npm run build` clean (`dist/server.cjs` shrank 118.2kb → 116.6kb); `git status` confirms only the intended files changed.
- Ran a deep code audit (2026-09-02) across `server/`, `src/components/`, `src/hooks/`/`src/lib/`/`src/services/`/`src/context/`, and test coverage against `testing-philosophy.md`'s own rating system, using parallel research passes then independently re-verifying the highest-severity claims by hand (confirmed real: the bundle `minRaise` silently dropped in `bundleService.ts`, `useCheckoutState`'s stuck refresh spinner, `WinnerCard`'s dead `-pay` loading condition, `ConfigContext`'s Firestore listener leak, `AuctionContext`'s two dead exported fetchers, the byte-identical `resequenceCategoryIds` duplication, the missing OAuth `postMessage` origin check, and `useBundling`'s un-awaited fetch letting the bundle button be double-submitted). Recorded ~35 findings into `refactor-opportunities.md`'s "Tasks To Refactor" section, organized into Correctness bugs, Dead code, Redundant/duplicated logic, Code quality, and Test coverage gaps (the last added per an explicit request to also assess test coverage against `testing-philosophy.md`'s guidelines mid-audit). Also corrected one now-stale "Passed Audit Inspection" claim ("test assertions aren't shallow placeholders") that this audit's own findings contradict (several SHALLOW-rated tests were found: `ConfigSettings.test.tsx`, `Checkout.test.tsx`, `Receipts.test.tsx`, `production.test.ts`) - narrowed it to the specific bare-`toHaveBeenCalled()` claim that still holds, with a pointer to the new findings. This entry records the audit itself, not fixes - no code was changed; each backlog item will be verified and fixed individually in follow-up work, matching how every prior audit in this project has been worked through.
- Removed `fetchItems`/`fetchWinners` from `AuctionContextType` and the provider's `value` object in `src/context/AuctionContext.tsx` - confirmed by repo-wide grep that no component ever calls `useAuction().fetchItems`/`.fetchWinners`. Both functions themselves are kept and still used internally (`fetchAllData` still calls `itemsData.fetchItems()`; the winners-refetch `useEffect` still calls `winnersData.fetchWinners()`) - only the dead public-API exposure was removed. `tsc --noEmit` clean; full suite 316/316, `npm run build` clean.
- Extracted the byte-identical `resequenceCategoryIds` function (previously duplicated verbatim in `server/services/itemService.ts` and `server/services/bundleService.ts`) into `server/utils/categoryHelpers.ts`, alongside `getCategoryBase`, the function it calls and that both files already imported from there. Both services now import `resequenceCategoryIds` from the shared helper instead of defining their own copy. `tsc --noEmit` clean; full suite 316/316, `npm run build` clean (`dist/server.cjs` shrank slightly, confirming the dedup).
- Collapsed `useAuctionItemsTableState.ts`'s unreachable bulk-field-column ternary (`bulkField === 'itemType' ? 'I' : 'J'`) to the constant `'I'` with an explanatory comment - `bulkField`'s type is `"category" | "itemType"` and the `'category'` case already returns earlier in `executeBulkUpdate`, so the `'J'` branch could never actually execute. `tsc --noEmit` clean; full suite 316/316, `npm run build` clean.
- Removed the dead `paymentMethod` field from `useCheckoutState.ts`'s `WinnerRecord` interface and its only construction site - it was always hardcoded to `""` and never read by `WinnerCard`, `WinnerGrid`, or `PaymentMethodDialog`, which all use a separate, unrelated `paymentMethod` piece of state for the actual payment-method-selection flow. `WinnerRecord` has no other consumers beyond type annotations, so this was a pure dead-field removal. `tsc --noEmit` clean; full suite 316/316, `npm run build` clean.
- Stopped exposing `checkAuth` from `useGoogleAuth.ts`'s return value - `App.tsx` (its only consumer) destructured it but never called it anywhere, confirmed by grep. `checkAuth` itself is unchanged and still used internally by the hook (its own mount effect, the `postMessage` handler, the fallback poll, and `handleLogout`) - only the dead external exposure was removed. `tsc --noEmit` clean; full suite 316/316, `npm run build` clean.
- Deleted `src/components/OAuthRedirectHelperCard.tsx`, per the user's decision on the "permanently hidden card" finding (unconditional `<Card className="hidden">`, never showable regardless of state). Removed its render call from `ConfigSettings.tsx` and the now-unused `redirectUri`/`copySuccess`/`copyToClipboard` state and handler from `useConfigSettingsState.ts`, which existed only to feed that card's props. If the OAuth redirect URI is needed again, it's `${window.location.origin}/auth/callback` - one line, not a resurrected UI. `tsc --noEmit` clean; full suite 316/316 (`ConfigSettings.test.tsx` never referenced this always-hidden card), `npm run build` clean.
- Removed unused `Badge` imports from `DocumentCreationTab.tsx` and `TicketDocumentCreationTab.tsx` - confirmed neither file's JSX ever renders it. This was the last item in the deep audit's "Dead code" subsection, which is now empty and removed. `tsc --noEmit` clean; full suite 316/316, `npm run build` clean.
- Fixed a critical checkout payment-method vocabulary mismatch between this app and the live Apps Script bound to the Bid-Management spreadsheet, found while investigating the user's request to test the checkout/PayPal workflow and reading the real `.gs` source they provided (`scripts/GoogleAppsScriptsCode/`). `useCheckoutState.ts`'s `confirmMarkPaid` sent `"CASH"`/`"CHECK"`/`"PAYPAL"` (uppercase) to `/api/auction/checkout-mark-paid`, which the server passed through verbatim as the Apps Script bridge call's second parameter; but the live `Class_GoogleAIStudio.gs`'s `recordPayment()` does an exact-case match against lowercase `"cash"`/`"check"`/`"credit_card"` and throws `"Unknown checkout payment method specified"` for anything else - confirmed against the team's own manual test file (`API_PAYPAL_TESTS.gs`), which calls `recordPayment` with lowercase `'credit_card'`. This meant every checkout-mark-paid call from this app, for any payment method, very likely failed and rolled back in production. Fixed on the Node side per the user's direction (the Apps Script vocabulary is the one already exercised by real invoices): `useCheckoutState.ts`'s `mappedMethod` now produces `"cash"`/`"check"`/`"credit_card"`, and the two server-side default fallbacks (`bidRoutes.ts:90`, `auctionService.ts:193`, both used when `paymentMethod` is missing from the request) changed from `"CASH"` to `"cash"` to match. This bug existed at the Node↔Apps-Script contract boundary, which is why the extensive existing backend tests (`bid-management.test.ts`, `tickets.test.ts`) never caught it - they call the API directly with hardcoded lowercase `paymentMethod` values, bypassing the real client's (buggy) mapping entirely. Logged three related, not-yet-fixed findings surfaced by the same investigation in `refactor-opportunities.md`: a receipt-vs-actual-charge fee-calculation mismatch, a likely-broken invoice-ID regex in the background PayPal-verification sweeper, and a stale duplicate of the bridge class in the repo. `tsc --noEmit` clean; full suite 316/316 (unaffected - no existing test exercises the client-side mapping this bug lived in), `npm run build` clean.
- Fixed two more confirmed bugs in the live PayPal checkout workflow's Apps Script reference copy (`scripts/GoogleAppsScriptsCode/`), found continuing the checkout investigation with real Bidder-Numbers sheet data the user provided. **Note: these are fixes to this repo's reference copy only - they do not change the live deployed script until manually synced back into the Apps Script editor.** (1) `PayPalSweeper.gs`'s invoice-ID extraction only matched PayPal's sandbox URL shape (`#inv-XXXX-...`) via a narrow inline regex, never the production shape (`#INV2-XXXX-...`) - confirmed against real Column J values (3 of 4 sample rows were production `INV2-` URLs, 1 was a sandbox `inv-` test row), meaning the background "keep checking until it's paid" sweeper likely never reconciled a single real production row. Fixed by hardening `Class_PayPalService.gs`'s `extractInvoiceIdFromUrl` to handle both shapes (normalizing sandbox's `inv-` prefix to the API's `INV2-` form) and having the sweeper call that shared method instead of its own narrower regex - eliminating the duplicate extraction logic in the same move. (2) `Class_GoogleAIStudio.gs`'s `recordPayment()` called `paypal.recordExternalPayment(invoiceId, label)` for cash/check payments, but `PayPalService` only ever defined `recordOfflinePayment` - the nonexistent-method call threw a `TypeError` that was silently swallowed by the surrounding `try/catch` (which logs a warning and returns `paypalRecorded: false`), so every cash/check checkout correctly marked the bidder paid in the sheet but never actually marked the corresponding PayPal invoice as paid via the API - it would sit open in PayPal's own dashboard indefinitely. Also found while fixing: the call site never captured Column K's invoiced amount at all, so even with the right method name it couldn't have supplied `recordOfflinePayment`'s required `totalAmount` argument. Fixed by capturing Column K in the row lookup and calling `recordOfflinePayment(invoiceId, label, amount)` correctly. Logged three related open items in `refactor-opportunities.md`: the confirmed-stale `scripts/API-BidManagement.gs` duplicate (user's go-ahead needed before deleting), two never-instantiated data-model classes (`AuctionItem`, `Bidder`), and whether the sweeper's trigger is actually registered live (unconfirmable from source alone).
- Deleted `scripts/API-BidManagement.gs`, per the user's go-ahead - confirmed stale: it duplicated the same `class GoogleAIStudio`/`recordPayment` as `scripts/GoogleAppsScriptsCode/Class_GoogleAIStudio.gs`, which the user confirmed is what's actually deployed for the Bid-Management spreadsheet, with materially different (and worse - no real PayPal verification) behavior. Kept `scripts/API-Items.gs` and `scripts/API-Tickets.gs` - traced every Apps Script bridge function this app actually calls (`server/routes/productionRoutes.ts`) and confirmed these two are the only reference copies for the Item-Entry and Tickets spreadsheets' scripts respectively; nothing in `GoogleAppsScriptsCode/` (Bid-Management only) supersedes them.
- Disabled the Bid-Management spreadsheet's custom "Auction" menu (`scripts/GoogleAppsScriptsCode/menu.gs`'s `onOpen`/`onInstall` are now no-ops), per the user's direction that this app is now the single source of use for running an auction and the spreadsheet should be data storage only. `createCustomMenu()` and the manager classes it drove (`Class_CheckInManager.gs`, `Class_CheckOutManager.gs`, `Class_BidEntryManager.gs`, `Class_AuctionSetupManager.gs`, `Class_SettingsManager.gs`) are left defined but now fully unreachable in the live script - logged as a new `refactor-opportunities.md` item (delete outright vs. keep as inert fallback code is the user's call). Confirmed via a screenshot the user shared that `PayPalSweeper.gs`'s background trigger is live (`checkPendingPayPalInvoices`, time-driven, every 5 minutes) - removed the "is it even registered" unknown from the backlog.
- Refactored `scripts/GoogleAppsScriptsCode/Class_PayPalService.gs`'s `PayPalService` to accept optional constructor overrides (`fetcher`, `clientId`, `clientSecret`, `isSandbox`) that default to the real `UrlFetchApp`/`PropertiesService` behavior when omitted - every existing production call site (`new PayPalService()`, zero args) is unaffected. This makes the class's network-calling methods unit-testable for the first time, on both sides of the Node/Apps-Script boundary, per the user's explicit request to harden this workflow with real tests. Built two complementary test tiers using this injection point: (1) `server/test/gs-paypal-service.test.ts` (24 tests) - loads the REAL `.gs` source (not a reimplementation) into a sandboxed Node `vm` context via a new `server/test/helpers/loadGasClass.ts` and runs it inside this project's own Vitest suite, covering `extractInvoiceIdFromUrl` (using the real production/sandbox URLs the user provided), a cross-runtime drift check against `server/utils/financeUtils.ts`'s independently-duplicated `calculatePayPalSurcharge`, and the full request/response contract of `createAndSendInvoice`/`recordOfflinePayment`/`getInvoiceStatus`/`getAccessToken_` against a mocked fetcher. (2) `scripts/GoogleAppsScriptsCode/PayPalService_UnitTests.gs` - a real-assertion native Apps Script test file (`runPayPalServiceUnitTests`, run from the editor's function dropdown) covering the same matrix using the same injection point, for sanity-checking the live script directly without a local checkout; unlike the existing `API_PAYPAL_TESTS.gs`, it never touches the real PayPal API or a real sheet and doesn't require a human to read Logger output to judge pass/fail. Both suites independently caught the same test-authoring gotcha before being trusted: `createAndSendInvoice`'s `.gs` source builds one `options` object and mutates/reuses it across its draft-then-send fetch calls, so naively recording `vi.fn()`/logged call arguments by reference (instead of capturing `options.payload` by value at call-time) made every captured call reflect only the last mutation - both test files now capture payloads correctly. Test count: 316 → 340 (228 → 252 backend); updated the count everywhere it's asserted (`AGENTS.md`, `docs/agents/README.md`, `docs/agents/testing-batches.md`). **Note: the `.gs` refactor and both test files are reference-copy work in this repo only - the injectable-overrides change needs to be manually synced into the live Apps Script editor before it (or the native test file) can run there.** `tsc --noEmit` clean; full suite 340/340, `npm run build` clean.
- Extended the injectable-overrides testability pattern (established for `PayPalService`) to `Class_GlobalVariables.gs` and `Class_GoogleAIStudio.gs`, and added the same to `PayPalSweeper.gs`'s trigger function, closing the remaining untested surface of the live checkout→PayPal chain: `GlobalVariables`'s constructor now accepts optional `activeSpreadSheet`/`config` overrides (skipping `SpreadsheetApp.getActiveSpreadsheet()` and the real `_Settings` tab read when given) instead of touching them unconditionally; `GoogleAIStudio`'s constructor forwards these and adds an optional `paypalService` override that `recordPayment` (the function `apiRecordPayment` - and so every checkout this app makes - calls) now uses in place of constructing its own `new PayPalService()`; `checkPendingPayPalInvoices` (the confirmed-live, 5-minute time-driven trigger) now accepts `activeSpreadSheet`/`paypalService`/`now` overrides, the last making its 10-minute escalation-to-Follow-Up threshold deterministically testable without real waiting. Every existing production call site (`new GoogleAIStudio()`, the real trigger firing with zero args) is unaffected. Built a small in-memory fake Spreadsheet/Sheet/Range (`server/test/helpers/fakeSheet.ts` for Node, and matching inline helpers in the new native test file) implementing only the surface these two call sites actually use - real writes are observable afterward via the fake's underlying data array, which is a genuine improvement over guessing from Logger output. Extended `loadGasClass.ts` with a `loadGasClasses` variant that evaluates multiple `.gs` files into one shared `vm` context, since `GoogleAIStudio extends GlobalVariables` needs both real source files loaded together. Added `server/test/gs-google-ai-studio.test.ts` (10 tests: cash/check success, PayPal-side failure surviving gracefully, the credit-card instant-PAID and deferred-to-sweeper paths, the currently-silent "no invoice ID" skip, bidder-not-found, unknown payment method) and `server/test/gs-paypal-sweeper.test.ts` (10 tests: PAID/MARKED_AS_PAID reconciliation, timestamp seeding, the 10-minute escalation to a new-or-existing Follow-Up sheet, leaving a recently-pending row untouched, skipping unextractable invoice IDs and non-pending rows, and the two "nothing to do" fallback paths) - both loading the real `.gs` source, not reimplementations. Added the matching native Apps Script tier, `scripts/GoogleAppsScriptsCode/GoogleAIStudioAndSweeper_UnitTests.gs` (`runGoogleAIStudioAndSweeperUnitTests`, reuses `PayPalService_UnitTests.gs`'s assert helpers), which the user should run once from the editor after copying these files over. Every test file was executed against the real combined source in Node before being trusted (not just syntax-checked), the same discipline that caught real gotchas in the earlier `PayPalService` test work - none surfaced this time, a good sign the injectable-overrides pattern itself is now well-understood. Test count: 340 → 360 (252 → 272 backend); updated the count everywhere it's asserted. **Note: as with the earlier PayPalService change, the `.gs` refactors here are reference-copy work only - they need to be manually copied into the live Apps Script editor before they (or the new native test file) take effect there.** `tsc --noEmit` clean; full suite 360/360, `npm run build` clean.
- Fixed the receipt-vs-actual-charge fee mismatch: `Class_ReceiptManager.gs`'s `calculateAdjustedTotalWithFees` (shown on both the emailed and printed receipt) computed a bidder's PayPal-inclusive total using a 2.2% + $0.30 rate, while the real PayPal invoice `createAndSendInvoice` creates is priced via `calculatePayPalSurcharge`'s 3.49% + $0.49 (the same rate `server/utils/financeUtils.ts` correctly uses Node-side). Didn't need a "which rate is correct" decision after all - every disclaimer surrounding the value already said "3.49% + $0.49" (this file's own printed-receipt QR caption, `Class_EmailManager.gs`'s email template, and `server/services/emailService.ts`), so the 2.2%/$0.30 formula was unambiguously the stale one. Worse than just a wrong number: the emailed receipt's "Pay $X via PayPal" button used the wrong total while linking to the real invoice (priced correctly), so a bidder could click expecting one amount and land on a different one. Fixed by having `calculateAdjustedTotalWithFees` delegate to `new PayPalService().calculatePayPalSurcharge(originalTotal).total` instead of its own separate math - kept the same method signature (number in, number out, `<= 0` returns `0`) so both call sites (`Class_ReceiptManager.gs:192`, `Class_ReceiptDoc.gs:49`) needed no changes. `ReceiptManager`/`ReceiptDoc` aren't yet part of the injectable-testing infrastructure (their constructors eagerly build several other heavy dependencies - `BidderManager`, `AuctionItemManager`, `EmailManager` - a bigger lift than `GoogleAIStudio`'s), so this fix leans on the already-thoroughly-tested `calculatePayPalSurcharge` it now delegates to rather than adding new tests of its own; noted as a real gap if `ReceiptManager` needs deeper coverage later. Syntax-checked; full suite 360/360 (unaffected - no existing test touches `ReceiptManager`), `npm run build` clean. **Reference-copy fix only - needs to be manually copied into the live Apps Script editor to take effect.**
- Closed the client-side test gap on the checkout mark-paid flow (`useCheckoutState.ts`'s `confirmMarkPaid`, `PaymentMethodDialog.tsx`) - the exact code that carried the payment-vocabulary bug fixed earlier this session, and previously untested. Added 9 tests to `Checkout.test.tsx` (following this file's existing integration-style convention - render the real `Checkout`, click through the real dialog - rather than introducing `renderHook`, which nothing else in this codebase uses): the dialog opens with the right bidder/total, "Confirm Payment" stays disabled until a method is picked, a parametrized regression test pinning the *exact* string sent for each of Cash/Check/Credit Card / PayPal (`"cash"`/`"check"`/`"credit_card"` - this specific assertion would have caught the original bug), the offline-method optimistic "Paid" state, the credit-card deferred-to-sweeper "Pending Verification" state (202/`deferred`), the optimistic-update rollback and error toast on a failed request, and the high-value Special-Winners warning interstitial gating the dialog. Fixed two test-authoring mistakes before trusting the suite: this file has no `@testing-library/jest-dom` import (unlike some sibling test files), so `toBeDisabled()` isn't available - checked the raw DOM `.disabled` property instead, consistent with this file's existing style; and an initial `$125.00` total assertion was wrong (assumed the mock bidder won both items, but Bidder 101/Jane only won `item1` at $50 - the other item belongs to Bidder 102), which also collided with the same dollar string already appearing elsewhere on the page (the winner card's own item-price and total-badge text) once corrected, fixed by scoping the query to `within(screen.getByRole("dialog"))`. Removed the now-resolved "Checkout mark-paid - untested client-side" item from `refactor-opportunities.md`'s test-coverage gaps. Test count: 360 → 369 (88 → 97 frontend); updated the count everywhere it's asserted. `tsc --noEmit` clean; full suite 369/369, `npm run build` clean.
